# Part I — The Narrative of Lieutenant von Belke (Of the German Navy) / 第一部分 — 冯·贝尔克中尉的自述（德国海军）

## I The Landing / 一 登陆
===Original===
If anyone had been watching the bay that August night (which, fortunately for us, there was not), they would have seen up till an hour after midnight as lonely and peaceful a scene as if it had been some inlet in Greenland. The war might have been waging on another planet. The segment of a waning moon was just rising, but the sky was covered with clouds, except right overhead where a bevy of stars twinkled, and it was a dim though not a dark night. The sea was as flat and calm as you can ever get on an Atlantic coast﻿—a glassy surface, but always a gentle regular bursting of foam upon the beach. In a semicircle the shore rose black, towering at either horn (and especially on the south) into high dark cliffs.
I suppose a bird or two may have been crying then as they were a little later, but there was not a light nor a sign of anything human being within a hundred miles. If one of the Vikings who used to live in those islands had revisited that particular glimpse of the moon, he could never have guessed that his old haunts had altered a tittle. But if he had waited a while he would have rubbed his eyes and wondered. Right between the headlands he would have seen it dimly:﻿—a great thing that was not a fish rising out of the calm water, and then very stealthily creeping in and in towards the southern shore.
When we were fairly on the surface I came on deck and gazed over the dark waters to the darker shore, with﻿—I don’t mind confessing it now﻿—a rather curious sensation. To tell the truth, I was a little nervous, but I think I showed no sign of it to Wiedermann.
“You have thought of everything you can possibly need?” he asked in a low voice.
“Everything, sir, I think,” I answered confidently.

===Chinese===
那个八月的夜晚，倘若有人一直在守望这片海湾（幸好为我们计，并没有人），那么直到午夜过后一个钟头，他看到的都会是一幅孤寂安宁的景象，仿佛这里是格陵兰的某处小湾。战争简直像在另一颗行星上进行。一弯残月刚刚升起，天空布满云层，唯独头顶正上方有一簇星星在眨眼，夜色朦胧，却并不黑暗。海面之平稳沉静，已是大西洋沿岸所能见到的极致——水面如镜，只有一阵阵轻柔而规律的浪花在海滩上绽开碎裂。海岸呈半环形黑沉沉地耸起，在两翼（尤其在南面）高峻地立成漆黑的峭壁。
我想那时已有一两只鸟在啼叫，正如稍后那样，但方圆百英里之内没有一点灯火，也没有任何人迹。倘若昔日住在那些岛上的某个维京人重访这片月光下的景象，他绝猜不出自己的老巢有一丝一毫的改变。可要是他稍等片刻，他就会揉着眼睛满心疑惑了。就在两个海岬之间，他会隐约看见那东西：——一个不是鱼的庞然大物从平静的水中升起，随后鬼鬼祟祟地、一点一点地朝南岸潜近。
等我们完全浮出水面，我走上甲板，越过深色的海水望向更幽暗的海岸，心里怀着——如今我不妨坦白承认——一种颇为古怪的感觉。说实在的，我有些紧张，但我想我没有在维德曼面前露出半点痕迹。
“可能用得着的东西你都想到了？”他低声问。
“都想到了，长官，我想是。”我自信地回答。

===Original===
“No need to give you tips!” he said with a laugh.
I felt flattered﻿—but still my heart was beating just a little faster than usual!
In we crept closer and closer, with the gentlest pulsation of our engines that could not have been heard above the lapping of the waves on the pebbles. An invisible gull or two wheeled and cried above us, but otherwise there was an almost too perfect stillness. I could not help an uncomfortable suspicion that *someone* was watching. *Someone* would soon be giving the alarm, *someone* would presently be playing the devil with my schemes. It was sheer nonsense, but then I had never played the spy before﻿—at least, not in wartime.
Along the middle of the bay ran a beach of sand and pebbles, with dunes and grass links above, but at the southern end the water was deep close inshore, and there were several convenient ledges of rock between the end of this beach and the beginning of the cliffs. The submarine came in as close as she dared, and then, without an instant’s delay, the boat was launched. Wiedermann, myself, two sailors, and the motor-bicycle just managed to squeeze in, and we cautiously pulled for the ledges.
The tide was just right (we had thought of everything, I must say that), and after a minute or two’s groping along the rocks, we found a capital landing. Wiedermann and I jumped ashore as easily as if it had been a quay, and my bicycle should have been landed without a hitch. How it happened I know not, but just as the sailors were lifting it out, the boat swayed a little and one of the clumsy fellows let his end of it slip. A splash of spray broke over it; a mere nothing, it seemed at the time, and then I had hold of it and we lifted it on to the ledge.

===Chinese===
“用不着给你什么叮嘱了！”他笑道。
我觉得颇受抬举——可我的心跳还是比较平日快了那么一点！
我们缓缓潜进，越来越近，机器以最轻微的脉动运转着，声息盖不过波浪拍打卵石的声响。一两只看不见的海鸥在头顶盘旋啼叫，除此之外，是一派近乎过分完美的寂静。我禁不住生出一种不快的疑心：有人在监视。有人马上就要发出警报，有人转眼间就要把我的计划搅得一塌糊涂。这纯属胡思乱想，可话又说回来，我从前从没干过间谍这一行——至少，战时没有干过。
海湾中部是一带沙砾海滩，上方是沙丘和长草的海滨草地，但在南端，深水紧逼岸边，海滩尽头与峭壁起点之间有几道方便落脚的岩台。潜艇尽可能大胆地驶近，随即一刻也不耽搁地放下了小艇。维德曼、我、两名水兵，还有那辆摩托自行车勉强挤了进去，我们小心地朝岩台划去。
潮水正合适（我必须说，我们把一切都考虑到了），沿着礁石摸索了一两分钟后，我们找到了一处绝佳的登陆点。维德曼和我跳上岸，轻松得就像踏上码头一样，我的自行车本该顺顺当当地卸下。事情怎么发生的我不得而知，但就在水兵们往外抬它的时候，小艇晃了一下，笨手笨脚的家伙中的一个让手里那头滑了出去。一股浪花溅在车上；当时看不过是小事一桩，接着我便抓住了它，我们把它抬上了岩台。

===Original===
Wiedermann spoke sharply to the man, but I assured him no harm had been done, and between us we wheeled the thing over the flat rocks, and pulled it up to the top of the grass bank beyond.
“I can manage all right by myself now,” I said. “Goodbye, sir!”
He gave my hand a hard clasp.
“This is Thursday night,” he said. “We shall be back on Sunday, Monday, and Tuesday nights, remember.”
“The British Navy and the weather permitting!” I laughed.

===Chinese===
维德曼厉声训斥了那人，但我向他保证没造成什么损害，我们两人合力把车子推过平坦的岩石，拉上那边草坡的坡顶。
“现在我自己一个人就行了，”我说，“再见，长官！”
他用力握了握我的手。
“今晚是星期四，”他说，“星期天、星期一和星期二夜里我们都会回来，记住。
“但愿英国海军和天气都肯行个方便！”我笑道。

===Original===
“Do not fear!” said he. “I shall be here, and we shall get you aboard somehow. Come any one of those nights that suits *him*.”
“That suits him?” I laughed. “Say rather that suits Providence!”
“Well,” he repeated, “I’ll be here anyhow. Good luck!”
We saluted, and I started on my way, wheeling my bicycle over the grass. I confess, however, that I had not gone many yards before I stopped and looked back. Wiedermann had disappeared from the top of the bank, and in a moment I heard the faint sounds of the boat rowing back. Very dimly against the grey sea I could just pick out the conning tower and low side of the submarine. The gulls were still crying, but in a more sombre key, I fancied.
So here was I, Conrad von Belke, lieutenant in the German Navy, treading British turf underfoot, cut off from any hope of escape for three full days at least! And it was not ordinary British turf either. I was on the holy of holies, actually landed on those sacred, jealously-guarded islands (which, I presume, I must not even name here), where the Grand Fleet had its lair. As to the mere act of landing, well, you have just seen that there was no insuperable difficulty in stepping ashore from a submarine at certain places, if the conditions were favourable and the moment cunningly chosen; but I proposed to penetrate to the innermost sanctuary, and spend at least three days there﻿—a very different proposition!

===Chinese===
“不用怕！”他说，“我会在这儿，无论如何也会把你接上艇。那几个晚上哪一夜方便，就听他的。”
“听他的？”我笑道，“不如说听天意吧！”
“总之，”他重复道，“我反正会来。祝你好运！”
我们互相敬礼，我推着自行车踏上了路程。不过我承认，没走出几码我就停下来回望。维德曼已从坡顶消失，片刻之后我听见小艇划回去的微弱声响。凭着灰暗的海面，我只能勉强辨认出潜艇的指挥塔和低矮的侧影。海鸥仍在啼叫，但我觉得调子更加阴沉了。
就这样，我，康拉德·冯·贝尔克，德国海军中尉，脚踏着英国的土地，至少整整三天之内断绝了一切逃走的希望！而且这还不是寻常的英国土地。我置身于至圣所中的至圣之所，实实在在地登上了那座神圣的、被严加防范的岛屿（我想，在这里就连它的名字我也不该提），大舰队就藏身于此。至于单说登陆这个举动嘛，你们方才已经看到，只要条件合适、时机挑选得巧妙，在某些地点从潜艇上跨步上岸并无不可克服的困难；但我打算深入到这座圣所最核心的密处，并在那里至少待上三天——这可完全是另一回事！

===Original===
I had been chosen for this service for three reasons: because I was supposed to be a cool hand in what the English call a “tight place”; because I could talk English not merely fluently, but with the real accent and intonation﻿—like a native, in fact; and I believe because they thought me not quite a fool. As you shall hear, there was to be one much wiser than I to guide me. He was indeed the brain of this desperate enterprise, and I but his messenger and assistant. Still, one wants a messenger with certain qualities, and as it is the chief object of this narrative to clear my honour in the eyes of those who sent me, I wish to point out that they deliberately chose me for this job﻿—I did not select myself﻿—and that I did my best.
It was my own idea to take a motor-bicycle, but it was an idea cordially approved by those above me. There were several obvious advantages. A motorcyclist is not an uncommon object on the roads even of those out-of-the-way islands, so that my mere appearance would attract no suspicion; and besides, they would scarcely expect a visitor of my sort to come ashore equipped with such an article. Also, I would cover the ground quickly, and, if it came to the worst, might have a chance of evading pursuit. But there was one reason which particularly appealed to me: I could wear my naval uniform underneath a suit of cyclist’s overalls, and so if I were caught might make a strong plea to escape the fate of a spy; in fact, I told myself I was not a spy﻿—simply a venturesome scout. Whether the British would take the same view of me was another question! Still, the motorcycle did give me a chance.
My first task was to cover the better part of twenty miles before daybreak and join forces with “him” in the very innermost shrine of this sanctuary﻿—or rather, on the shore of it. This seemed a simple enough job; I had plenty of time, the roads, I knew, were good, nobody would be stirring (or anyhow, ought to be) at that hour, and the arrangements for my safe reception were, as you shall hear, remarkably ingenious. If I once struck the hard main road, I really saw nothing that could stop me.
The first thing was to strike this road. Of course I knew the map by heart, and had a copy in my pocket as a precaution that was almost superfluous, but working by map-memory in the dark is not so easy when one is going across country.

===Chinese===
选中我来干这差事，有三个理由：一是因为人们认为我在英国人所谓的“紧要关头”是个沉得住气的人；二是因为我讲英语不只是流利，还带着地道的口音和语调——事实上就像个本地人；我相信还有一条，就是他们认为我不算太蠢。你们下面还会听到，本来有一位远比我聪明的人来引导我。他才是这项孤注一掷的行动的头脑，我不过是他的信使和助手。不过，信使也得有相当的素质才行，而这篇自述的主要目的，正是在派我前来的人面前洗清我的名誉，所以我想指出：是他们郑重其事地挑中我来干这差事——不是我自告奋勇——而且我已竭尽全力。
带一辆摩托自行车是我自己的主意，不过这个主意得到了上头的热烈赞同。它有几样显而易见的好处。即便在那些偏僻的岛上，骑摩托车的人在路上也不算稀罕，所以我单单这副模样不会招来怀疑；再者，他们决想不到我这个路数的访客上岸时会配备这样一件东西。此外，我赶路快，万一到了最坏的地步，还有一线甩脱追捕的机会。但有一个理由对我格外有吸引力：我可以在一套摩托车手的罩衫底下穿着海军制服，这样万一被抓住，还可以振振有词地请求免遭间谍的命运；实际上，我对自己说，我并不是间谍——不过是个胆子大的侦察员罢了。英国人是否也这样看我，那是另一个问题！不过，摩托车终究给了我一线机会。
我的第一桩任务，是在天亮前赶完将近二十英里的路，在这座圣所最核心的密殿里与“他”会合——或者不如说，在它的岸上会合。这差事看起来够简单了；时间充裕，我知道路况良好，那个钟点没有人会走动（无论如何不该有），而接应我安全到达的安排，你们下面会听到，是格外出奇的巧妙。只要一上了坚硬的干道，我实在看不出还有什么能拦得住我。
头一件事是找到这条干道。地图当然早已烂熟于胸，口袋里还揣着一份以防万一——这预防措施几乎是多余的——可是在黑暗里凭记忆越野赶路，就没那么容易了。

===Original===
The grassy bank fell gently before me as the land sloped down from the cliffs to the beach, and I knew that within a couple of hundred yards I should find a rough road which followed the shore for a short way, and then when it reached the links above the beach, turned at right angles across them to join the highroad. Accordingly I bumped my motorcycle patiently over the rough grass, keeping close to the edge of the bank so as to guide myself, and every now and then making a detour of a few yards inland to see whether the road had begun. The minutes passed, the ground kept falling till I was but a little above the level of the glimmering sea, the road ought to have begun to keep me company long ago, but never a sign of it could I find. Twice in my detours I stumbled into what seemed sand-holes, and turned back out of them sharply. And then at last I realised that I had ceased to descend for the last hundred yards or more, and in fact must be on the broad stretch of undulating sea links that fringed the head of the bay. But where was my road?
I stopped, bade myself keep quite cool and composed, and peered round me into the night. The moon was farther up and it had become a little lighter, but the clouds still obscured most of the sky and it was not light enough to see much. Overhead were the stars; on one hand the pale sea merged into the dark horizon; all around me were low black hummocks that seem to fade into an infinity of shadows. The gulls still cried mournfully, and a strong pungent odour of seaweed filled the night air. I remember that pause very vividly.
I should have been reckless enough to light a cigarette had I not feared that our submarine might still be on the surface, and Wiedermann might see the flash and dub me an idiot. I certainly needed a smoke very badly and took some credit to myself for refraining (though perhaps I ought really have given it to Wiedermann). And then I decided to turn back, slanting, however, a little away from the sea so as to try and cut across the road. A minute or two later I tumbled into a small chasm and came down with the bicycle on top of me. I had found my road.
The fact was that the thing, though marked on the large-scale map as a road of the third, fourth, or tenth quality (I forget which), was actually nothing more or less than three parallel crevasses in the turf filled with loose sand. It was into these crevasses that I had twice stumbled already. Now with my back to the sea and keeping a yard or two away from this wretched track, but with its white sand to guide me, I pushed my motorcycle laboriously over the rough turf for what seemed the better part of half an hour. In reality I suppose it was under ten minutes, but with the night passing and that long ride before me, I never want a more patience-testing job. And then suddenly the white sand ceased. I stepped across to see what was the matter, and found myself on a hard highroad. It was a branch of the main road that led towards the shore, and for the moment I had quite forgotten its existence. I could have shouted for joy.

===Chinese===
草坡在我面前缓缓下降，地势从峭壁向海滩倾斜，我知道不出两三百码就该有一条崎岖的小路，先沿海岸延伸一小段，到了海滩上方的草地后，再直角折转横穿过去与大道相接。于是我把摩托车颠颠簸簸地推过长满粗草的坡地，紧贴坡沿走以便有个指引，还不时向内陆绕出几码，看看路是否已经出现。时间一分一分过去，地面持续下降，直到我离微微发光的海面只高出一丁点，那条路早该来与我作伴了，却始终不见踪影。绕行时我两次跌进似乎是沙坑的地方，急忙退了出来。最后我终于明白，刚才那一百多码地面早已不再下降，实际上我必定已在环绕湾顶那片起伏的海滨草地上了。可我的路在哪里呢？
我停下脚步，命令自己保持绝对的镇定从容，向四周的夜色里张望。月亮升高了些，天也亮了一点，但云仍遮住大半天穹，光线还不足以看清多少东西。头顶是星星；一侧，苍白的海水融入黑暗的地平线；四周尽是一个个低矮的黑色土丘，仿佛消融进无穷无尽的暗影里。海鸥仍在哀啼，浓烈刺鼻的海藻味充满夜空。那一刻的停驻我记得清清楚楚。
若不是怕我们的潜艇也许还在水面上，维德曼会看见火光而骂我是蠢货，我真是会不管不顾地点上一支烟。我确实极想抽烟，还为自己的克制暗自居功（虽说这份功劳或许本该记在维德曼名下）。随后我决定往回走，不过稍稍偏离海的方向斜行，好设法横穿那条路。一两分钟后，我跌进一道小小的沟壑，连人带车倒了下去。我找到我的路了。
事实是，这条东西虽在大比例尺地图上标作三等、四等或是十等的路（我忘了是哪一等），实际上不折不扣就是草皮上三道平行的裂缝，里面填满了浮沙。先前我两次跌进去的正是这些裂缝。这时我背朝大海，与这条糟糕的路径保持一两码的距离，靠它的白沙指引，吃力地推着摩托车碾过粗粝的草皮，似乎走了足足大半个钟头。实际上我估计不到十分钟，可眼看着夜色流逝、前面还有漫漫旅途，我从没干过比这更磨性子的活。忽然，白沙到头了。我跨过去看看是怎么回事，却发现自己已站在一条坚硬的大道上。这是通向海岸的一条干道支线，刚才我竟把它忘得一干二净。我差点要欢呼出声。

===Original===
“Now,” I said to myself, “I’m off!”
And off I went, phut-phut-phutting through the cool night air, with a heart extraordinarily lightened. That little bit of trouble at the start had made the rest of the whole wild enterprise seem quite simple now that it was safely over.
I reached the end of this branch, swung round to the right into the highroad proper and buzzed along like a tornado. The sea by this time had vanished, but I saw the glimmer of a loch on my left, and close at hand low walls and dim vistas of cultivated fields. A dark low building whizzed by, and then a gaunt eerie-looking standing stone, and then came a dip and beyond it a little rise in the ground. As I took this rise there suddenly came upon me a terrible sinking of the heart. Phut-phut! went my cycle, loudly and emphatically, and then came a horrible pause. Phut! once more; then two or three feeble explosions, and then silence. My way stopped; I threw over my leg and landed on the road.
“What the devil!” I muttered.
I had cleaned the thing, oiled it, seen that everything was in order; what in heaven’s name could be the matter? And then with a dreadful sensation I remembered that wave of salt water.

===Chinese===
“好了，”我对自己说，“出发！”
于是我出发了，噗噗噗地穿过清凉的夜气，心情空前地轻快。开头那点小麻烦，如今平安过去了，倒显得这桩整个疯狂的冒险行动余下的部分都十分简单了。
我驶到支线尽头，向右一拐上了正牌的干道，像一阵旋风似的呼啸前进。这时海已经看不见了，但左侧有一面湖泊的微光，近旁是低矮的石墙和影影绰绰的耕地。一座黑黢黢的低矮建筑飞掠而过，接着是一块嶙峋狰狞的立石，然后路面下凹，过了凹处又是一段小小的上坡。正当我冲上这道坡时，心里猛地一沉。噗噗！我的车响亮而断然地叫着，接着便是一阵可怕的停顿。噗！又是一声；随后两三声有气无力的爆炸，再往后就是寂静。我的路走完了；我跨下车，落到了路面上。
“见鬼！”我喃喃道。
我擦洗过这东西，上了油，查验过一切正常；到底见鬼出了什么毛病？接着，我带着一种可怕的感觉，想起了那股咸涩的海水。

## II Night in the Ruined House / 二 废屋之夜
===Original===
You may smile to think of a sailor being dismayed by a splash of salt water; but not if you are a motorcyclist! Several very diabolical consequences may ensue.
In the middle of that empty road, in that alien land, under the hostile stars, I took my electric torch and endeavoured to discover what was the matter. From the moment I remembered the probable salt, wet cause of my mishap I had a pretty hopeless feeling. At the end of ten minutes I felt not merely quite hopeless, but utterly helpless. Helpless as a child before a charging elephant, hopeless as a man at the bottom of an Alpine crevasse. Ignition, carburettor, what had been damaged? In good daylight it might take me an hour or two first to discover and then to mend. By the radiance of my torch I would probably spend a night or two, and be none the wiser.
And meantime the precious dark hours were slipping away, and scattered all over the miles of country lay foemen sleeping﻿—nothing but foes. I was in a seagirt isle with but one solitary friend, and he was nearly twenty miles away, and I had the strictest orders not to approach him save under the cover of darkness. Enough cause for a few pretty black moments, I think you will allow.
And then I took myself by the scruff of the neck and gave myself a hearty shake. Had I been picked for this errand because I was a coward or a resourceless fool? No! Well, then, I must keep my head and use my wits, and if I could not achieve the best thing, I must try to do the second best. I ran over all the factors in the problem.
Firstly, to wait in the middle of that road trying to accomplish a job which I knew perfectly well it was a thousand chances to one against my managing, was sheer perverse folly.

===Chinese===
想到一个水手竟被一股咸水浇得心慌意乱，你们尽可以发笑；可要是你也是个骑摩托车的，就笑不出来了！随后可能跟着来一连串非常凶险的后果。
在那条空荡荡的路的中央，在异国的土地上，在怀有敌意的星空底下，我取出电筒，努力查明毛病出在哪里。从想起这场灾祸多半是咸水打湿所致的那一刻起，我心里就涌起一种近乎无望的感觉。十分钟后，我不仅觉得希望渺茫，简直束手无策。无助得像迎面撞上大象冲锋的孩子，绝望得像坠入阿尔卑斯冰缝底的人。点火装置、汽化器，到底是什么坏了？在明亮的日光下，我先找毛病再动手修，也许要一两个钟头。靠我电筒那点光亮，多半得耗上一两夜，还未必弄出个所以然。
与此同时，宝贵的黑夜时辰正在溜走，方圆多少英里的乡野间到处躺着酣睡的敌人——清一色的敌人。我身处一座四面环海的孤岛，只有一个朋友，他远在将近二十英里之外，而我有最严格的命令：非有夜色掩护不得接近他。我想你们会承认，这足够让人有好一阵子心情灰暗了。
随后我拎住自己的后颈，狠狠摇了自己一通。选中我来办这差事，难道是因为我是个懦夫，或者是个一筹莫展的蠢货？不是！那么好，我必须保持头脑冷静，善用机智，就算做不到上策，也得力求做到次一等的办法。我把问题的一切因素从头到尾理了一遍。
第一，待在这条路当中，死磕一件我心里明镜似的、一千个里难有一个能弄成的活计，纯属执迷不悟的愚蠢。

===Original===
Secondly, to leave my cycle in a ditch and try to cover the distance on my own two legs before daybreak was a physical impossibility. My cycle being one of the modern kind with no pedals, I could not even essay the dreadful task of grinding it along with my feet. Therefore I could not reach my haven tonight by any conceivable means.
On the other hand, I would still be expected tomorrow night, for our plans were laid to allow something for mischances; so if I could conceal myself and my cycle through the coming day, all might yet be well. Therefore I must devise some plan for concealing myself.
Logic had brought me beautifully so far, but now came the rub﻿—Where was I to hide? These islands, you may or may not know, are to all practical purposes treeless and hedgeless. They have many moors and waste places, but of an abominable kind for a fugitive﻿—especially a fugitive with a motorcycle. The slopes are long and usually gentle and quite exposed; ravines and dells are few and far between and farther still to reach. Caves and clefts among the rocks might be found no doubt, but I should probably break my neck looking for them in the dark. Conceive of a man with a motor-bicycle looking for a cave by starlight!
And then a heaven-sent inspiration visited me. On board we had of course maps with every house marked, however small, and who lived in it, and so on. We do things thoroughly, even though at the moment there may not be any apparent reason for some of the details. I blessed our system now, for suddenly in my mind’s eye I saw a certain group of farm buildings marked “ruinous and uninhabited.” And now where the devil was it?
My own pocket map of course had no such minute details and I had to work my memory hard. And then in a flash I saw the map as distinctly as if it had really been under my eye instead of safely under the Atlantic. “I have a chance still!” I said to myself.

===Chinese===
第二，把车丢在沟里，靠我这两条腿在天亮前赶完这段路，体力上绝无可能。我这辆车是那种没有脚蹬的新式车，我连用双脚蹭着地面一步步磨着走这种可怕的苦差都无法尝试。所以，今夜无论用什么可想见的办法，我都到不了我的安身之处了。
另一方面，明晚依然会有人等我，因为我们的计划本就留出了应对意外的余地；所以只要我能把人和车藏过明天白天，一切或许还来得及。因此我必须想出一个藏身的办法。
逻辑一路把我顺顺当当地送到这里，可现在难处来了——我上哪儿藏呢？这些岛屿，你们知道也罢、不知道也罢，实际上既无树又无篱。荒原和旷地倒很多，但对一个亡命者来说糟糕透顶——尤其是带着一辆摩托车的亡命者。山坡漫长而平缓，完全暴露在外；沟壑和谷地稀稀落落，且更难抵达。岩石间的洞穴和裂隙无疑能找到，但在黑暗里摸索着找，我很可能摔断脖子。想想看，一个推着摩托自行车的人，借着星光找山洞！
接着，一个天赐的灵感降临了。在艇上，我们当然备有地图，每一座房屋，再小也标了出来，还注明谁住在里面等等。我们办事讲究彻底，尽管有些细节当时未必看得出有什么明显的用处。此刻我为我们这套体系暗暗称颂，因为我猛然在心眼里看见图上标着“倾颓无人居住”字样的一组农舍。可它到底在哪儿呢？
我自己那张口袋地图当然没有这么细的详情，我只好拼命调动记忆。忽然间，那张图清清楚楚地呈现在我眼前，仿佛它真真切切摆在我眼皮底下，而不是安然躺在大西洋底。“我还有一线机会！”我对自己说。

===Original===
By the light of my torch I had a careful look at my small map, and then I set forth pushing my lifeless cycle. To get to my refuge I had to turn back and retrace my steps (or perhaps I should rather say my revolutions) part way to the shore till I came to a road branching southwards, roughly parallel to the coast. It ascended continuously and pretty steeply, and I can assure you it was stiff work pushing a motorcycle up that interminable hill, especially when one was clad for warmth and not for exercise. Dimly in the waxing moonlight I could see low farm buildings here and there, but luckily not a light shone nor a dog barked from one of them. Glancing over my shoulder I saw the sea, now quite distinct and with a faint sheen upon its surface, widening and widening as I rose. But I merely glanced at it enviously and concentrated my attention on the task of finding my “ruinous and uninhabited” farm. I twice nearly turned off the road too soon, but I did find it at last﻿—a low tumbledown group of little buildings some two hundred yards or so off the road on the right, or seaward side. Here the cultivated fields stopped, and beyond them the road ascended through barren moorland. My refuge was, in fact, the very last of the farms as one went up the hill. It lay pretty isolated from the others, and there was a track leading to it that enabled me to push my cycle along fairly comfortably.
“I might have come to a much worse place!” I said to myself hopefully.
Though there was not a sign of life about the place, and not a sound of any kind, I still proceeded warily, as I explored the derelict farm. I dared not even use my torch till I had stooped through an open door, and was safely within one of the buildings. When I flashed it round me I saw then that I stood in a small and absolutely empty room, which might at one time have been anything from a parlour to a byre, but now seemed consecrated to the cultivation of nettles. It had part of a roof overhead, and seemed as likely to suit my purpose as any other of the dilapidated group, so I brought my cycle in, flattened a square yard or two of nettles, and sat down on the floor with my back against the wall. And then I lit a cigarette and meditated.
“My young friend,” I said to myself, “you are in an awkward position, but, remember, you have been in awkward positions before when there were no such compensating advantages! Let us consider these advantages and grow cheerful. You are privileged to render your country such a service as few single Germans have been able to render her﻿—if this plan succeeds! If it fails, your sacrifice will not be unknown or unappreciated. Whatever happens, you will have climbed a rung or two up the ladder of duty, and perhaps of fame.”
This eloquence pleased my young friend so much that he lit another cigarette.

===Chinese===
借着电筒的光，我仔细看了一回小地图，随即推着那辆死气沉沉的车上了路。要去我的避难所，就得折回去，沿来路（或许我该说沿来时的一圈圈轮转）朝海岸方向回走一程，直到碰上一条向南岔出的路，大致与海岸平行。它一路持续上坡，而且相当陡，我可以向你们保证，推着一辆摩托车爬那道没完没了的坡实在够呛，尤其当你一身装束是为御寒而非为运动的时候。借着渐盈的月光，我隐约看见四处低矮的农舍，所幸没有一户亮灯，也没有一声狗吠。回头一瞥，我看见大海——此刻已相当清晰，水面泛着微光——随着我升高而越来越开阔。但我只艳羡地望了它一眼，便专心去找我那座“倾颓无人居住”的农庄。我两次差点过早拐下大路，但终于还是找到了——在大路右侧，也就是临海一侧，离路约两百码的地方，一组塌败的低矮小屋。耕地到此为止，再往前，大路便在荒芜的沼原间攀升而上。我的避难所，实际上是上山路上的最后一座农庄。它与其他农舍相隔颇远，有一条小径通向它，让我推起车来还算顺当。
“我落脚的地方本可能糟糕得多呢！”我满怀希望地自语道。
尽管这地方没有一丝生命的迹象，也没有任何声响，我在探看这座废弃农庄时依然步步谨慎。直到俯身穿过一扇敞开的门、安全进了其中一座屋子，我才敢用那支电筒。朝四下一照，我发现自己站在一个小而空空荡荡的房间里，它从前也许是客厅，也许是牛棚，什么都有可能，如今却似乎专门奉献给了荨麻的种植。头顶还剩着半边屋顶，在这破破烂烂的一组屋子里，它似乎和其他任何一间一样合用，于是我把车推了进去，踩平一两码见方的荨麻，背靠着墙在地板上坐下。然后我点上一支烟，沉思起来。
“我年轻的朋友，”我对自己说，“你眼下的处境够尴尬，可是记住，你从前也陷入过尴尬的处境，那时可没有这些足以补偿的好处！让我们来数数这些好处，振作起来。你有幸为祖国建下这样的功勋，单个的德国人少有人能企及——只要这计划成功！倘若失败，你的牺牲也不会无人知晓、无人感念。无论结果如何，你都在责任的天梯上、也许还在名声的天梯上，攀上了一两级。”
这番雄辩让我那位年轻朋友大为受用，他又点上一支烟。

===Original===
“Consider again,” I resumed, “what an opportunity you have been unexpectedly presented with for exhibiting your resourcefulness and your coolness and your nerve! If it had not been for that wave of salt water your task would have been almost too simple. Your own share of the enterprise would merely have consisted in a couple of easy rides on a motorcycle, and perhaps the giving of a few suggestions, or the making of a few objections, which would probably have been brushed aside as worthless. Now you have really something to test you!”
This oration produced a less exhilarating effect. In fact, it set me to wondering very gravely how I could best justify this implied tribute to my powers of surmounting difficulties. Till the day broke all I had to do was to sit still, but after that﻿—what? I pondered for a few minutes, and then I came to the conclusion that an hour or two’s sleep would probably freshen my wits. I knew I could count on waking when the sun rose, and so I closed my eyes, and presently was fast asleep.
When I awoke, it was broad daylight. Looking first through the pane-less window and then through the gap in the roof, I saw that it was a grey, still morning that held promise of a fine day, though whether that was to my advantage or disadvantage I did not feel quite sure. Nobody seemed to be stirring yet about the houses or fields, so I had still time for deliberation before fate forced my hand.
First of all, I had a look round my immediate surroundings. I was well sheltered, as all the walls were standing, and there was most of a roof over my head (the last being a point of some importance in case any aircraft chanced to make a flight in this direction). It is true that the door was gone, but even here I seemed fortunate, for another small building, also dilapidated-looking but in somewhat better condition, stood right opposite the open doorway and hid it completely. This little building still had a dishevelled door which stood closed, and for a moment I half thought of changing my shelter and taking possession of it; and then I decided that where fate had directed my steps, there should I abide.

===Chinese===
“再想想，”我接着说，“命运意外送给你一个多么好的机会，正好展示你的机变、你的冷静和你的胆量！若不是那股咸水，你的任务简直就太简单了。你在这桩行动里的份内事，不过是骑摩托车轻松跑两趟，也许再提几条建议、发几条反对意见，多半还会被当作废话搁到一边。如今你总算有真正考验你的东西了！”
这番演说的效果就不那么令人振奋了。事实上，它使我极其严肃地思索起来：这番隐含的、对我克服困难之能力的赞许，我怎样才能最好地予以兑现。天亮之前，我只需安坐不动，可天亮之后呢——怎么办？我沉思了几分钟，随后得出结论：睡上一两个钟头多半能让头脑清新。我知道太阳一出我准会醒，于是合上眼，不多时便沉沉睡去。
我醒来时已是天光大亮。先从没有窗扇的窗口望出去，又从屋顶的破洞望出去，我看见一个灰白、沉静的早晨，预示着会是晴朗的一天，虽然这于我是利是弊，我还拿不太准。农舍和田野周围似乎还没有人走动，所以在命运逼我摊牌之前，我还有从容斟酌的时间。
首先，我把近旁的环境查看了一遍。我的藏身处遮蔽良好，四壁俱全，头顶上还剩大半边屋顶（后一点颇为要紧，万一有飞行器碰巧朝这个方向飞来的话）。门确实没了，但就连这一点我似乎也走运：另一座小屋正对着敞开的门口，同样一副破败相，只是状况略好，把门口完全挡住了。那座小屋还有一扇歪斜的门，闭着，一时之间我有点想换个地方，把那一间占为己有；转念又决定，命运既已引我至此，我便在此安身。

===Original===
The next thing obviously was to overhaul my motorcycle, and this I set about at once, though all the time my thoughts kept working. In the course of an hour or so I had located the trouble in the carburettor and put it right again, and I had also begun to realise a few of the pros and cons of the situation.
I now ate a few sandwiches, had a pull at my flask, lit a cigarette, and put the case to myself squarely.
“With a motorcycle, the whole island at my disposal, and daylight in which to search it through, I can surely find a hiding-place a little farther removed from inquisitive neighbours,” I said to myself. “So the sooner I am off the better.”
But then I answered back﻿—

===Chinese===
接下来显然该检修摩托车了，我立刻动手，只是脑子里始终没停止盘算。约莫一个钟头工夫，我查明毛病出在汽化器，把它重新修好了，与此同时，我也开始掂量出处境的几分利弊。
接着我吃了几块三明治，对着小酒壶抿了一口，点上一支烟，把眼下的局面端端正正摆到自己面前。
“有摩托车，全岛任我驰骋，又有白天可以把岛搜个遍，我肯定能找到一个离好事的邻人们稍远些的藏身处，”我对自己说，“所以动身越早越好。”
可随即我又自己反驳道——

===Original===
“On the other hand it may take me some hours to find a better spot than this, and a man tearing about the country on a motorcycle is decidedly more conspicuous in the early morning than in the middle of the day or the afternoon when cyclists are natural objects.
“But again, if I do think of leaving this place I certainly ought not to be seen in the act of emerging from a ruinous house pushing my cycle﻿—not, at least, if I wish to be considered a normal feature of the landscape. I have a chance of escaping now unobserved; shall I have such a chance later in the day?”
Finally I decided to compromise. I should stay where I was till the hour when all the farmers had their midday meal. Then I might well hope to slip out unobserved, and thereafter scour the country looking for the ideal hiding-place without attracting any particular attention. But whatever merits this scheme may have had were destined never to be tested.
From my seat amid the nettles I could see right through the open door, and my eyes all this while were resting on the glimpse of grey building outside. All at once I held my breath, and the hand that was lifting a cigarette to my lips grew rigid. A thin wisp of smoke was rising from the chimney.

===Chinese===
“话又说回来，要找到比这更好的地点，也许得花上好几个钟头，而一个人骑着摩托车在乡野间横冲直撞，清早时分绝对比正午或下午更显眼——到了那时，骑摩托车的人已是寻常景物。
“再说，倘若我当真想离开此地，那也决不能让人瞧见我从一座破屋里推着车钻出来——至少，若我还希望被当作这片风景里一个正常的点缀，就不能。眼下我还有机会不为人察觉地脱身；等到今天晚些时候，还会有这样的机会吗？”
最后我决定折中行事。我就待在原地，等到农户家家吃午饭的时辰。那时我大可指望悄没声息地溜出去，随后再纵横乡野寻找理想的藏身处，不致惹人特别注意。然而这个方案无论有什么可取之处，都注定得不到检验了。
从荨麻丛中的坐处，我可以径直望穿那扇敞开的门，这一路我的目光始终落在门外隐约可见的那座灰色小屋上。突然间我屏住了呼吸，正把一支烟举向唇边的手僵在了半空。一缕细细的炊烟，正从那烟囱里袅袅升起。

## III Behind the Wall / 三 墙后
===Original===
“Ruinous” these farm buildings certainly were; but “uninhabited”﻿—obviously not quite! I rose stealthily and crossed to the door, and just as I reached it the door of the other house began to open. I stepped back and peered round the corner for quite a minute before anything more happened. My neighbour, whoever he was, seemed unconscionably slow in his movements.
And then a very old, bent, and withered woman appeared, with a grey shawl about her head. As she looked slowly round her, first to one side and then to the other, I cautiously drew back; but even as I did so I knew it was too late. A wisp of smoke had given us both away. This time it was a trail from my cigarette which I could see quite plainly drifting through the open door.
I heard her steps coming towards me, and then her shadow filled the doorway. There was nothing for it but taking the bull by the horns.
“Good morning!” I said genially.
She did not start. She did not speak. She just stared at me out of as unpleasant-looking a pair of old eyes as I have ever looked into. I suspected at once why the old crone lived here by herself; she did not look as if she would be popular among her neighbours.

===Chinese===
“倾颓”，这组农舍确实当之无愧；可“无人居住”——显然不大尽然！我悄悄起身，走向门口，刚到门边，对面那座屋子的门便开始打开。我退后一步，从墙角探望出去，足足等了将近一分钟才有下文。我那位邻居，不管是谁，动作似乎慢得没道理。
随后出现了一个非常苍老、佝偻、干瘪的女人，头上裹着灰披肩。她缓缓环顾四周，先看一边，再看另一边，我小心地缩回身子；但即便在缩回时我也知道为时已晚。一缕烟把我们两个都暴露了。这回是我那支烟冒出的一缕青烟，我看得清清楚楚，正从敞开的门口飘出去。
我听见她的脚步朝我走来，接着她的影子堵满了门口。除了毅然迎上前去，别无他法。
“早上好！”我和颜悦色地说。
她没有吃惊。她也没有说话。她只是盯着我瞧，那是我所见过的最难看的一双老眼睛。我立刻就猜到这老太婆为什么独自住在这里了；她那副模样，就不像能在邻居中讨人喜欢的。

===Original===
“I think it is going to be a fine day,” I continued breezily.
She simply continued to stare; and if ever I saw suspicion in human eyes, I saw it in hers.
“What do you think yourself?” I inquired with a smile. “I have no doubt you are more weatherwise than I.”
Then at last she spoke, and I thought I had never heard a more sinister remark.
“Maybe it will be a fine day for some,” she replied.

===Chinese===
“我看今天会是个晴天，”我轻快地接着说。
她只管继续盯着我；倘若说我曾在人的眼睛里见过猜疑，那么我在她眼里看见的就是。
“您自己怎么看？”我含笑问道。“您比我会看天色，这我毫不怀疑。”
最后她终于开口了，而我从不曾听过比这更不祥的话。
“对某些人来说，也许是个晴天。”她答道。

===Original===
“I hope I may be one of them!” I said as cheerfully as possible.
She said not one word in reply, and her silence completed the ominous innuendo.
It struck me that a word of explanation would be advisable.
“My bicycle broke down,” I said, “and I took the liberty of bringing it in here to repair it.”
Her baleful gaze turned upon my hapless motorcycle.

===Chinese===
“但愿我也算其中一个！”我尽可能快活地说。
她一个字也没有回答，而她的沉默把这番不祥的影射补得完完整整。
我觉得最好还是解释一句。
“我的车坏了，”我说，“我冒昧把它推进这儿来修。”
她那凶狠的目光转到我那倒霉的摩托车上。

===Original===
“What for did you have to mend it in here?” she inquired; very pertinently, I could not but admit.
“It was the most convenient place I could find,” I replied carelessly.
“To keep it from the rain maybe?” she suggested.
“Well,” I admitted, “a roof has some advantages.”
“Then,” said she, “you’ve been here a long while, for there’s been no rain since I wakened up.”

===Chinese===
“你干吗非得在这儿头修？”她问道；问得确实在点子上，我不能不承认。
“这是我找得到的最方便的地方。”我漫不经心地答道。
“兴许是怕它淋雨？”她提示道。
“唔，”我承认，“有个屋顶总有几分好处。”
“那么，”她说，“你在这儿待了好一阵子了，我睡醒到现在可一直没下雨。”

===Original===
“But I didn’t say I came here for shelter,” I said hastily.
She stared at me again for a few moments.
“You’re saying first one thing and then the other,” she pronounced.
I felt inclined to tell her that she had missed her vocation. What a terrible specimen of the browbeating, cross-examining lawyer she would have made! However, I decided that my safest line was cheerful politeness.
“Have it your own way, my good dame!” I said lightly.

===Chinese===
“可我没说我是进来躲雨的。”我急忙说。
她又盯了我片刻。
“你一会儿这么说，一会儿那么说。”她断言道。
我真想告诉她，她入错了行。她要是当了那种吹胡子瞪眼、盘问不休的律师，会是何等厉害的角色！不过，我认定最稳妥的路数还是快活客气。
“随您怎么想吧，我这位好心的老太太（dame）！”我轻巧地说。

===Original===
Her evil eyes transfixed me.
“You’ll be a foreigner,” she said.
“A foreigner!” I exclaimed; “why on earth should you think that?”
“You’re using queer words,” she replied.
“What words?” I demanded.

===Chinese===
她那双恶狠狠的眼睛把我钉在原地。
“你是个外国人吧。”她说。
“外国人！”我喊道，“您怎么会想到这上头？”
“你用的词怪。”她答道。
“什么词？”我追问。

===Original===
“Dame is the German for an old woman,” said she.
This astonishing philological discovery might have amused me at another time, but at this moment it only showed me too clearly how her thoughts were running.
“Well,” said I, “if it’s German, I can only say it is the first word of that beastly language I’ve ever spoken!”
Again I was answered by a very ominous silence. It occurred to me very forcibly that the sooner I removed myself from this neighbourhood the better.
“Well,” I said, “my bicycle is mended now, so I had better be off.”

===Chinese===
“Dame 在德国话里就是老女人的意思。”她说。
这项惊人的语文学发现，换在别的时候准会逗笑我，可此刻它只是再清楚不过地向我表明，她的心思正往哪儿跑。
“唔，”我说，“那要是德国话，我只能说，这门可恶的语言我生平还是头一回说出口！”
回答我的又是一阵极不祥的沉默。我痛切地感到，越快离开这一带越好。
“好了，”我说，“我的车修好了，我该走了。”

===Original===
“You had that,” she agreed.
“Goodbye!” I cried as I led my cycle out, but she never spoke a syllable in reply.
“Fate has not lost much time in forcing my hand!” I said to myself as I pushed my motorcycle along the track towards the highroad. I thought it wiser not to look round, but just before I reached the road I glanced over my left shoulder, and there was the old woman crossing the fields at a much brisker pace than I should have given her credit for, and heading straight for the nearest farm. My hand was being forced with a vengeance.
Instinctively I should liked to have turned uphill and got clear of this district immediately, but I was not sure how my cycle would behave itself, and dared not risk a stiff ascent to begin with. So I set off at top speed down the road I had come the night before, passing the old crone at a little distance off, and noticing more than one labourer in the fields or woman at a house door, staring with interest at this early morning rider. When the news had spread of where he had come from, and with what language he interlarded his speech, they might do something more than stare. There was a telegraph-office not at all far away.
As I sped down that hill and swung round away from the sea at the foot, I did a heap of quick thinking. As things had turned out I dared not make for any place of concealment far off the highroads. Now that there was a probability of the hue and cry being raised, or at least of a lookout being kept for me, the chances of successfully slipping up the valley of some burn without anyone’s notice were enormously decreased. I had but to glance round at the openness of the countryside to realise that. No; on the highroads I could at least run away, but up in the moors I should be a mere trapped rat.

===Chinese===
“是该走了。”她表示同意。
“再见！”我推车出去时喊道，可她一个音节也没有回。
“命运逼我摊牌，可真是一点工夫也不耽误！”我推着摩托车沿小径向大路走去时自语道。我觉得不回头看更明智，但刚到路边，我从左肩回望了一眼，那老太婆正在穿田过野，步子比我料想的麻利得多，直奔最近的农舍而去。命运这是要狠狠地逼我摊牌了。
凭本能，我本想立刻转上坡路，马上离开这个地段，但我拿不准车子的脾气，不敢一上来就冒险爬陡坡。于是我全速沿前一晚来的那条路冲下坡去，在与那老太婆相距不远的地方擦过，还注意到不止一个田里的雇工或门口的女人，饶有兴味地盯着这个清早骑车的家伙。等到消息传开，说他从哪儿来、说话里掺和着哪国语言，他们要做的可能就不止是盯着看了。不远的地方就有一处电报局。
我顺着山坡飞驰而下，在坡底拐弯离开海的方向，一路上飞快地盘算。照眼下的情形，我不敢再去大路以外很远的任何藏身之处了。既然很可能要闹得一片缉捕声，至少也会有人留神提防我，想不声不响地溜进哪条溪谷而不被察觉，把握就大大减小了。只需环顾一下这一带的开阔乡野，就能明白这一点。不；在大路上我至少还能逃跑，可到了荒原里，我就不过是只落了陷阱的老鼠。

===Original===
Then I had the bright thought of touring in zigzag fashion round and round the island, stopping every here and there to address an inhabitant and leave a false clue, so as to confuse my possible pursuers. But what about my petrol? I might need every drop if I actually did come to be chased. So I gave up that scheme.
Finally, I decided upon a plan which really seems to me now to be as promising as any I could think of. About the least likely place to look for me would be a few miles farther along the same road that ran past my last night’s refuge, in the opposite direction from that in which people had seen me start. I resolved to make a detour and then work back to that road.
I had arrived at this decision by the time I reached the scene of last night’s mishap. Fortunately my cycle was running like a deer now, and I swept up the little slope in a few seconds and sped round the loch, opening up fresh vistas of round-topped heather hills and wide green or brown valleys every minute. At a lonely bit of the road I jumped off, studied my map afresh, and then dashed on again.
Presently a side road opened, leading back towards the coast, and round the corner I sped; but even as I did so the utter hopelessness of my performance struck me vividly﻿—that is to say, if a really serious and organised hunt for me were to be set afoot. For the roadside was dotted with houses, often at considerable intervals it is true, but then all of them had such confoundedly wide views over that open country. There was a house or two at the very corner where I turned, and I distinctly saw a face appearing at a window to watch me thunder past. The noise these motorcycles make is simply infernal!
It was then that I fell into the true spirit for such an adventure. Since the chances were everywhere against me *if* my enemies took certain steps, well then, the only thing to do was to hope they did not take them and dismiss that matter from my mind. I was taking the best precautions I could think of, and the cooler I kept and better spirits I was in, the more likely would luck be to follow me. For luck is a discerning lady and likes those who trust her. Accordingly, the sun being now out and the morning beautifully fine, I decided to enjoy the scenery and make the most of a day ashore.

===Chinese===
接着我灵机一动，想骑着车在岛上来回兜“之”字，走走停停，逢人便搭句话，留下假线索，好把可能的追兵搅糊涂。可是汽油怎么办？万一真被追起来，每一滴都可能用得着。于是这个方案也被我放弃了。
最后，我定下了一个方案，如今回想，它的胜算确也不亚于我能想到的任何办法。要找我，最不大可能找的地方，就是从我昨夜避难所旁经过的那条路再往前几英里处，方向恰与人们看见我出发的方向相反。我决定绕个弯子，再折回那条路上去。
到达昨夜出事地点时，我已拿定这个主意。所幸我的车此刻跑得像头鹿，几秒钟便掠上那道小坡，绕着湖疾驰，每分钟都展现出新的景致：圆顶的石楠山冈和宽阔的绿色或褐色谷地。在路上一处冷清的地段，我跳下车，重新研究了地图，然后再度疾驰而去。
不久，一条岔路出现，折回海岸方向，我拐过弯疾驰而去；可就在那时，我痛切地感到自己这番表演实在全无指望——也就是说，倘若真有人为我发动一场认真而有组织的搜捕。因为路边星罗棋布尽是人家，诚然往往相隔很远，可偏偏家家都把那片开阔乡野尽收眼底。就在我转弯的角上便有一两座屋子，我分明看见一张脸出现在窗口，望着我轰隆隆驶过。这种摩托车闹出的动静，简直像地狱里放出来的！
正是在那时，我进入了这类冒险的正宗心境。既然只要敌人采取某些步骤，形势便处处于我不利，那么，唯一的办法就是指望他们不采取，并把这桩事从心里撇开。我正采取着所能想到的最周全的预防措施，人越冷静、心情越好，运气就越可能跟上来。因为运气是位有眼光的女士，喜欢信任她的人。于是，太阳既已出来，晨光又这样美好，我决定好好欣赏风景，把上岸的这一天过得尽量有滋味。

===Original===
My first step was to ease up and ride just as slowly as I could, and then I saw at once that I was doing the wisest thing in every way. I made less noise and less dust, and was altogether much less of a phenomenon. And this encouraged me greatly to keep to my new resolution.
“If I leave it all to luck, she will advise me well!” I said to myself.
I headed coastwards through a wide marshy valley with but few houses about, and in a short time saw the sea widening before me and presently struck the road I was seeking. At the junction I obeyed an impulse, and, jumping off my cycle, paused to survey the scenery. A fertile vale fell from where I stood, down to a small bay between headlands. It was filled with little farms, and all at once there came over me an extraordinary impression of peacefulness and rest. Could it actually be that this was a country at war; that naval war, indeed, was very very close at hand, and beneath those shining waters a submarine might even now be stealing or a loose mine drifting? The wide, sunshiny, placid atmosphere of the scene, with its vast expanse of clear blue sky, larks singing high up and seabirds crying about the shore, soothed my spirits like a magician’s wand. I mounted and rode on again in an amazingly pleasant frame of mind for a spy within a hair’s-breadth of capture, and very probably of ignominious death.
Up a long hill my engine gently throbbed, with moorland on either side that seemed to be so desolated by the gales and sea spray that even heather could scarcely flourish. I meant to stop and rest by the wayside, but after a look at the map I thought on the whole I had better put another mile or two between me and the lady with the baleful eyes. At the top I had a very wide prospect of inland country to the left, a treeless northern-looking scene, all green and brown with many lakes reflecting the sunshine. A more hopeless land to hide in I never beheld, and I was confirmed in my reckless resolution. Chance alone must protect me.

===Chinese===
第一步是放慢车速，能骑多慢骑多慢，随即我便看出，这样各方面都是最明智的。动静小了，扬尘少了，整个人也远不那么像个稀罕物了。这大大鼓舞了我坚守这个新决定。
“我一切都托付给运气，她自会指点我！”我对自己说。
我朝海岸方向驶去，穿过一条宽阔的沼泽谷地，四周人家寥寥，不多时便看见大海在眼前铺展开来，很快踏上了我要找的那条路。在路口，我凭一阵冲动跳下车，驻足眺望四外风景。一片肥沃的谷地从我立足处倾泻而下，直落到海岬之间的小海湾。谷中布满小小的农庄，霎时间，一种非同寻常的安宁与静谧之感攫住了我。这里当真是一个处于战争中的国家吗？海战当真已近在咫尺，在那片闪亮的水面底下，此刻说不定正有一艘潜艇潜行，或一枚脱锚的水雷漂荡？眼前开阔、明丽、安详的气象——大片澄澈的蓝天，云雀在高空歌唱，海鸟在岸边啼叫——像魔杖一样抚慰了我的心绪。我重新跨上车，怀着一种令人惊异的愉快心情继续前行——对一个距被捕只差毫厘、且很可能距受辱而死也只差毫厘的间谍来说。
发动机轻轻震颤着爬上一道长长的山坡，两侧尽是沼原，看那样子被狂风和海上的浪花蹂躏得连石楠都难以茂盛。我本打算在路边停下歇歇，但看了一眼地图，总的说来，我还是觉得最好再跑出一两英里，把自己和那位眼神凶狠的老太太拉开些距离。到了坡顶，左侧的内陆乡野尽收眼底，一派不见树木的北地风光，青绿与褐色相间，许多湖泊映着日光。比这更无望的藏身之地我从未见过，而我那孤注一掷的决心反倒更坚定了。只能靠运气保护我了。

===Original===
Down a still steeper hill I rode, only now amid numberless small farms and with another bay shining ahead. The road ran nearly straight into the water and then bent suddenly and followed the rim of the bay, with nothing but empty sea-links on the landward side. The farms were left behind, a mansion-house by the shore was still a little distance ahead, and there was not a living soul in sight as I came to a small stonewalled enclosure squeezed in between the road and the beach below. I jumped off, led my cycle round this and laid it on the ground, and then seated myself with my back against the low wall of loose stones and my feet almost projecting over the edge of the steep slope of pebbles that fell down to the sand.
I was only just out of sight, but unless anyone should walk along the beach, out of sight I certainly was, and it struck me forcibly that ever since I had given myself up to luck, every impulse had been an inspiration. If I were conducting the search for myself, would I ever dream of looking for the mysterious runaway behind a wall three feet high within twenty paces of a public road and absolutely exposed to a wide sweep of beach? “No,” I told myself, “I certainly should not!”
There I sat for hour after hour basking in the sunshine, and yet despite my heavy clothing kept at a bearable temperature by gentle airs of cool breeze off the sea. The tide, which was pretty high when I arrived, crept slowly down the sands, but save for the cruising and running of gulls and little piping shorebirds, that was all the movement on the beach. Not a soul appeared below me all that time. The calm shining sea remained absolutely empty except once for quarter of an hour or so when a destroyer was creeping past far out. To the seaward there was not a hint of danger or the least cause for apprehension.
On the road behind me I did hear sounds several times, which I confess disturbed my equanimity much more than I meant to let them. Once a motorcar buzzed past, and not to hold my breath as the sound swelled so rapidly and formidably was more than I could achieve. The jogging of a horse and trap twice set me wondering, despite myself, whether there were a couple of men with carbines aboard. But the slow prolonged rattling and creaking of carts was perhaps the sound that worried me most. They took such an interminable time to pass! I conceived a very violent distaste for carts.

===Chinese===
我又骑下一道更陡的坡，只是此刻置身于无数小农庄之间，前方又有一个海湾闪着光。那条路几乎笔直地扎向水面，随即猛一拐弯，沿着湾沿而行，向陆一侧只有空荡荡的海滨草地。农庄落在身后，岸边的一座宅邸还在前方不远处，而当我来到一处夹在大路和下方海滩之间的小小石墙围地里时，四下不见一个人影。我跳下车，把它牵过围地放倒在地上，然后背靠着那道乱石矮墙坐下，双脚几乎伸出砾石陡坡的边沿，坡下便是沙滩。
我只不过刚刚避出视线，可除非有人沿海滩步行，我确乎是看不见了。我强烈地感到，自从把自己交托给运气，我的每一阵冲动都成了神启。倘若由我来搜捕我自己，我会做梦想到去寻找那个神秘的逃亡者吗——在一道三英尺高的墙后，离大路不过二十步，而且完全暴露在一片开阔的滩头前？“不会，”我对自己说，“我肯定不会！”
我坐在那里，一个钟头接一个钟头地晒着太阳，虽然衣裳厚重，海上吹来的阵阵清凉微风使我不至于热不可耐。潮水在我到达时还相当高，此时缓缓退下沙滩，但除了海鸥和尖声啼叫的小水鸟的盘旋奔跑，海滩上再无别的动静。整整那段时间，下面没有出现一个人影。平静闪亮的海面一直空空荡荡，只有一回，约莫一刻钟工夫，一艘驱逐舰在远处缓缓驶过。朝海的一面没有一丝危险的迹象，也没有半点可忧之处。
身后的路上，我确实几次听见声响，我承认，它们对我心境平静的搅扰，远比我打算流露的大。有一回一辆汽车嗡嗡驶过，那声音如此急速而可怕地涨大，要不屏住呼吸，我实在做不到。马车得得的蹄声有两次使我不由自主地寻思，车上是不是坐着两个带卡宾枪的人。但最让我心烦的，也许还是大车那种缓慢而漫长的吱嘎声。它们过一趟竟要那么久！我对大车生出了一种极其强烈的厌恶。

===Original===
I do take some credit to myself that not once did I yield to the temptation to peep over my wall and see who it was that passed along the road. I did not even turn and try to peer through the chinks in the stones, but simply sat like a limpet till the sounds had died completely away. The only precaution I took was to extinguish my cigarette if I chanced at the moment to be smoking.
In the course of my long bask in that sun bath I ate most of my remaining sandwiches and a cake or two of chocolate, but kept the remainder against emergencies. At last as the sun wore round, gradually descending till it shone right into my eyes, and I realised that the afternoon was getting far through, hope began to rise higher and higher. It actually seemed as if I were going to be allowed to remain within twenty yards of a highroad till night fell. “And then let them look for me!” I thought.
I don’t think my access of optimism caused me to make any incautious movement. I know I was not smoking, in fact it must simply have been luck determined to show me that I was not her only favourite. Anyhow, when I first heard a footstep it was on the grass within five yards of me, and the next moment a man came round the corner of the wall and stopped dead short at the sight of me.
He was a countryman, a small farmer or hired man, I should judge﻿—a broad-faced, red-bearded, wide-shouldered, pleasant-looking fellow, and he must have been walking for some distance on the grass by the roadside, though what made him step the few yards out of his way to look round the corner of the wall, I have never discovered to this day. Possibly he meant to descend to the beach at that point. Anyhow there he was, and as we looked into one another’s eyes for a moment in silence I could tell as surely as if he had said the words that he had heard the story of the suspicious motorcyclist.

===Chinese===
我确实引以自许的是，我不曾一次屈从于诱惑，探头望过墙去看路上过的是谁。我甚至连头也不转、不去试图从石缝里窥看，只是像只帽贝一样钉坐不动，直到那些声响完全消逝。我采取的唯一预防措施是，倘若当时恰好在抽烟，就把烟掐灭。
在这场漫长的日光浴里，我吃掉了大部分剩下的三明治和一两块巧克力，其余的留着应急。终于，随着太阳转移，渐渐下落，直到正照进我的眼睛，我意识到下午已过去大半，希望也越升越高。看来我真要获准在大路二十码之内一直待到天黑了。“到那时就随他们找我吧！”我想。
我不认为那阵乐观的劲头使我做出过任何轻率的举动。我知道当时我没有抽烟，其实，那必定只是运气存心向我表明，我不是她唯一宠爱的人。总之，我最初听见脚步声时，它已在离我不到五码的草地上，紧接着一个人从墙角转出来，一眼看见我，猛地僵住了。
他是个乡下人，依我看是个小自耕农或雇工——宽脸膛，红胡子，阔肩膀，模样挺和善。他想必已沿着路边的草地走了一段路，至于什么缘故让他偏离几码绕到墙角来看看，我至今也没弄明白。也许他本想从那里下海滩去。总之他就站在那里，我们默默对视了片刻，我敢断言，就像他亲口说了那样：那个形迹可疑的骑摩托车者的故事，他已经听说了。

## IV The Nails / 四 钉子
===Original===
“A fine afternoon,” I remarked, without rising, and I hope without showing any sign of emotion other than pleasure at making an acquaintance.
“Aye,” said he, briefly and warily.
This discouraging manner was very ominous, for the man was as good-natured and agreeable-looking a fellow as I ever met.
“The weather looks like keeping up,” I said.
He continued to look at me steadily, and made no answer at all this time. Then he turned his back to me very deliberately, lifted his felt hat, and waved it two or three times round his head, evidently to someone in the distance. I saw instantly that mischief was afoot and time precious, yet the fellow was evidently determined and stouthearted, besides being physically very powerful, and it would never do to rouse his suspicions to the pitch of grappling with me. Of course I might use my revolver, but I had no wish to add a civilian’s death to the other charge I might have to face before that sun had set. Suddenly luck served me well again by putting into my head a well-known English cant phrase.

===Chinese===
“下午天气真好。”我搭话说，人没有起身，而且我希望，除了结识新相识的愉快之外，没有显出任何激动的迹象。
“啊。”他说，简短而警惕。
这种拒人千里的态度很不祥，因为这个人是我所遇过的脾气最好、模样最和善的家伙之一。
“看这天色，一时半会儿晴定。”我说。
他仍旧目不转睛地打量我，这一回干脆一声不吭。随后他不慌不忙地背过身去，举起毡帽，绕着头顶挥了两三下，显然是冲着远处的什么人。我立刻看出大事不妙，时间宝贵，可这家伙显然又坚决又胆壮，体格还十分强壮，绝不能把他的疑心激到跟我动手的地步。当然，我可以动用手枪，但我无意在那轮太阳落山之前，在我可能要面对的罪名之外，再添上一条平民的死。忽然，运气又帮了我的忙，让我脑子里蹦出一句有名的英语俗语。

===Original===
“Are you often taken like that?” I inquired with a smile.
He turned round again and stared blankly. I imitated the movement of waving a hat, and laughed.
“Or is it a family custom?” I asked.
He was utterly taken aback, and looked rather foolish. I sat still and continued to smile at him. And then he broke into a smile himself.
“I was just waving on a friend,” he explained, and I could detect a note of apology in his voice. For the moment he was completely hoodwinked. How long it would last Heaven knew, but I clearly could not afford to imitate Mr. Asquith, and “wait and see.”

===Chinese===
“你常这样发作吗？”我含笑问道。
他又转过身来，茫然瞪着我。我学着他挥帽子的动作，笑了。
“还是说，这是贵府的家传规矩？”我问。
他整个人愣住了，样子显得相当傻。我坐着不动，继续冲他微笑。接着他自己也咧嘴笑了。
“我不过是朝一个朋友挥挥手，催他快些。”他解释道，话音里听得出几分歉意。此刻他是完全被蒙住了。这能维持多久只有天知道，但我显然学不起阿斯奎斯先生那套“等着瞧”。

===Original===
“Oh,” I said with a laugh, “I see!”
And then I glanced at my wristlet watch, and sprang to my feet with an exclamation.
“By Jove, I’ll be late!” I said, and picking up my cycle wheeled it briskly to the road, remarking genially as I went, “the days are not so long as they were!”
I never saw a man more obviously divided in mind. Was I the suspicious person he fancied at first? Or was I an honest and peaceable gentleman? Meanwhile I had cast one brief but sufficient glance along the road. Just at the foot of the steep hill down which I had come in the morning a man was mounting a motorcycle. Beside him stood one or two others﻿—country folk, so far as I could judge at the distance, and piecing things together, it seemed plain that my friend had lately been one of the party, and that the man they had been gossiping with was a motorcyclist in search of me, who had actually paused to make inquiries within little over a quarter of a mile from where I sat. Quite possibly he had been there for some time, and almost certainly he would have ridden past without suspecting my presence if it had not been for the diabolical mishap of this chance encounter.
I had planted my cycle on the road, and was ready to mount before my friend had made up his mind what to do. Even then his procedure luckily lacked decision.

===Chinese===
“哦，”我笑道，“我明白了！”
接着我瞥了一眼腕表，喊了一声，一跃而起。
“天哪，我要迟到了！”我说着，拎起车子轻快地推上大路，一路还亲切地念叨：“日子可不如先前那么长了！”
我从没见过一个人如此明显地左右为难。我到底是他起初以为的那个可疑人物？还是一位正派安分的绅士？趁这工夫，我朝大路方向飞快扫了一眼，一眼便足够了。就在我早上下来的那道陡坡脚下，一个人正跨上一辆摩托车。他旁边还站着一两个人——就远距离判断，是乡下人。把事情串起来看，情况很明白：我这位朋友方才正是那一伙里的一个，他们闲聊的那个对象，是一个搜寻我的骑摩托车的人，他竟然停在离我坐处刚过四分之一英里的地方打听消息。他很可能已在那儿待了一些时候，而且几乎可以肯定，若不是这场冤家路窄的倒霉巧遇，他本会径直骑过去，根本疑不到我的存在。
我已把车撑上大路，还没等我的朋友拿定主意怎么办，就做好了上车的准备。即便那时，幸而他的举动也还犹豫不决。

===Original===
“Beg pardon, sir﻿—!” he began, making a step towards me.
“Good evening!” I shouted, and the next instant the engine had started, and I was in my saddle.
Even then my pursuer had got up so much speed that he must surely have caught me had he not stopped to make inquiry of my late acquaintance. I was rounding a corner at the moment, and so was able to glance over my shoulder and see what was happening. The cyclist was then in the act of remounting, and I noted that he was in very dark clothes. It might or might not have been a uniform, but I fancied it was. Anyhow, I felt peculiarly little enthusiasm for making his acquaintance.
On I sped, working rapidly up to forty miles an hour, and quite careless now of any little sensation I might cause. I had sensations myself, and did not grudge them to other people. The road quickly left the coast and turned directly inland, and presently it began to wind along the edge of a long reedy stretch of water, with a steep bank above it on the other side. The windings gave me several chances of catching a glimpse of my pursuer, and I saw that I was gaining nothing; in fact, if anything he was overhauling me.
“I’ll try them!” I said to myself.

===Chinese===
“对不起，先生——！”他开口道，朝我迈了一步。
“晚上见！”我大喊一声，下一瞬发动机已经点着，我已坐上了车座。
即便如此，我的追兵也已跑出了很大的速度，若不是他停下来向我刚才那位相识打听，他必定能追上我。当时我正拐过一个弯，所以能回头瞥一眼，看清了情形。那个骑车的人正在重新上车，我注意到他穿着十分深色的衣服。那也许是不是制服，说不准，但我觉得就是。无论如何，我对与他结识提不起半点兴致。
我一路疾驰，很快加到每小时四十英里，此刻全然顾不上自己会引起什么小小的轰动。我自己心里的轰动已经够多，也就不吝惜给别人一点。公路很快离开海岸，径直折向内陆，不久便沿着一片长长的芦苇水泽的边缘蜿蜒起来，对岸是一道陡岸。这一路蜿蜒给了我几次瞥见追兵的机会，我看自己一点儿也没甩开他；说实在的，他反而在一步步赶上来。
“那就试试它们吧！”我对自己说。

===Original===
“Them” were nails. Wiedermann had done me no more than justice in assuming I had come well provided against possible contingencies. Each of my side-pockets had a little packet of large-headed, sharp-pointed nails. I had several times thrown them experimentally on the floor of my cabin, and found that a gratifying number lay point upwards. I devoutly prayed they would behave as reasonably now.
This stretch of road was ideal for their use﻿—narrow, and with not a house to give succour or a spectator to witness such a very suspicious performance, I threw a handful behind me, and at the next turn of the road glanced round to see results. The man was still going strong. I threw another handful and then a third, but after that the road ran straight for a space, and it was only when it bent to the right round the head of the loch that I was able to see him again. He had stopped far back, and was examining his tyres.
The shadows by this time were growing long, but there were still some hours before darkness would really shelter me, and in the meantime what was I to do with myself, and where to turn? Judging from the long time that had elapsed between my discovery in the early morning and the appearance of this cyclist at the very place which I had thought would be the last where they would seek me, the rest of the island had probably been searched and the hue and cry had died down by this time. So for some time I ought to be fairly safe anywhere: until, in fact, my pursuer had reached a telegraph office, and other scouts had then been collected and sent out. And if my man was an average human being, he would certainly waste a lot of precious time in trying to pump up his tyres or mend them before giving it up as a bad job and walking to a telegraph office.
That, in fact, was what he did, for in this open country I was able a few minutes later to see him in the far distance still stopping by that loch shore. But though I believe in trusting to chance, I like to give myself as many chances as possible. I knew where all the telegraph offices were, and one was a little nearer him than I quite liked. So half a mile farther on, at a quiet spot on a hill, I jumped off and swarmed up one of the telegraph-posts by the roadside, and then I took out of my pocket another happy inspiration. When I came down again, there was a gap in the wire.
There was now quite a good chance that I might retain my freedom till night fell, and if I could hold out so long as that﻿—well, we should see what happened then! But what was to be done in the meantime? A strong temptation assailed me, and I yielded to it. I should get as near to my night’s rendezvous as possible, and try to find some secluded spot there. It was not perhaps the very wisest thing to risk being seen there by daylight and bring suspicion on the neighbourhood where I meant to spend two or three days; but you will presently see why I was so strongly tempted. So great, in fact, was the temptation that till I got there I hardly thought of the risk.

===Chinese===
“它们”是钉子。维德曼认定我对可能的不测作了充分准备，这倒没有高估我。我两侧的口袋里各有一小包大头尖钉。我曾几次在舱室地板上做过试验，发现相当可观的一部分都是尖头朝上落定的。我虔诚地祈祷它们此刻也这般识趣。
这段路用来施展它们再理想不过——狭窄，既没有一户人家可以救援，也没有一个看客目睹这幕形迹十分可疑的表演。我朝身后撒了一把，在下一个路弯回头查看战果。那人还跑得正欢。我又撒了一把，接着第三把，但此后道路直了一段，直到它向右绕过湖头，我才又看见他。他远远地停在了后面，正在检查轮胎。
这时影子已渐渐拉长，但要等黑暗真正庇护我，还有几个钟头，这段时间我拿自己怎么办，又往哪里去？从清晨我被发觉，到这个骑车人出现在我原以为他们最后才会搜到的地方，其间隔了那么久，据此判断，岛上的其余地方多半已搜过一遍，喧腾的缉捕声到这时也已平息。所以在一段时间里，我在哪里都该相当安全：直到我的追兵抵达一处电报局，其他探子再被召集派出为止。而且，只要我这人是寻常人的性子，他必定会先浪费大量宝贵时间给轮胎打气、补胎，最后才认栽放弃，步行去电报局。
事实正是如此，因为在这开阔的乡野，几分钟之后我还能远远望见他停在湖边没动。不过，我虽然相信听凭运气，却喜欢给自己尽可能多的机会。所有电报局的位置我都心中有数，其中一处离他比我乐意的稍近了些。于是又往前半英里，在一处山坡上的僻静地点，我跳下车，手脚并用地爬上路边一根电线杆，随后从口袋里掏出又一个灵机一动的产物。等我再爬下来时，线上已经断了一截。
现在，我很有机会把自由一直保到夜幕降临，而只要我能撑到那时候——好，到时看我们的！可是这段时间里该做些什么呢？一股强烈的诱惑向我袭来，我屈服了。我要尽量靠近夜里的会合点，在那附近找个僻静地方待下。冒着白天在那里被人看见、给我打算住上两三天的那一带招来怀疑的风险，这也许不是最明智的事；但你们马上就会明白，我为何受这么强的诱惑。那诱惑实在太大，直到抵达那里，我几乎没想过风险。

===Original===
I rode for a little longer through the same kind of undulating, loch-strewn inland country, and then I came again close to the sea. But it was not the open sea this time. It was a fairly wide sound that led from the ocean into a very important place, and immediately I began to see things. What things they were precisely I may not say, but they had to do with warfare, with making this sound about as easy for a hostile ship to get through, whether above the water or below, as a pane of glass is for a bluebottle. As I rode very leisurely, with my head half turned round all the while, I felt that my time was not wasted if I escaped safely, having seen simply what I now noted. For my eye could put interpretations on features that would convey nothing to the ordinary traveller.
Gradually up and up a long gentle incline I rode, with the sound falling below me and a mass of high dark hills rising beyond it. Behind me the sun was now low, and my shadow stretched long on the empty road ahead. For it was singularly empty, and the countryside was utterly peaceful; only at sea was there life﻿—with death very close beside it. And now and then there rose at intervals a succession of dull, heavy sounds that made the earth quiver. I knew what they meant!
Then came a dip, and then a very steep long hill through moorland country. And then quite suddenly and abruptly I came to the top. It was a mere knife-edge, with the road instantly beginning to descend steeply on the other side, but I did not descend with the road. I jumped off and stared with bated breath.
Ahead of me and far below, a wide island-encircled sheet of water lay placid and smiling in the late afternoon sunshine. Strung along one side of it were lines of grey ships, with a little smoke rising from most of their funnels, but lying quite still and silent﻿—as still and silent as the farms and fields on shore. Those distant patches of grey, with the thin drifts of smoke and the masts encrusted with small grey blobs rising out of their midst, those were the cause of all my country’s troubles. But for them peace would have long since been dictated and a mightier German Empire would be towering above all other States in the world. How I hated﻿—and yet (being a sailor myself) how I respected them!

===Chinese===
我又骑行了一程，穿过的还是那种起伏不平、湖泊散布的内陆乡野，随后再次来到海边。但这一回不是外海。这是一条相当宽的海峡，从大洋通向一个极其重要的地方，我立刻开始看见种种东西。具体是什么东西，我不能说，但都与战争有关，与让这条海峡对一艘敌舰来说——无论在水上还是水下——如同玻璃板之对于青蝇一样难过有关。我骑得非常从容，脑袋始终半转着，一边觉得，只要我能安全脱身，单单看到我此刻记下的这些东西，这趟就没算白来。因为我的眼睛能从种种地貌上读出意味，而这些对寻常旅人来说什么也不会说明。
我缓缓骑上一道长长的漫坡，越爬越高，海峡落在我脚下，海峡那头涌起一大片高峻幽暗的山峦。身后太阳已经低垂，我的影子在前面空荡荡的路上拉得老长。那路空得出奇，四乡也彻底安宁；唯有海上才有生气——而死亡就紧挨在它旁边。间或传来一连串沉闷厚重的声响，震得大地微颤。我知道那意味着什么！
接着是一段凹路，然后是一段穿行于沼原之间的又陡又长的坡。再往后，十分突兀地，我到了坡顶。那顶上薄得像道刀刃，路面随即在另一侧陡峭而下，但我没有随路下去。我跳下车，屏息凝望。
在我前方、深深的下方，一片被群岛环抱的宽阔水面，安详明媚地卧在傍晚的阳光里。沿它一侧排开一列列灰色的军舰，大多数烟囱升起淡淡的烟，却静静地停着，悄无声息——同岸上的农庄田地一样静，一样无声。那些远远的灰色斑块，连同袅袅的薄烟和从中升起、缀满灰色小点的桅杆，正是我祖国一切灾难的根源。若不是它们，和约早已强加于人，一个更强大的德意志帝国早已凌驾于世界各国之上。我多么恨它们——然而（我自己也是个水手）我又多么敬重它们！

===Original===
One solitary monster of this Armada was slowly moving across the landlocked basin. Parallel to her and far away moved a tiny vessel with a small square thing following her at an even distance, and the sun shining on this showed its colour red. Suddenly out of the monster shot a series of long bright flashes. Nothing else happened for several seconds, and then almost simultaneously “Boom! boom! boom!” hit my ear, and a group of tall white fountains sprang up around the distant red target. The Grand Fleet of England was preparing for “The Day”!
I knew the big vessel at a glance; I knew her, at least, as one of a certain four, and for some moments I watched her gunnery practice, too fascinated to stir. I noted how the fall of her shells was spread﻿—in fact I noted several things; and then it occurred to me abruptly that I stood a remarkably good chance of having a wall at my back and a handkerchief over my eyes if I lingered in this open road much longer. And the plea that I was enjoying the excellent gun-practice made by H.M.S. *Blank* would scarcely be accepted as an extenuating circumstance!
I glanced quickly round, and then I realised how wonderfully luck was standing by me. At the summit of that hill there were naturally no houses, and as the descending road on either side made a sharp twist almost immediately, I stood quite invisible on my outlook tower. The road, moreover, ran through a kind of neck, with heather rising on either side; and in a moment I had hauled my cycle up the bank on the landward side, and was out of sight over the edge, even should any traveller appear.
After a few minutes’ laborious dragging of my cycle I found myself in a small depression in the heather, where, by lying down, I could remain quite out of sight unless someone walked right into me﻿—and it seemed improbable that anyone should take such a promenade with the good road so close at hand. By raising myself on my knees I could command the same engrossing view I had seen from the road, only I now also saw something of the country that sloped down to the sea; and with a thrill of exultation I realised that this prospect actually included our rendezvous.

===Chinese===
这支大舰队中一个孤零零的庞然大物，正缓缓横越这片闭锁的湾池。与它平行、相隔很远的地方，一条小船在移动，后面匀速跟着一个方形小物，阳光照在那上面，显出红色。突然，那庞然大物喷出一连串长长的亮光。几秒钟内再无别的动静，随后几乎是同时，“轰！轰！轰！”撞进我的耳朵，一道道高高的白色水柱在远处那个红色靶标周围腾起。英国的大舰队正在为“那一天”作准备！
我一眼就认出了那艘大舰；至少认出它是某四艘之一。有一阵子我竟看它的炮术操演练得入了迷，挪不动步子。我留意到它的炮弹落点如何分布——事实上我留意了好几件事；随后猛然醒悟，再在这条露天的路上耽搁下去，我很有可能背靠一面墙、眼蒙一条手帕。而“我正在欣赏‘某某’号军舰的精彩炮术”这一申辩，多半不会被当作减轻罪行情有可原的理由！
我飞快环顾四周，这才明白运气正何等奇妙地守护着我。那座山顶上自然没有人家，而两侧下山的路几乎立刻就各拐一个急弯，我立在这座瞭望塔上，完全无人看得见。加之公路穿过一道鞍状的山颈，两旁石楠丛生；转眼间我已把车拖上向陆一侧的路坡，翻过坡沿不见了踪影，就算真有旅人出现也瞧不见。
吃力地拖了几分钟车之后，我在石楠丛中找到一处小小的洼地。人往里一躺，便可完全避人耳目，除非有人径直撞到我身上——大路就在近旁，谁也不像是会来此散步的。跪起身子，方才在路上看到的那幅摄人心魄的景色便尽收眼底，而且此刻我还望见了向海边倾斜下去的一部分乡野；我心头一阵狂喜，意识到这幅展望里竟真的包括我们的会合点。

## V Waiting / 五 等待
===Original===
What I saw when I cautiously peered over the rim of that little hollow was (beginning at the top) a vast expanse of pale-blue sky, with fleecy clouds down near the horizon already tinged with pink reflections from the sunset far off behind my back. Then came a shining glimpse of the North Sea; then a rim of green islands, rising on the right to high heather hills; then the landlocked waters and the grey ships now getting blurred and less distinct; then some portions of the green land that sloped up to where I lay; and among these fields, and not far away from me, the steep roof and gable-top of a grey, old-fashioned house. It was the parish manse, the pacific abode of the professional exponent and exemplar of peace﻿—the parish minister; and yet, curiously enough, it was that house which my eyes devoured.
The single ship had now ceased firing and anchored with her consorts, the fleet had grown too indistinct to note anything of its composition, and there was nothing to distract my attention from the house. I looked at it hard and long and studied the lie of the ground between it and me, and then I lay down on a couch of soft heather and began to think.
So far as I could see I had done nothing yet to draw suspicion to this particular spot, for no one at all seemed to have seen me, but it was manifest that there would be a hard and close hunt for the mysterious motorcyclist on the morrow. I began to half regret that I had cut that telegraph wire and advertised myself so patently for what I was. Now it was quite obvious that for some days to come motorcycling would be an unhealthy pastime in these islands. Even at night how many ears would be listening for my “phut-phut-phut,” and how many eyes would be scanning the dark roads? A few judiciously placed and very simple barricades﻿—a mere bar on two uprights, with a sentry beside each﻿—and what chance would I have of getting back to that distant bay, especially as I had just been seen so near it?
“However,” I said to myself, “that is looking too far ahead. It was not my fault I brought this hornet’s nest about my ears. Just bad luck and a clumsy sailor!”

===Chinese===
我小心翼翼地从那个小洼地的边沿探头张望时，看到的（从顶上数起）是：一大片淡蓝色的天空，贴近地平线处的絮状云朵已染上粉色，那是远远照在我身后的落日返照。接着是北海闪亮的一角；接着是一环绿色的岛屿，在右侧升起为高高的石楠山冈；接着是那片闭锁的水域和灰色的舰船，此刻已模糊难辨；接着是几片向上斜到我卧处的绿色陆地；而在这片田野间，离我不远，是一座灰色老式房屋的陡顶和山墙尖。那是本教区的牧师住宅，和平的职业阐释者与化身——教区牧师——那座安宁的居所；然而说来奇怪，恰恰是那座房子，让我目光贪馋地盯住不放。
那艘单独的军舰已停止射击，随同它的伙伴们下了锚，舰队已模糊得看不出编成的细节，再没有什么能分散我对那座房子的注意了。我久久地紧紧盯住它，研究它与我这之间的地势，随后往一榻柔软的石楠上躺下，开始思考。
依我看，我还没有做过什么引人怀疑这处地点的事，因为似乎根本没有人见到过我；但显而易见，明天必将对这个神秘的骑摩托车者展开一场严密紧逼的搜捕。我开始有点后悔剪断了那根电报线，把自己是何等人物张扬得那样明白。事到如今已很清楚，往后好些天里，在这些岛上骑摩托车将是一种不利于健康的消遣。即便在夜里，会有多少只耳朵谛听着我的“噗噗噗”，多少双眼睛扫视着黑暗中的道路？几处布置得当、构造简单的路障——不过是两根立柱架一根横木，每处再站一名哨兵——我还能有什么机会赶回那遥远的海湾，何况我刚刚又是在离它那么近的地方被人看见？
“不过，”我对自己说，“这想得太远了。把这一窝马蜂招到耳边，不是我的过错。只能怪运气不好，加上一个笨手笨脚的水兵！”

===Original===
Just then I heard something approaching on the road below me, and in a minute or two it became unmistakably the sound of a horse and trap. At one place I could catch a glimpse of this road between the hummocks of heather, and I raised myself again and looked out. In a moment the horse and trap appeared and I got a sensation I shall not soon forget. Not that there seemed to the casual passerby anything in the least sensational about this equipage. He would merely have noticed that it contained, besides the driver, a few articles of luggage and a gentleman in a flat-looking felt hat and an overcoat﻿—both of them black. This gentleman was sitting with his back to me (he was in a small wagonette), but I could scarcely doubt who it was. But only arriving tonight!
Curiosity and anxiety so devoured me that I ran a little risk. Getting out of my hollow, I crawled forward on my hands and knees till I could catch a glimpse of the side road leading to that house; and there I lay flat on my face and watched.
Down the steep hill the horse proceeded at a walk, and what between my impatience to make sure, and my consciousness of my own rashness in quitting even for a moment my sheltered hollow, I passed a few very uncomfortable minutes. The light by this time was failing fast, but it was quite clear enough to see (or be seen), and at last I caught one more glimpse of that horse and trap﻿—turning off the road just where I expected. And then I was crawling back with more haste than dignity.
It was “him”! And he had only arrived tonight. If it had not been for my accident, in what a nice dilemma I should have been landed! Never did I bless anyone more fervently than that awkward sailor who had let my cycle slip, and as for the wave of salt water which wet it, it seemed to have sprung from the age of miracles.
The trouble of my discovery and its possible consequences still remained, but I thought little enough of that now, so thankful did I feel for what had *not* happened. And then I stretched myself out again on the heather, waiting with all the patience I could muster for the falling of night.

===Chinese===
就在那时，我听见下面的路上有什么东西驶近，一两分钟后，那声音已明白无误是马车的动静。有一处地方，我能从石楠丛的土丘间瞥见这段路，我便又撑起身子望出去。马车很快就出现了，我得到一种不会轻易忘却的感觉。倒不是这辆车在过路人眼里有什么丝毫耸动之处。他只会注意到，车上除了赶车的，还有几件行李和一位戴扁平毡帽、穿大衣的先生——两样都是黑色。那位先生背朝我坐着（他坐的是一辆小型四轮马车），可我几乎不会怀疑他是谁。但他竟今晚才到！
好奇心和焦灼如此吞噬着我，以致我稍稍冒了点险。出了洼地，我手脚并用地匍匐向前，爬到能瞥见通向那座房子的支路的地方，便伏在地上观望。
那匹马一步一步走下陡坡，我既急切地想看个分明，又意识到自己哪怕片刻离开那处隐蔽的洼地也是孟浪，两下夹攻，度过了很难受的几分钟。这时天光正迅速暗下去，但要看见（或被看见）还是绰绰有余的，我终于又瞥见了那辆马车——恰在我料想的地方拐下了大路。随后我往回爬去，顾不得体面，只求快些。
正是“他”！而他今晚才到。倘若不是我这桩意外，我会陷入怎样的尴尬境地！我从未如此热诚地感念过谁，除了那个让我的车滑落的笨拙水兵；至于打湿车子的那股咸水，简直像出自奇迹迭现的年代。
我被发觉的麻烦及其可能的后果依然存在，但此刻我几乎不去想它了，因为没发生的事已让我感激不尽。随后我又在石楠丛中舒展开身子，拿出全部的耐心，等待夜幕降临。

# Part II — A Few Chapters by the Editor / 第二部分 — 编者的几章

## I The Pleasant Stranger / 一 和气的陌生人
===Original===
It was in July of that same year that the Rev. Alexander Burnett was abashed to find himself inadvertently conspicuous. He had very heartily permitted himself to be photographed in the centre of a small group of lads from his parish who had heard their country’s call and were home in their khaki for a last leave-taking. Moreover, the excellence of the photograph and the undeniably close resemblance of his own portrait to the reflection he surveyed each morning when shaving, had decidedly pleased him. But the appearance of this group, first as an illustration in a local paper and then in one that enjoyed a very wide circulation indeed, embarrassed him not a little. For he was a modest, publicity-avoiding man, and also he felt he ought to have been in khaki too.
Not that Mr. Burnett had anything really to reproach himself with, for he was in the forties, some years above military age. But he was a widower without a family, who had already spent fifteen years in a sparsely inhabited parish in the southeast of Scotland not very far from the Border; and ever since he lost his wife had been uneasy in mind and a little morbid, and anxious for change of scene and fresh experiences. He was to get them, and little though he dreamt it, that group was their beginning. Indeed, it would have taken as cunning a brain to scent danger in the trifling incidents with which his strange adventure began as it took to arrange them. And Mr. Burnett was not at all cunning, being a simple, quiet man. In appearance he was rather tall, with a clean-shaven, thoughtful face, and hair beginning to turn grey.
A few days later a newspaper arrived by post. He had received several already from well-meaning friends, each with that group in it, and he sighed as he opened this one. It was quite a different paper, however, with no illustrations, but with a certain page indicated in blue pencil, and a blue pencil mark in the margin of that page. What his attention was called to was simply the announcement that the Rev. Mr. Maxwell, minister of the parish of Myredale, had been appointed to another charge, and that there was now a vacancy there.
Mr. Burnett looked at the wrapper, but his name and address had been typewritten and gave him no clue. He wondered who had sent him the paper, and then his thoughts naturally turned to the vacant parish. He knew that it lay in a certain group of northern islands, which we may call here the Windy Isles, and he presumed that the stipend would not be great. Still, it was probably a better living than his own small parish, and as for its remoteness, well, he liked quiet, out-of-the-way places, and it would certainly be a complete change of scene. He let the matter lie in the back of his mind, and there it would very likely have remained but for a curious circumstance on the following Sunday.
His little parish church was seldom visited by strangers, and when by any chance one did appear, the minister was very quickly conscious of the fact. He always took stock of his congregation during the first psalm, and on this Sabbath his experienced eye had noted a stranger before the end of the opening verse. A pleasant-looking gentleman in spectacles he appeared to be, and of a most exemplary and devout habit of mind. In fact, he hardly once seemed to take his spectacled gaze off the minister’s face during the whole service; and Mr. Burnett believed in giving his congregation good measure.

===Chinese===
就在同年七月，亚历山大·伯内特牧师发现自己无意间出了名，颇感窘迫。教区里一群小伙子响应祖国召唤，穿着卡其军装回家作最后的道别，他曾十分热心地容许自己坐在他们中间拍了张合影。况且那张照片拍得极好，他自己的肖像同他每天早晨刮脸时端详的那张脸确实惟妙惟肖，这使他相当满意。但这群人的合影先是在一家地方报纸上登了出来，随后又登上了一家销路确实极广的报纸，这就让他颇为难堪了。因为他是个谦逊、不爱抛头露面的人，再说他也觉得自己本该同样穿上卡其军装。
这倒不是说伯内特先生真有什么可自责的，他已年过四十，超出兵役年龄好几年了。但他是个无儿无女的鳏夫，已在苏格兰东南部离边境不远处一个人烟稀少的教区待了十五年；自打失去妻子，他心里一直不安生，有点郁结，渴望换个环境、有些新的经历。他就要得着这些了，而尽管他自己毫无所觉，那张合影正是这一切的开端。说实在的，他那场离奇冒险起头的这些琐碎事，要从中嗅出危险，得有一颗与安排它们同样狡诈的头脑。而伯内特先生一点也不狡诈，是个单纯安静的人。外表上他身材偏高，脸刮得干干净净，神情若有所思，头发已开始花白。
几天后，邮局送来一份报纸。好心的朋友已经寄来过好几份，每份都登着那张合影，所以他拆开这一份时不免叹了口气。然而这份报纸截然不同，没有图片，只是用蓝铅笔标出了某一页，那一页的空白处还有一道蓝铅笔道。他的注意力被引到的，无非是一条通告：迈尔代尔教区的牧师马克斯维尔先生已受任新职，该教区如今出了空缺。
伯内特先生看了看包纸，姓名地址都是打字机打的，给不出什么线索。他纳闷是谁寄的报纸，随后心思自然转到了那个空缺的教区上。他知道那教区在北方某群岛中，我们不妨称之为“风之群岛”，而且他料想圣俸不会丰厚。话虽如此，那多半还是比他自己的小教区强些，至于那地方偏僻嘛，唔，他就喜欢清静偏远的所在，况且这必定是彻底换个环境。他把这件事搁在心底，若不是第二个星期日出了一桩古怪的情形，它多半也就会一直搁在那里。
他那座小教区教堂很少有生人造访，万一真出现一位，牧师也总能很快察觉。他习惯在第一篇赞美诗的时间里把会众打量一遍，这个安息日，开篇一节还没唱完，他老练的眼睛就已发现一个生人。那似乎是位戴眼镜的和气先生，心境极其虔敬堪称表率。事实上，整场礼拜中，他那副镜片后面的目光几乎没有离开过牧师的脸；而伯内特先生向来讲究给会众十足的分量。

===Original===
It was a fine day, and when service was over the minister walked back to his manse at a very leisurely pace, enjoying the sunshine after a week of showery weather. The road he followed crossed the river, and as he approached the bridge he saw the same stranger leaning over the parapet, smoking a cigar, and gazing at the brown stream. Near him at the side of the road was drawn up a large dark-green touring car, which apparently the gentleman had driven himself, for there was no sign of a chauffeur.
“Good day, sir!” said the stranger affably, as the minister came up to him. “Lovely weather!”
Mr. Burnett, nothing loath to hear a fresh voice, stopped and smiled and agreed that the day was fine. He saw now that the stranger was a middle-sized man with a full fair moustache, jovial eyes behind his gold-rimmed spectacles, and a rosy healthy colour; while his manner was friendliness itself. The minister felt pleasantly impressed with him at once.
“Any trout in this stream?” inquired the stranger.
Mr. Burnett answered that it was famed as a fishing river, at which the stranger seemed vastly interested and pleased, and put several questions regarding the baskets that were caught. Then he grew a little more serious and said﻿—

===Chinese===
那天天气晴好，礼拜结束后，牧师迈着十分悠闲的步子走回牧师住宅，享受一星期阴雨之后的阳光。他走的路要过河，快到桥头时，他看见那个生人正倚在栏杆上，抽着一支雪茄，望着褐色的河水。他身旁的路边停着一辆深绿色的大型旅行车，看样子是这位先生自己开的，因为不见司机的影子。
“您好，先生！”牧师走近时，那生人和蔼地招呼道。“天气真好！”
伯内特先生正乐意听听新鲜的声音，便停下脚步，微笑着附和说今天天气确实好。他此刻看清了，这生人中等身材，留着浓密的淡色髭须，金丝眼镜后面一双乐呵呵的眼睛，面色红润健康；举止更是友好到家。牧师顿时对他有了愉快的好感。
“这条河里有鳟鱼吗？”生人问道。
伯内特先生回答说这条河素以渔场闻名，生人听了似乎大感兴趣，很是欢喜，又问了好几个关于渔获的问题。随后他略正经了些，说道——

===Original===
“I hope you will pardon me, sir, for thanking you for a very excellent sermon. As I happened to be motoring past just as church was going in I thought I’d look in too. But I assure you I had no suspicion I should hear so good a discourse. I appreciated it highly.”
Though a modest man, Mr. Burnett granted the stranger’s pardon very readily. Indeed, he became more favourably impressed with him than ever.
“I am very pleased to hear you say so,” he replied, “for in an out-of-the-way place like this one is apt to get very rusty.”
“I don’t agree with you at all, sir,” said the stranger energetically, “if you’ll pardon my saying so. In my experience﻿—which is pretty wide, I may add﻿—the best thinking is done in out-of-the-way places. I don’t say the showiest, mind you, but the *best*!”
Again the minister pardoned him without difficulty.

===Chinese===
“恕我冒昧，先生，我要为今天一篇极好的布道向您道谢。我碰巧驾车经过，正赶上大家进教堂，就想自己也进去坐坐。可我向您保证，我万万没想到会听到这样一篇出色的讲道。我欣赏极了。”
伯内特先生虽然谦逊，却毫不迟疑地成全了生人这份“请恕冒昧”。实际上，他对这人的好感又添了几分。
“听您这么说我很高兴，”他答道，“因为在这样一个偏僻地方，人很容易生锈的。”
“我完全不同意，先生，”生人起劲地说，“恕我直言。照我的经验——我还可以补一句，这经验相当广——最好的思想都是在偏僻地方做出的。我不说是最炫耀的，您注意，而是最好的！”
牧师又一次毫不费力地宽恕了他。

===Original===
“Of course, one needs a change now and then, I admit,” continued the stranger. “But, my dear sir, whatever you do, don’t go and bury yourself in a crowd!”
This struck Mr. Burnett as a novel and very interesting way of putting the matter. He forgot all about the dinner awaiting him at the manse, and when the stranger offered him a very promising-looking cigar, he accepted it with pleasure, and leaned over the parapet beside him. There, with his eyes on the running water, he listened and talked for some time.
The stranger began to talk about the various charming out-of-the-way places in Scotland. It seemed he was a perfervid admirer of everything Scottish, and had motored or tramped all over the country from Berwick to the Pentland Firth. In fact, he had even crossed the waters, for he presently burst forth into a eulogy of the Windy Islands.
“The most delightful spot, sir, I have ever visited!” he said enthusiastically. “There is a peacefulness and charm, and at the same time something stimulating in the air I simply can’t describe. In body and mind I felt a new man after a week there!”
The minister was so clearly struck by this, and his interest so roused, that the stranger pursued the topic and added a number of enticing details.

===Chinese===
“当然，人时不时需要换换空气，这我承认，”生人接着说，“可是，我亲爱的先生，无论您做什么，千万别一头扎进人堆里埋掉自己！”
这种说法在伯内特先生听来新颖而有趣。他把牧师住宅里等着他的那顿晚饭忘了个精光，生人递上一支看样子很不错的雪茄，他也欣然接受，挨着对方倚在栏杆上。就这样，他望着流淌的河水，一边听一边聊了好一阵。
生人谈起苏格兰各地迷人的偏僻角落。看样子他是苏格兰万事的火热崇拜者，从贝里克到彭特兰海峡，开车也好徒步也好，足迹遍及全境。事实上他连海都渡过了，因为不多时他就滔滔不绝地颂扬起风之群岛来。
“那是我到过的最惬意的地方，先生！”他热情洋溢地说。“那里的空气里有一种安宁和魅力，同时又有点叫人振奋的东西，我简直形容不出来。在那儿待了一个星期，我觉得身心都焕然一新！”
牧师显然被打动了，兴致也被撩了起来，于是生人抓住这个话题，又添上了好些诱人的细节。

===Original===
“By the way,” he exclaimed presently, “do you happen to know a fellow-clergyman there called Maxwell? His parish is﻿—let me see﻿—Ah, Myredale, that’s the name.”
This struck Mr. Burnett as quite extraordinary.
“I don’t know him personally,” he began.
“A very sensible fellow,” continued the stranger impetuously. “He told me his parish was as like heaven as anything on this mortal earth!”
“He has just left it,” said Mr. Burnett.

===Chinese===
“对了，”他忽又说道，“您可巧认识那边一位同行牧师，叫马克斯维尔的？他的教区是——让我想想——啊，迈尔代尔，就是这个名字。”
这在伯内特先生看来实在异乎寻常。
“我同他本人并不相识。”他开口道。
“非常通情达理的一个人，”生人又冒冒失失地接着说，“他跟我说，他的教区是这凡世上最像天堂的地方！”
“他刚刚离开了那里。”伯内特先生说。

===Original===
The stranger seemed surprised and interested.
“What a chance for someone!” he exclaimed.
Mr. Burnett gazed thoughtfully through the smoke of his cigar into the brown water of the river below him.
“I have had thoughts of making a change myself,” he said slowly. “But of course they might not select me even if I applied for Myredale.”
“In the Scottish Church the custom is to go to the vacant parish to preach a trial sermon, isn’t it?” inquired the stranger.

===Chinese===
生人显得又惊讶又感兴趣。
“这对什么人可是个好机会啊！”他喊道。
伯内特先生若有所思，隔着雪茄的烟雾，凝望脚下褐色的河水。
“我自己也动过换换的念头，”他缓缓地说，“不过当然，就算我申请迈尔代尔，人家也未必挑中我。”
“苏格兰教会的规矩，是到出缺的教区去讲一次试讲道，对吧？”生人问道。

===Original===
The minister nodded. “A system I disapprove of, I may say,” said he.
“I quite agree with you,” said the stranger sympathetically. “Still, so long as that is the system, why not try your luck? Mind you, I talk as one who knows the place, and knows Mr. Maxwell and his opinion of it. You’ll have an enviable visit, whatever happens.”
“It is a very long way,” said Mr. Burnett.
“Don’t they pay your expenses?”
“Yes,” admitted the minister. “But then I understand that those islands are very difficult for a stranger to enter at present. The naval authorities are extremely strict.”

===Chinese===
牧师点点头。“可以说，这是个我不赞成的制度。”他说。
