「我正琢磨，您是不是纯种的德国人。」我补了一句。

「我对德国的感情，同您一样强烈，贝尔克先生，」她答道。「的确，我想谁也不会比我更忠于自己的国家，可论血统我不是纯德国人。我母亲是爱尔兰人，所以我有这个名字——艾琳。」

「那么，这是您的真名？」我叫道，又惊又喜。

「是的，我这人身上，就这一件是真的。」她微笑道。

「可您既然是半个英国人——」

===Original===
“Irish,” she corrected.

“Ah!” I cried. “I see﻿—of course! I was going to ask whether your sympathies were not at all divided. But Irish is very different. Then you hate the English with a double hatred?”

“With one or two exceptions﻿—friends I have made﻿—I abhor the whole race I am fighting against quite as much as you could possibly wish me to! Indeed, I wish it were fighting and not merely plotting!”

There was an earnestness and intensity in her voice and a kindling of her eye as she said this that thrilled and inspired me like a trumpet.

“We shall defeat them﻿—never fear!” I cried. “We shall trample on the pride of England. It will be hard to do, but I have no doubt as to the result; have you?”

“None,” she said, quietly but with absolute confidence.

===Chinese===
「爱尔兰人。」她纠正道。

「啊！」我叫道。「我懂了——当然！我本来还要问，您的感情会不会两头分。可爱尔兰就完全不同了。这么说，您对英国人怀着双倍的仇恨？」

「除了一两位例外——我交下的朋友——这场战争里与我交战的整个种族，我厌弃的程度，只怕您盼望的也不过如此！说实话，我但愿这是明刀明枪地打，而不只是阴谋算计！」

她说这话时，声音里的恳切与炽烈，眼中的光焰，像一声号角，叫我激奋。

「咱们定会打败他们——别怕！」我叫道。「咱们定要把英格兰的骄横踩在脚下。这事难办，可结果如何，我毫不怀疑；您呢？」

「毫不怀疑。」她说，平静而斩钉截铁。

===Original===
Then that quick smile of hers, a little grave but very charming, broke over her face.

“But let us get away for a little from war,” she said. “You aren’t smoking. Please do, if you wish to.”

I lit a cigarette, and offered one to her, but she said she did not smoke. And I liked her all the better. We talked more lightly for a while, or perhaps I should rather say less earnestly, for our situation did not lend itself to frivolity. It did lend itself however to romance﻿—we two sitting on either side of the peat fire, with a shaded lamp and the friendly flames throwing odd lights and shadows through the low, primitive room with its sloping attic-like walls and its scanty furniture; and the wind all the while tempestuously booming in the chimney and scouring land and sea. And neither on land nor sea was there a single friend; surrounded by enemies who would have given a heavy price to have learned who sat in that room, we talked of many things.

At last, all too soon, she rose and wished me good night. A demon of perversity seized me.

“I shall escort you down to Mr. Tiel, and the devil take his precautions!” I exclaimed.

“Oh no,” she protested. “After all he is in command.”

===Chinese===
随后她脸上又绽开那快捷的微笑，略带庄重，却非常动人。

「不过，咱们且把战争放一放吧，」她说。「您没抽烟呢。想抽请尽管抽。」

我点上一支烟，也敬她一支，她说她不抽烟。我因此愈发喜欢她了。有一阵子我们谈得轻快了些，或者不如说，不那么一本正经了，因为我们的处境本不容轻浮。可这处境倒容得下罗曼史——我们两个坐在泥炭火两旁，罩着灯罩的灯光和友好的火苗，把光影投遍这间低矮简朴、四壁像阁楼般倾斜、家具寥寥的屋子；风则整夜在烟囱里狂轰，扫荡着陆地和海洋。陆上海上，我们没有半个朋友；四面全是敌人，只要探得这屋里坐的是谁，他们出多大的价钱都肯——而我们，就这样谈了许多事。

终于，时间快得不像话，她起身道晚安。一个促狭的魔鬼攫住了我。

「我送您下去见蒂尔先生，让他的种种防备见鬼去吧！」我叫道。

「哦，不行，」她反对说。「他毕竟是总负责啊。」

===Original===
She really seemed quite concerned at my intention, but I can be very obstinate when I choose.

“Tuts!” I said. “It is sheer rubbish to pretend that there is any risk at this time of night. Probably he is still out, and anyhow he will not have visitors at this hour.”

She looked at me very hard and quickly as if to see if I were possible to argue with, and then she gave a little laugh and merely said﻿—

“You are terribly wilful, Mr. Belke!”

And she ran downstairs very quickly, as though to run away from me. I followed fast, but she was some paces ahead of me as we went down the dark passage to the front of the house. And then suddenly I heard guarded voices, and stopped dead.

There was a bend in the passage just before it reached the hall, and Eileen had passed this while I had not, and so I could see nothing ahead. Then I heard the voice of Tiel say﻿—

===Chinese===
她对我的打算似乎当真很着了急，可我认准的事，是执拗得很的。

「嘁！」我说。「深更半夜硬说有什么风险，纯粹是胡扯。多半他还没回来呢，再说这个钟点他也不会有客。」

她又快又狠地看了我一眼，像在掂量我讲不听讲得进，随后轻笑一声，只说——

「您任性得吓人，贝尔克先生！」

说罢她飞快跑下楼去，像是要甩开我。我紧追，可沿着黑暗的走廊朝宅子前部去的一路，她始终领先我几步。突然，我听见压低的人声，猛地站住了。

走廊在到达过厅前有一处拐角，艾琳已过了拐角，我还没有，因此前面我什么也看不见。这时我听见蒂尔的声音说——

===Original===
“Well?”

It was a simple word of little significance, but the voice in which it was said filled me with a very unpleasant sensation. The man spoke in such a familiar, confidential way that I suddenly felt I could have shot him cheerfully. For the instant I forgot the problem of the other voice I had heard.

“Mr. Belke is with me! He insisted,” she cried.

At this I knew that the unknown voice could not belong to an enemy, and I advanced again. As I passed the bend in the passage I was just in time to see Tiel closing the front door behind a man in a long dark coat with a gleam of brass buttons, and to hear him say,

“Good night, Ashington.”

Eileen passed into the parlour with a smiling glance for me to follow, and Tiel came in after us. I was not in the most pleasant temper. In fact, for some reason I was in a very black humour.

===Chinese===
「怎么样？」

这是个再简单不过、无甚分量的词，可说它的那个嗓音，叫我浑身极不舒服。那人说话用那样亲昵、那样知交的口气，我刹那间觉得，一枪崩了他我都乐意。一时间，我竟忘了追究我听见的另一个嗓音。

「贝尔克先生同我在一起！是他坚持要来。」她喊道。

这一来我明白了，那个陌生嗓音的主人不会是敌人，便又向前走。我赶到走廊拐角时，恰好看见蒂尔正把前门关上，门外是个穿深色长外套、铜纽扣一闪的人，又听见他说——

「晚安，阿兴顿。」

艾琳进了客厅，含笑一瞥，示意我跟上，蒂尔随后也进来了。我的心绪说不上最好。说实在的，不知怎么，我正满怀乌云。

===Original===
“I thought you had gone out,” I said to him at once.

“I did go out.”

“But now I understand that the worthy Captain Ashington has been visiting you here!”

“Both these remarkable events have occurred,” said Tiel drily.

When I recalled how long Eileen had been up in my room, I realised that this was quite possible, but this did not, for some reason, soothe me.

“Why did he come?” I asked.

===Chinese===
「我还以为您出去了。」我劈头对他说。

「我是出去过。」

「可现在我明白了，那位可敬的阿兴顿上校是一直在您这里做客！」

「这两件奇事都发生了。」蒂尔干巴巴地说。

我回想艾琳在我房里待了多久，便明白这完全可能；可不知怎么，这并不能平息我。

「他来做什么？」我问。

===Original===
“The fleet is going out on Friday.”

“Aha!” I exclaimed, forgetting my annoyance for the moment.

“So that is settled at last,” said Tiel with a satisfied smile.

He happened to turn his smile on Eileen also, and my annoyance returned.

“You dismissed our dear friend Ashington very quickly when you heard me coming,” I remarked in no very amiable tone.

Tiel looked at me gravely.

===Chinese===
「舰队星期五出海。」

「啊哈！」我叫道，一时间忘了恼。

「总算定了。」蒂尔满意地微笑道。

他碰巧又把微笑转向了艾琳，我的恼意又回来了。

「听见我来，您可把咱们亲爱的朋友阿兴顿打发得好快。」我的口气不大友善。

蒂尔正色看我。

===Original===
“Belke,” he said, “you might quite well have done serious mischief by showing your dislike for Ashington so palpably the other day. Even a man of that sort has feelings. I have soothed them, I am glad to say, but he was not very anxious to meet you again.”

“So much the better!” said I. “Traitors are not the usual company a German officer keeps.”

“Many of us have to mix with strange company nowadays, Mr. Belke,” said Eileen.

Her sparkling eye and her grave smile disarmed me instantly. I felt suddenly conscious I was not playing a very judicious part, or showing myself perhaps to great advantage. So I bade them both good night and returned to my room.

But it was not to go to bed. For two mortal hours I paced my floor, and thought and thought, but not about any problem of the war. I kept hearing Tiel’s “Well,” spoken in that hatefully intimate way, and then remembering that those two were alone﻿—all night!﻿—in the front part of the house, far out of sound or reach of me. I did not doubt Eileen for an instant, but that calm, cool, cosmopolitan adventurer, who could knock an unsuspecting clergyman on the head and throw him over a cliff, and then tell the story with a smile﻿—what was he not capable of?

Again and again I asked myself why it concerned me. This was a girl I had only known for hours. But her smile was the last thing I saw before I fell asleep at length about three o’clock in the morning.

===Chinese===
「贝尔克，」他说，「前天您把对阿兴顿的厌恶摆得那样露骨，很可能已经闯了大祸。连那种人也有感觉。我很高兴地说，我把它们抚平了，可他原本实在不想再见您。」

「那再好不过！」我说。「德国军官寻常结交的，可不是叛徒。」

「如今我们许多人，都不得不结交些奇怪的人物，贝尔克先生。」艾琳说。

她那灼灼的目光和庄重的微笑，顷刻叫我缴了械。我猛然自觉，我扮演的角色并不怎样得体，或者说，没显出自己多高明。于是我向两位道了晚安，回房去了。

可回房并不是为了睡。整整两个钟头，我在地板上踱来踱去，想了又想，想的却不是战争中的任何问题。我耳中一直响着蒂尔那一声「怎么样」，用那亲昵得可憎的腔调说的；接着又想起，那两个人单独待着——一整夜！——在宅子的前部，我喊不应也够不着。艾琳我是一瞬也不曾疑心；可那个冷静从容、走遍世界的冒险家，那个能把一个毫无戒心的牧师敲昏过去抛下悬崖、还能笑着说这件事的人——还有什么是他做不出来的？

我一遍又一遍地问自己，这与我何干。这姑娘，我认识她不过几个钟头。可临了我大约凌晨三点才睡着，闭眼前的最后一样东西，是她的微笑。

## VIII The Decision / 八 决断

===Original===
In the morning I came down to breakfast without asking anybody’s leave, and I looked at those two very hard. To see Eileen fresh and calm and smiling gave me the most intense relief, while, as for Tiel, he looked as cool and imperturbable as he always did﻿—and I cannot put it stronger than that, for nothing more cool and imperturbable than Tiel ever breathed. In fact it could not have breathed, for it would have had to be a graven image.

He looked at me critically, but all he said was﻿—

“If it wasn’t too wet for your nice uniform, Belke, we might have had breakfast on the lawn.”

“You are afraid someone may come and look in at this window?” I asked.

===Chinese===
早晨，我没向任何人告假就下楼来吃早饭，并且狠狠打量了那两位。看见艾琳神清气爽、从容含笑，我心中顿时一块石头落地；至于蒂尔，他看上去一如既往地冷静沉着——话我只能说到这个份上，因为天下再没有比蒂尔更冷静沉着的东西呼吸过空气了。说实在的，那样的东西根本不可能呼吸，它得是一尊石雕才行。

他挑剔地打量我，但只说了一句——

「要不是怕你那身漂亮军装淋得太湿，贝尔克，我们本可以在草坪上吃早饭的。」

「你是怕有人会走过来朝这扇窗子里张望吧？」我问。

===Original===
“On the whole there is rather more risk of that than of someone climbing up to look in at your bedroom window,” said he.

“You think a great deal of risks,” I observed.

“Yes,” said he. “I am a nervous man.”

Eileen laughed merrily, and I could not but confess that for once he had scored. I resolved not to give him the chance again. He then proceeded to draw the table towards one end of the room, pulled the nearest curtain part way across, and then locked the front door. But I made no comments this time.

===Chinese===
「总的说来，这可比有人爬上你卧室窗子张望的风险要大那么一点。」他说。

「你对风险想得可真多。」我说。

「是啊，」他说，「我是个胆子小的人。」

艾琳快活地笑起来，我不得不承认这一回他占了个上风。我打定主意不再给他这种机会。接着他把桌子拖到房间一头，把近处的窗帘拉上一半，又锁上了前门。这一回我没有作声。

===Original===
At breakfast Eileen acted as hostess, and so charming and natural was she that the little cloud seemed to blow over, and we all three discussed our coming plan of attack on the fleet fully and quite freely. Tiel made several suggestions, which he said he had been discussing with Ashington, and, as they seemed extremely sound, I made notes of them and promised to lay them before Wiedermann.

When we had finished and had a smoke, Tiel rose and said he must go out “on parish business.” I asked him what he meant, and learned to my amusement that in his capacity of the Rev. Alexander Burnett he had to attend a meeting of what he called the “kirk-session.” We both laughed, and wished him good luck, and then before he left he said﻿—

“You had better get back to your room, Belke. Remember we are here on *business*.”

And with that he put on his black felt hat, and bade us lock the front door after him, and if anybody called, explain that it was to keep the wind from shaking it. I must say he thought of these small points very thoroughly.

===Chinese===
吃饭时艾琳尽起女主人的职分，她是那样迷人、那样自然，仿佛那一小片阴云已经风吹云散。我们三个把即将对舰队发动进攻的计划充分而畅快地讨论了一遍。蒂尔提了几条建议，说是他先前同阿兴顿讨论过的；这些建议看来极为妥当，我便一一记下，答应转呈维德曼。

饭后我们抽了抽烟，蒂尔站起身来，说他得出去办「教区事务」。我问他是怎么回事，这才好笑地得知：他以亚历山大·伯内特牧师的身份，得去出席一个他所谓的「长老教务会」的会议。我们两个都笑了，祝他好运，临走前他说——

「你还是回你自己屋里去吧，贝尔克。记住，我们到这儿来是办*正事*的。」

说完他戴上那顶黑毡帽，嘱咐我们在他身后把前门锁上；若是有人来访，就解释说这是防风把门震响。我得说，这些细节他都想得非常周到。

===Original===
The suggestion in his last words that I was placing something else before my duty stung me a little. I was not going to let Tiel see that they had any effect, but as soon as he had gone I rose and said to Eileen﻿—

“It is quite clear that I ought to return to my room. I have notes to write up, and several things to do before tonight.”

“Then you are really going to leave us tonight?” said she; “I am very sorry.”

So was I. Indeed, the thought of leaving her﻿—probably forever﻿—would have been bitter enough in any case, but to leave her alone with Tiel was maddening. It had troubled me greatly last night, yet the thought of remaining was one I did not really care to face.

===Chinese===
他末了那句话暗指我把别的事摆在了职责前头，颇有点刺痛我。我不打算让蒂尔看出这话起了作用，但他一走我就站起来对艾琳说——

「很清楚，我应当回我的屋里去。我有笔记要誊清，今晚之前还有好几件事要办。」

「那么今晚你真的要离开我们了？」她说，「我真舍不得。」

我又何尝舍得。说实话，一想到要离开她——多半是永别——无论怎样都已够苦涩的了，而把她单独留给蒂尔，更是令人发狂。昨夜这事就搅得我心神不宁，然而留下来这个念头，我又实在不愿去正视。

===Original===
“I fear I must,” I replied, in a voice which must have revealed something of what I felt.

“Tiel told me you absolutely refused to listen to him when he wished you to remain.”

“Oh no!” I cried. “That is putting it far too strongly. I offered to put the case to Commander Wiedermann, and then Tiel at once assumed I was going to leave him, and told me to say no more about it.”

“Really! That is somewhat extraordinary!” she exclaimed in rather a low voice, as though she were much struck with this. She had been standing, and she sat as she spoke. I felt that she wished to go further into this matter, and I sat down again too.

===Chinese===
「恐怕我必须走了。」我答道，那嗓音想必泄露了几分我的心绪。

「蒂尔告诉我，他劝你留下时，你根本听不进去。」

「哦，不是！」我叫道，「这话说得太过头了。是我提出要把这事禀报维德曼中校定夺，蒂尔当即断定我要弃他而去，叫我休要再提。」

「真的吗！这可有点不寻常！」她压低声音惊叹道，像是被这事深深触动。她本来站着，说话间便坐下了。我觉出她想把这桩事深谈下去，于是也重新落座。

===Original===
“What is extraordinary about it?” I asked.

“Do you mean to say that Tiel didn’t press you?”

“No,” I said.

“Mr. Belke,” she said earnestly, “I know enough of the orders under which we are acting and the plans that Tiel has got to further, to be quite certain that you were intended to stay and assist him. It is *most* important.”

===Chinese===
「这有什么不寻常的？」我问。

「你是说，蒂尔没有坚劝你留下？」

「没有。」我说。

「贝尔克先生，」她恳切地说，「对我们奉命行事的种种指令，对蒂尔须要推进的种种计划，我知道得足够清楚，可以断定：你本来就是该留下协助他的。这*极其*重要。」

===Original===
“You are quite sure of this?”

“Absolutely.”

“Then why did Tiel give up trying to persuade me so readily? Why didn’t he try to use more authority?”

“I wonder,” she said in a musing tone, and yet I could see from her eye that she had an idea.

===Chinese===
「你能完全肯定？」

「完全肯定。」

「那么蒂尔为什么那样轻易就放弃了劝我？他为什么不拿出更多权威来压一压？」

「我也纳闷呢。」她若有所思地说，然而我从她眼中看得出，她心里已经有了主意。

===Original===
“You know!” I exclaimed. “Tell me what is in your mind!”

Already I guessed, but I dared not put it into words.

“It is difficult to guess Tiel’s motives﻿—exactly,” she said rather slowly.

I felt I had to say it outright.

===Chinese===
「你知道了！」我喊道，「把你心里想的告诉我！」

其实我已经猜到了，却不敢说出口。

「蒂尔的动机——很难猜得确切。」她说得相当慢。

我觉得非得把话挑明不可。

===Original===
“Are you his motive?” I demanded.

She looked at me quickly, but quite candidly.

“I scarcely like to say﻿—or even think such a thing, but﻿—”

She broke off, and I finished her sentence for her.

===Chinese===
「你就是他的动机吧？」我逼问。

她飞快看了我一眼，目光却十分坦然。

「这样的话我简直不愿说出口——连想也不愿想，可是——」

她顿住了，我替她把话说完。

===Original===
“But you know he admires you, and is not the man to stick at anything in order to get what he wants.”

“Ah! Don’t be unjust to him,” she answered; and then in a different voice added, “But to think of his letting you go like that!”

“So it was to get rid of me, and have you alone here with him?”

“He must have had some motive,” she admitted, “for you *ought* to stay.”

===Chinese===
「可你知道他倾慕你，而且他这个人，为了得到想要的东西，是什么都在所不惜的。」

「啊！别对他不公道。」她答道，随即换了一种口吻补充道：「可是想想看，他竟就这样放你走了！」

「这么说，他是为了甩开我，好单独同你守在这里？」

「他总归是有某种动机的。」她承认道，「因为你*理应*留下。」

===Original===
“I shall stay!” I said.

She gave me her brightest smile.

“Really? Oh, how good of you! Or rather﻿—how brave of you, for it is certainly running a risk.”

If I had been decided before, I was doubly decided now.

===Chinese===
「我留下！」我说。

她给了我一个最明媚的微笑。

「真的吗？哦，你多好啊！倒不如说——你多勇敢啊，因为这分明是在冒险。」

如果说先前我已下定决心，现在便是决心加倍的决心。

===Original===
“It is not the German navy’s way to fear risks,” I said. “It is my duty to stay﻿—for two reasons﻿—and I am going to stay!”

“And Commander Wiedermann?”

“I shall simply tell him I am under higher orders, given me by Herr Tiel.”

“If you added that there is a second plan directed against the British navy, and that you are needed to advise on the details, it might help to convince Commander Wiedermann how essential your presence here is,” she suggested.

===Chinese===
「畏惧风险不是德国海军的作风。」我说，「留下来是我的职责——出于两个缘由——我这就留下！」

「那维德曼中校那边呢？」

「我就直说，我奉有蒂尔先生给我的更高一层的命令。」

「要是你再加上一句：还有一项针对英国海军的第二计划，需要你留下来商定细节，这或许有助于让维德曼中校相信，你留在这里何等必要。」她提议道。

===Original===
“Yes,” I agreed, “it would be well to mention that.”

“Also,” she said, “you would require to have all the details of this first plan so fully written out that he would not need to keep you to explain anything.”

“You think of everything!” I cried with an admiration I made no pretence of concealing. “I shall go now and set to work.”

===Chinese===
「对，」我表示赞同，「提一句这个也好。」

「还有，」她说，「你得把第一项计划的全盘细节都写得一清二楚，让他用不着把你扣下来讲解什么。」

「你真是事事都想到！」我喊道，钦佩之情毫不掩饰，「我这就去动手干活。」

===Original===
“Do!” she cried, “and when Tiel comes in I shall tell him you are going to stay. I wonder what he will say!”

“I wonder too,” said I. “But do you care what he says?”

“No,” she replied, “because of course he won’t say it. He will only think.”

“Let him think!” I laughed.

===Chinese===
「去吧！」她喊道，「等蒂尔回来，我就告诉他你要留下了。真不知他会说什么！」

「我也不知。」我说，「不过他说什么，你在乎吗？」

「不在乎。」她答道，「因为他自然什么也不会说出口。他只会想。」

「让他想去吧！」我笑道。

===Original===
I went back to my room in a strange state of exhilaration for a man who had just decided to forgo the thing he had most looked forward to, and run a horrible risk instead. For I felt in my bones that uniform or no uniform I should be shot if I were caught. I put little trust in English justice or clemency. But, as I said before, when I am obstinate, I am very obstinate; and I was firmly resolved that if Wiedermann wanted me back on board tonight, he would have to call a guard and carry me! However, acting on Eileen’s suggestions, I had little doubt I should convince him. And thereupon I set to work on my notes. By evening I had everything so fully written out and so clearly explained that I felt I could say with a clear conscience that even my own presence at a council of war could add no further information.

In the course of the day I had a talk with Tiel, and, just as Eileen had anticipated, he left one to guess at what was in his mind. He certainly professed to be glad I had changed my mind, and he thanked me with every appearance of cordiality.

“You are doing the right thing, Belke,” he said. “And, let me tell you, I appreciate your courage.”

===Chinese===
我回到自己房里，心里有一种奇异的兴奋——对一个刚刚决定放弃平生最期盼之事、转而去冒一场可怕风险的人来说，这兴奋未免反常。因为我骨子里明白，一旦被擒，无论穿没穿军装，我都难逃枪决。英国人的公正与宽仁，我是不大信的。可是，正如我先前所说，我一旦固执起来，便固执得厉害；我铁了心：今晚维德曼若要我回舰上，他就得叫一队卫兵来把我抬走！不过，依照艾琳的建议行事，我不怀疑自己能说得他信服。于是我便着手整理笔记。到傍晚，一切都写得完备、交代得清楚，我可以问心无愧地说：纵然我亲身出席一场军事会议，也再添不出半点新情况了。

白天里我同蒂尔谈了一次，果然不出艾琳所料，他任凭人去揣度他的心思。他当然表示很高兴我回心转意，谢我时也一副热诚模样，毫无破绽。

「你这样做是对的，贝尔克。」他说，「而且我告诉你，我敬重你的勇气。」

===Original===
There was a ring of evident sincerity in his voice as he said this, and whatever I might think of the man’s moral character, a compliment from Tiel on one’s courage was not a thing to despise.

In the late afternoon he set out to obtain a motorcar for the evening’s expedition, but through what ingenious machinery of lies he got it, I was too busy to inquire.

Finally, about ten o’clock at night we sat down to a little supper, my pockets bulging with my notes, and my cyclist’s overalls lying ready to be donned once more.

===Chinese===
他说这话时嗓音里分明有一股真诚，因此姑且不论此人人品如何，蒂尔夸人的勇气，倒不是可以轻慢的事。

傍晚时分他出去为夜里的行动张罗一辆汽车，至于他凭了何等精巧的谎言机器弄到手，我忙得无暇过问。

最后，夜里十点钟光景，我们坐下来吃了点宵夜，我口袋里塞满了笔记，那身摩托车手的连体服也摆在一旁，随时可以再穿上。

## IX On the Shore / 九 岸上

===Original===
Soon after eleven o’clock two dark figures slipped unostentatiously out of the back door, and a moment later a third followed them. My heart leapt with joy and surprise at the sight of it, and Tiel stopped and turned.

“What’s the matter?” he asked.

“I’m coming too,” said Eileen.

“Why?” he demanded in that tone of his which seemed to call upon the questioned to answer with exceeding accuracy.

“Because I’d like a drive,” she answered, with a woman’s confidence that her reason is good enough for anybody.

“As you please,” he said, drily and with unfathomable calm; and then he turned again, and in a voice that betrayed his interest in her, asked, “What have you got on?”

===Chinese===
十一点刚过，两条黑影不动声色地从后门溜了出去，片刻之后，第三条影子跟了上来。一见这情形，我的心又惊又喜地一跳，蒂尔停住脚，回过头来。

「怎么了？」他问。

「我也去。」艾琳说。

「为什么？」他以他那种腔调质问，仿佛在责令被问者以极高的精确度作答。

「因为我想坐一趟车。」她答道，带着女人特有的自信——她的理由对谁都够用了。

「随你的便。」他说，干巴巴的，平静得深不可测；随即他又回过头，用一种泄露了他对她的挂虑的嗓音问道：「你身上穿的够不够？」

===Original===
“Quite enough, thank you.”

“You are sure? I’ve lent my spare coat to Belke, but I can get another rug.”

“I am quite sure,” she smiled.

More than ever I felt glad I was staying beside her.

===Chinese===
「尽够了，多谢。」

「你确定？我那件备用的外套借给贝尔克了，不过我还可以再拿条毯子。」

「我确定。」她微笑道。

我比什么时候都庆幸自己留在了她身边。

===Original===
Tiel sat in front and drove, and Eileen and I got in behind. He offered no objections to this arrangement, though as she seated herself while he was starting the engine, he was certainly not given much choice. And then with a deep purr we rolled off into the night.

There would be no moon till getting on towards morning, but the rain had luckily ceased and the wind fallen, and overhead the stars were everywhere breaking through the last wisps of cloud. Already they gave light enough to distinguish sea from land quite plainly, and very soon they faintly lit the whole wide treeless countryside. The car was a good one, however Tiel had come by it, and the engine was pulling well, and we swept along the lonely roads at a great pace, one bare telegraph post after another flitting swiftly out of the gloom ahead into the gloom behind, and the night air rushing against our faces. At first I looked round me and recognised some features of the way we had come, the steep hill, and the sound that led to the western ocean, and the dark mass of hills beyond, but very soon my thoughts and my eyes alike had ceased to wander out of the car.

We said little, just enough to serve as an excuse for my looking constantly at her profile, and, the longer I looked, admiring the more every line and every curve. All at once she leaned towards me and said in a low beseeching voice﻿—

===Chinese===
蒂尔坐在前座开车，艾琳和我坐在后面。他对这个安排未表异议，不过她趁他发动引擎时坐了进来，他也实在没得选择。随后，随着一阵低沉的嗡鸣，我们驶入了夜色。

天快亮之前都不会有月亮，好在雨已幸而停歇，风也止了，头顶上星星正处处钻出最后几缕残云。星光已经足以清清楚楚地分辨海与陆，不多时，便淡淡地照亮了整片开阔而无树的乡野。车是辆好车——不论蒂尔是怎么弄来的——引擎牵引有力，我们沿着孤寂的道路疾驰，一根根光秃秃的电线杆从前方的幽暗中飞快掠过，隐入身后的幽暗，夜风扑面而来。起初我还环顾四周，认出了我们来路上的一些地貌：那道陡坡，那条通往西面大洋的海峡，以及远处黑黢黢的山影；但很快，我的思绪和我的眼睛便都再无心离开车厢半步。

我们话说得很少，刚够给我不时凝望她的侧影找个借口；而我望得越久，就越赞叹那每一道线条、每一分曲线。忽然，她朝我倾过身来，低声恳求道——

===Original===
“You will come back, won’t you?”

“I swear it!” I answered fervently, and to give force to my oath I gently took her hand and pressed it. If it did not return the pressure, it at least did not shrink from my clasp. And for the rest of the way I sat holding it.

Presently I in turn leaned towards her and whispered﻿—

“One thing I have been wondering. Should I take Tiel with me to see Wiedermann? It might perhaps be expected.”

===Chinese===
「你会回来的，是不是？」

「我发誓！」我热切地答道，并为给我的誓言添一份分量，轻轻执起她的手握了握。那只手纵然没有回握，至少也没有从我掌中抽走。余下的一路，我便一直握着它。

不久，我也朝她倾过身去，悄声说——

「有件事我一直在琢磨。我去见维德曼，该不该带蒂尔同去？说不定人家有此指望。」

===Original===
“No!” she replied emphatically.

“You feel sure?”

For reply she very gently pressed my hand at last. So confident did I feel of her sure judgment that I considered that question settled.

“By the way,” she said in a moment, “I think perhaps it might be advisable to say nothing to Commander Wiedermann about me. It is quite unnecessary, and he﻿—well, some men are always suspicious if they think there is a woman in the case. Of course I admit they sometimes have enough excuse, but﻿—what do you think?”

===Chinese===
「不！」她断然答道。

「你很确定？」

作为回答，她终于十分轻柔地握了握我的手。我对她可靠的判断如此信赖，当即认定此事已成定论。

「对了，」过了一会儿她说，「我看最好还是不要向维德曼中校提起我。这全无必要，而且他——唉，有些男人一旦觉得事情里头有个女人，就总要起疑。当然我承认，他们有时也不无理由，可是——你说呢？」

===Original===
“I agree with you entirely,” I said emphatically.

I know Wiedermann very intimately, and had been divided in mind whether I should drop a little hint that there were consolations, or whether I had better not. Now I saw quite clearly I had better not.

“What’s that?” said Eileen in a moment.

It was a tall gaunt monolith close to the roadside, and then, looking round, I saw a loch on the other side, and remembered the spot with a start. It was close by here that my cycle had broken down, and we were almost at the end of our drive. Round the corner we swung, straight for the sea, until we stopped where the road ended at the edge of the links.

===Chinese===
「我完全同意你的看法。」我郑重地说。

我同维德曼熟得很，原本还拿不定主意，要不要暗示他一句：此行亦有慰藉；还是不说为妙。此刻我看得明明白白：还是不说为妙。

「那是什么？」过了一会儿艾琳说。

那是路边一根瘦高嶙峋的石柱；再一环顾，我又看见另一侧有一片湖，猛地记起了这个地方。我的摩托车正是在这附近抛的锚，我们也差不多到了行程的尽头。我们拐过弯，直朝海边驶去，最后停在大路尽头、海岸草地的边缘。

===Original===
I gave Eileen’s hand one last swift pressure, and jumped out.

“We shall wait for you here,” said Tiel in a low voice, “but don’t be longer than you can help. Remember my nerves!”

He spoke so cheerily and genially, that for the moment I liked him again. In fact, if it had not been for Eileen, and his love of mystery, there was much that was very attractive in Tiel. As I set out on my solitary walk down to the shore, I suddenly wondered what made him so cheerful and bright at this particular moment, for it did not strike me as an exhilarating occasion. And then I was reminded of the man I had known most like Tiel, a captain I once served under, who was silence and calmness itself at most times, but grew strangely genial on critical occasions﻿—a heaven-sent gift. But from Tiel’s point of view, what was critical about this moment? The risk he ran at this hour in such an isolated spot was almost negligible, and as to the other circumstances, did it matter much to him whether I stayed or changed my mind and went away? I could scarcely believe it.

I kept along by the side of the sandy track, just as I had done before, only this time I did not lose it. The rolling hummocky links were a little darker, but the stars shone in myriads, bright and clear as a winter’s night, and I could see my way well enough. As I advanced, I smelt the same pungent seaweed odour, and heard the same gulls crying, disturbed (I hoped) by the same monster in the waters. Fortunately the storm had blown from the southeast, and the sea in this westward-facing bay heaved quietly, reflecting the radiance of the stars. It was another perfect night for our purpose.

===Chinese===
我最后飞快地握了一下艾琳的手，跳下车。

「我们就在这儿等你。」蒂尔低声说，「不过能多快就多快。记住，我神经脆弱！」

他说得那样快活和气，一时之间我又喜欢起他来。说实在的，若不是为了艾琳的缘故，也抛开他那股神秘癖，蒂尔身上原有许多非常招人喜欢的地方。当我独自一人朝岸边走去时，忽然纳闷起来：偏偏在这个时刻，是什么让他这样兴致勃勃、满面春风？在我看来这可不是个叫人振奋的场合。于是我想起我所认识的人里最像蒂尔的一个——我曾在其麾下服役的一位上校——此人平日沉默冷静到了极点，可一到紧要关头反而变得出奇地和蔼，这是天赐的本事。可是在蒂尔看来，此刻有什么紧要可言？这个时辰、这样一处僻静地方，他冒的风险几乎微不足道；至于别的情况，我留下也好，回心转意走掉也好，与他何干？我简直不能相信。

我沿着沙径一侧走，同上回一模一样，只是这一回没有迷路。起伏如丘的海岸草地略显得暗些，但繁星漫天，明亮澄澈一如冬夜，认路尽够了。往前走，我又闻到那股刺鼻的海藻腥气，听到同样的鸥鸟啼鸣——它们（我希望）是被水中那同一头怪物惊扰的。幸而风暴是从东南刮来的，这个朝西的海湾里，海水平静地起伏着，映出满天星辉。对我们此行的目的而言，又是一个再好不过的夜晚。

===Original===
I reached the shore and turned to the left along the rising circumference of the bay, looking hard into the night as I went. Something dark lay on the water, I felt certain of it, and presently something else dark and upright loomed ahead. A moment later I had grasped Wiedermann by the hand. He spoke but a word of cordial greeting, and then turned to descend to the boat.

“We’ll get aboard before we talk,” said he.

The difficult moment had come. Frankly, I had dreaded it a little, but it had to be faced and got over.

“I am not coming aboard tonight, sir,” I replied.

===Chinese===
我到了岸边，向左沿着海湾渐升的弧线走去，一路使劲望进夜色里。水上躺着一样黑影，我对此确信不疑，不多时，前方又隐隐现出另一个黑黢黢直立的身影。片刻之后，我已握住维德曼的手。他只说了一句热络的问候，便转身朝小艇走下去。

「上船再谈。」他说。

难堪的时刻到了。坦白说，我一直有点怕它，可它终究得面对、得熬过去。

「今晚我不上船了，长官。」我答道。

===Original===
He turned and stared at me.

“Haven’t you settled anything?” he demanded.

“Something,” I said, “but there is more to be done.”

I told him then concisely and clearly what we had arranged, and handed him the chart and all my notes. That he was honestly delighted with my news, and satisfied with my own performance, there could be no doubt. He shook me warmly by the hand and said﻿—

===Chinese===
他转过身，瞪着我。

「你什么都没谈妥吗？」他质问。

「谈妥了一些，」我说，「可还有后续要办。」

于是我简明扼要地把我们的安排讲给他听，把海图和全部笔记交到他手上。他这消息听得由衷欢喜，对我的表现也颇满意，这是毫无疑问的。他热忱地同我握手，说道——

===Original===
“Splendid, Belke! I knew we could count on you! It’s lucky you have a chest broad enough to hold all your decorations! For you will get them﻿—never doubt it. But what is all this about staying on shore? What else are you needed for? And who the devil has given you such orders?”

“Herr Tiel,” I said. “I was placed under his orders, as you will remember, sir.”

“But what does he want you for? And how long does he imagine the British are going to let you stay in this house of yours unsuspected? They are not idiots! It seems to me you have been extraordinarily lucky to have escaped detection so far. Surely you are not going to risk a longer stay?”

“If it is my duty I must run the risk.”

===Chinese===
「好极了，贝尔克！我就知道靠得住你！幸亏你胸脯够宽，挂得下你将来那一枚枚勋章！勋章你是拿定了的——别怀疑。可留岸上算怎么回事？还有什么用得着你？到底是谁他妈的给你下了这种命令？」

「蒂尔先生。」我说，「您总记得，长官，我原是奉命听他调遣的。」

「可他要你干什么？他还以为英国人会放你在你那所宅子里待多久而不起疑？他们不是白痴！依我看，你至今没被察觉，已是走了天大的运。你总不至于要冒险久留吧？」

「倘若这是我的职责，风险我必须担。」

===Original===
“But is it your duty? I am just wondering, Belke, whether I can spare you, with this attack coming on, and whether I ought to override Herr Tiel’s orders and damn the consequences!”

I knew his independence and resolution, but just at that moment there passed before my mind’s eye such a distinct, sweet picture of Eileen, that I was filled with a resolution and independence even greater than his.

“If it were not my duty, sir,” I said firmly, “clearly and strongly pointed out by Herr Tiel, I should never dream of asking you to spare me for a little longer.”

“He was then very clear and strong on the question?”

===Chinese===
「可这真是你的职责吗？贝尔克，我正在琢磨，眼看进攻在即，我是不是离得开你；也在琢磨我是不是该把蒂尔先生的命令踩在脚下，管它什么后果！」

我知道他的果决与主见，可恰恰在那一刻，艾琳的身影那么清晰、那么甜美地掠过我眼前，使我胸中涌起一股比他更甚的果决与主见。

「倘若这不是我的职责，长官，」我坚定地说，「倘若不是蒂尔先生明明白白、着重向我指出的职责，我做梦也不会开口求您再宽限我些时日。」

「那么在这件事上，他说得又明白又坚决？」

===Original===
“Extremely.”

“And this other scheme of his﻿—do you feel yourself that it is feasible enough to justify you in leaving your ship and running such a terrible risk? Remember, you will be a man lost to Germany!”

I have put down exactly what he said, though it convicts me of having departed a little from the truth when I answered﻿—

“Yes, it will justify the risk.”

===Chinese===
「坚决之至。」

「还有他那另一项计划——你自己觉得，它当真可行到值得你离开军舰、去冒这样可怕的险吗？记住，你这一去，对德国来说就是个回不来的人了！」

我把他原话一字不差地记在这里，尽管这恰恰坐实了我回答时稍稍偏离了事实——

「是的，它担得起这场风险。」

===Original===
After all, I had confidence enough in Tiel’s abilities to feel sure that I was really justified in saying this; but I determined to press him for some details of his plans tomorrow.

Wiedermann stood silent for a moment; then he held out his hand and said in a sad voice﻿—

“Goodbye! But my mind misgives me. I fear we may never meet again.”

“That is nonsense, sir!” I cried as cheerfully as I could. “We shall meet again very soon. And if you wish something to cheer you, just study those plans!”

===Chinese===
毕竟，我对蒂尔的本事有足够的信心，觉得这句话说得并非无据；但我打定主意，明天定要逼他交代他那些计划的若干细节。

维德曼默立了片刻，然后伸出手来，伤感地说——

「再见！可我心里直打鼓。我怕我们再也见不着了。」

「那是胡说，长官！」我尽量快活地喊道，「我们很快就会再见的。您若想要点提神的东西，就把那些计划好好研读一番吧！」

===Original===
And so we parted, he descending the bank without another word, and I setting out along the path that by now was beginning to feel quite familiar. I did not even pause to look back this time. My boats were burnt and I felt it was better to hurry on without dwelling longer on the parting. Besides, there was a meeting awaiting me.

When I reached the end of the road, I found that Tiel had been spending the time in turning the car, and now he and Eileen stood beside it, but apparently not conversing.

“All right?” he asked.

“Yes,” I said. “I met Wiedermann and gave him all the plans.”

===Chinese===
我们就这样别过了。他一言不发地下堤坡去了，我则顺着那条小径上路——如今这路已开始让人感到颇为熟稔。这一回我甚至没有停下回头望。我的船已烧了，我觉得还是快些赶路，别在离别上多作盘桓为好。再说，还有一场会面等着我呢。

我回到大路尽头，发现蒂尔一直利用这段时间把车调了头。此刻他和艾琳站在车旁，却显然并没有交谈。

「顺利吗？」他问。

「顺利。」我说，「见到维德曼了，计划全交给他了。」

===Original===
He merely nodded and went to start the engine. Again I was forcibly reminded of my old captain, and the way in which he became calmer and more silent than ever the moment the crisis was passed. But surely this crisis had been mine and not his! Anyhow, I felt a singularly strong sense of reaction and seated myself beside Eileen without a word. We had gone for a little way on our homeward road before either of us spoke, and then it was to exchange some quite ordinary remark. I put out my hand gently, but hers was nowhere to be found, and this increased my depression. I fell very silent, and then suddenly, when we were nearly back, I exclaimed﻿—

“I wonder whether you are really glad that I returned?”

“Very!” she said, and there was such deep sincerity in her voice that the cloud began to lift at once.

Yet I was not in high spirits when I reentered my familiar room.

===Chinese===
他只点了点头，便去发动引擎。我又一次不由得想起我那位老上校，想起危机一过他便愈发沉静寡言的那副样子。可这一次的危机分明是我的，不是他的！不管怎样，我心里涌起一股异样强烈的激荡之后的疲怠，一言不发地在艾琳身边坐下。回程走了一小段，我们谁也没开口，开口之后也不过是说了句极寻常的话。我轻轻伸出手去，她的手却无处可寻，这更添了我的黯然。我彻底沉默下来，直到快到家时，才突然脱口喊道——

「不知你对我回来，是不是真心觉得高兴？」

「真心高兴！」她说，嗓音里的诚挚那样深，我心头的阴云顿时开始消散。

然而，当我重新走进那间熟悉的屋子时，心里仍不见轻松。

# Part IV — Lieutenant von Belke’s Narrative Concluded / 第四部分 — 冯·贝尔克中尉自述（完）

## I Wednesday / 一 星期三

===Original===
I woke on Wednesday morning with an outlook so changed that I felt as if some magician must have altered my nature. Theoretically I had taken a momentous and dangerous decision at the call of duty, and all my energies ought to have concentrated on the task of carrying it through safely, thoroughly, and warily. I had need of more caution than ever, and of the most constant vigilance﻿—both for the sake of my skin and my country. As a matter of fact I was possessed with the recklessness of a man drifting on a plank down a rapid, where taking thought will not serve him an iota. In vain I preached theoretical caution to myself﻿—exactly how vainly may be judged by my first performance in the morning when I found myself alone with Eileen in the parlour. She suggested that for my own sake I had better be getting back to my room.

“Will you come and sit there with me?” I asked.

“I may pay a call upon you perhaps.”

“After hours of loneliness! And then leave me lonelier than ever! No, thank you, I shall stay down here.”

===Chinese===
星期三早晨醒来，我的整个心境已变得面目全非，仿佛有哪位魔术师改换了我的天性。理论上说，我已在职责的召唤下作出了一个重大而危险的决定，全部精力本该集中于把这件事办得安全、周全、审慎。我比以往任何时候都更需要小心，需要最经常的警觉——既为我这条性命，也为我的国家。可实际上，我却满怀着一种亡命之徒的莽撞，就像一个人抱着木板漂下急流，再多盘算也无济于事。我白费力气地向自己宣讲理论上的谨慎——到底有多白费，只看我早晨头一件事便可知晓：那会儿我正和艾琳单独待在客厅里。她提出，为我自己着想，我还是回楼上的好。

「你愿意来陪我坐在那儿吗？」我问。

「我或许可以来拜访你一回。」

「先让我孤零零熬上几个钟头！然后再把我撇得比先前更孤单！不，多谢了，我就待在楼下。」

===Original===
“In your uniform?” she asked, opening her eyes a little. “No, no, Mr. Belke!”

“Well then, get me a suit of mufti!”

She looked at me hard.

“You will really run that risk?”

===Chinese===
「穿着你这身军装？」她微微睁大了眼睛问，「不行，不行，贝尔克先生！」

「那就给我弄套便服来！」

她紧紧盯着我看。

「你真要冒那个险？」

===Original===
“It is now worth it,” I said with meaning.

She looked away, and for a moment I thought she was pained﻿—not displeased, I am sure, but as if something had given her a pang of sorrow. Then the look passed, and she cried﻿—

“Well, if Tiel agrees!”

“Tiel be hanged! I don’t care what he says!”

===Chinese===
「如今这险冒得值了。」我意味深长地说。

她把目光移开了，一时之间我以为她心里难受——我敢肯定不是不快，倒像是什么东西刺得她隐隐作痛。随后这神色过去了，她扬声道——

「好吧，只要蒂尔答应！」

「让蒂尔见鬼去吧！他说什么我才不管！」

===Original===
She began to smile.

“Do you propose to wear my clothes?” she inquired.

“Yours!” I exclaimed.

“Otherwise,” she continued, “you must persuade Tiel to agree, for it is only he who can provide you with a suit of mufti.”

===Chinese===
她不禁莞尔。

「你打算穿我的衣服不成？」她问道。

「穿你的！」我叫起来。

「要不然，」她接着说，「你就得说服蒂尔答应了，因为能给你弄到一套便服的，只有他一个人。」

===Original===
Presently Tiel came in and I put the demand to him at once. He looked a little surprised, but, somewhat to my surprise, raised no serious objections. His motives are hard to fathom, but I cannot help suspecting that despite his air of self-confidence and authority, he has an instinctive respect for an officer and acknowledges in his heart that I am really his superior.

“You mustn’t go outside the house, of course,” he said, “and if by any evil chance any visitor were to come in unexpectedly, you must have some kind of a story ready.”

“Have you had many visitors yet?” I asked with a touch of sarcasm.

“You never know your luck,” said he, “and I believe in guarding against all chances. If you are surprised, please remember that your name is Mr. Wilson.”

===Chinese===
不多时蒂尔进来，我立刻向他提出了这个要求。他显得有点意外，但颇出乎我意料的是，并未认真反对。他的动机难以揣度，可我不由得疑心：尽管他一副自信威权的做派，骨子里却对军官怀着一种本能的敬重，心底里承认我其实是他上司。

「当然，你不得走出这所宅子。」他说，「万一哪个倒霉时刻有客人不速而至，你手头得备好一套说辞。」

「你们至今到过许多客人吗？」我略带讥诮地问。

「运气的事谁说得准。」他说，「我信奉的是有备无患。倘若你被人撞见了，请记住，你名叫威尔逊先生。」

===Original===
“Wilson?” I said with some disgust. “Am I named in honour of that swine in America?”

“You are named Wilson,” said he, “because it is very like Watson and Williams and several other common names. The less conspicuous and more easily forgotten a name one takes, the better.”

There is no doubt about the thoroughness of the man and the cunning with which he lays even the smallest plans, and though I was a little contemptuous of his finesse at the moment, I must confess I was thankful enough for it not so very long afterwards.

“As for your business,” added Tiel, “you are a Government inspector.”

===Chinese===
「威尔逊？」我不无嫌恶地说，「取这名字，是向美国那头猪致意么？」

「你叫威尔逊，」他说，「是因为它同沃森、威廉斯以及另外几个寻常姓氏都很相像。用的名字越不显眼、越容易被忘掉越好。」

此人办事的周密，连同他连最细小的计划都安排得工于心计，是毋庸置疑的；尽管当时我对这套手腕有点不屑，但我不久之后便不得不承认，多亏了它，我感激还来不及呢。

「至于你的职业，」蒂尔补充道，「你是位政府稽查员。」

===Original===
“Of what?” I asked.

“If you are asked, look deep and say nothing,” said he. “The islands are full of people on what they call in the Navy ‘hush’ jobs.”

“You seem pretty intimately acquainted with the British Navy down to its slang,” I observed.

My nerves were perhaps a little strained this morning, and I meant by this to make a sarcastic allusion to the kind of blackguards he dealt with﻿—such as Ashington. I glanced at Eileen as I spoke, and I was surprised to see a sudden look, almost of alarm, in her eye. It was turned on Tiel, but he appeared absolutely indifferent. I presumed she feared he might take offence and make a row, but she need not have worried. It would take a very pointed insult to rouse that calculating machine.

===Chinese===
「稽查什么？」我问。

「若有人问起，你就高深莫测地看人一眼，什么也别说。」他说，「这几座岛上到处都是干海军里所谓『机密』差事的人。」

「你同英国海军混得可真熟，连他们的行话都通。」我说。

那天早晨我的神经或许有点紧绷，这话是存心讥刺他打交道的那些无赖——比如阿兴顿之流。说话时我瞥了艾琳一眼，却吃惊地看见她眼中骤然掠过近乎惊惶的神色。那神色是冲着蒂尔去的，他却显出全然无所谓的样子。我猜她是怕他动怒闹将起来，其实她大可不必担心。要惊动那台精于算计的机器，得相当露骨的侮辱才成。

===Original===
“Can you get a suit of mufti for me?” I inquired.

“I’ll look one out presently,” said he.

“I presume you keep a few disguises!” I added.

“A few,” said he with one of his brief smiles. “You had better go up to your room in the meantime, and I’ll bring it to you.”

===Chinese===
「你能给我弄套便服吗？」我问。

「回头我挑一套出来。」他说。

「我猜你备着几套化装行头吧！」我补了一句。

「几套。」他说，脸上闪过他那短短一笑，「你先回楼上去，我给你送去。」

===Original===
I fumed at the idea of any delay, and as I went to the door I said﻿—

“Don’t be long about it, please!”

More and more the thought of leaving those two alone together, even for a short while, filled me with angry uneasiness, and I paced my bedroom floor impatiently enough. Judge then of my relief and delight when within a few minutes Eileen knocked at my door and said﻿—

“I have come to pay you a morning call if I may.”

===Chinese===
一想到还要耽搁我便窝火，走到门口时我说——

「请别磨蹭太久！」

一想到把那两个人单独留在一起，哪怕只留一小会儿，我心里就越来越填满愠怒的不安，在卧室的地板上相当不耐烦地踱来踱去。于是不难想见，不出几分钟艾琳便来敲我的门、说了下面这句话时，我是何等如释重负、何等欢喜——

「要是可以，我来对你作个晨间拜访。」

===Original===
I began to wish then that Herr Tiel would spend an hour or two in looking out clothes for me, and as a matter of fact he did. Eileen explained that he had said he must do some errand in his capacity as parish minister, but what the mystery-monger was really about, Heaven knows!

“Now,” said I to Eileen, when we were seated and I had lit a cigarette, “I want to ask you something about this new scheme that we three are embarked upon.”

She began to shake her head at once.

“I am very much in the dark,” said she. “Tiel tells me as little as he tells you.”

===Chinese===
从那时起我便巴不得蒂尔先生为我挑衣服挑上一两个钟头，而事实上他也果真如此。艾琳解释说，他声称须以教区牧师的身份出去办点事，可那位卖关子的大行家究竟去干了什么，只有天知道！

「好了，」等我们坐定、我点上一支烟，我对艾琳说，「关于我们三个携手干上的这项新计划，我有话要问你。」

她立刻摇起头来。

「我两眼一抹黑。」她说，「蒂尔对我瞒多少，对你也瞒多少。」

===Original===
“You must surely know one thing. What is your own part in it? Why were you brought into the islands? Such risks are not run for nothing.”

“What is a woman’s part in such a plan usually?” she asked in a quiet voice.

I was a little taken aback. It was not exactly pleasant to think of﻿—in connection with Eileen.

“I believe they sometimes act as decoys,” I said bluntly.

===Chinese===
「有一件事你总该知道。你自己在其中扮的是什么角色？为什么要把你带进这几座岛？这样的险不会是白冒的。」

「这类计划里，女人通常扮的是什么角色？」她声音平静地问。

我不由得怔了一下。这种事——同艾琳联系起来想——并不怎么愉快。

「我相信她们有时充当诱饵。」我直截了当地说。

===Original===
She merely nodded.

“Then that is your role?”

“I presume so,” she said frankly.

“Who are you going to decoy?” I asked, and I felt that my voice was harsh.

===Chinese===
她只点了点头。

「那么这就是你的角色？」

「我想是吧。」她坦然说。

「你打算去诱谁？」我问，只觉得自己的嗓音发狠。

===Original===
“Ask Herr Tiel,” she answered.

“Not that gross brute Ashington surely!”

She shook her head emphatically, and I felt a little relieved.

“You have seen for yourself that he needs no further decoying,” she said.

===Chinese===
「问蒂尔先生去。」她答道。

「总不会是阿兴顿那头蠢笨的畜生吧！」

她用力摇头，我心里稍觉宽慰。

「你自己也看见了，他已用不着再诱。」她说。

===Original===
“Then it must be some even higher game you’re to be flown at.”

“I wonder!” she said, and smiled a little.

I hated to see her smile.

“I don’t like to think of you doing this,” I exclaimed suddenly.

===Chinese===
「那么你要被放出去对付的，必是更高级的猎物了。」

「谁知道呢！」她说，微微笑了笑。

我见不得她这样笑。

「我不愿意想你干这种事。」我突然喊道。

===Original===
“Not even for Germany?” she asked.

I was silenced, but my blood continued to boil at the thought of what might not be asked of her.

“Would you go to any lengths,” I asked abruptly.

“For my country I would, to any lengths!” she answered proudly.

===Chinese===
「哪怕是为了德国？」她问。

我语塞了，可一想到她兴许被要求做的事，血仍在一个劲儿地沸腾。

「你肯做到任何地步么？」我唐突地问。

「为了我的国家，我肯，任何地步！」她骄傲地答道。

===Original===
Again I felt rebuked, yet still more savage at the thought.

“You would even become some British Admiral’s mistress?” I asked in a low voice.

Her colour suddenly rose, and for an instant she seemed to start. Then in rather a cool voice she said﻿—

“Perhaps we are thinking of rather different things.”

===Chinese===
我又一次觉得受了训斥，可想到这里，心头反而愈加狂躁。

「你甚至肯去做哪位英国海军上将的情妇？」我低声问。

她的脸色骤然涨红，一瞬之间似乎怔了一怔。随后她用相当冷淡的声音说——

「也许我们想的根本不是一回事。」

===Original===
And with that she changed the subject, nor could I induce her to return to it. I admit frankly I was a little puzzled. Her reception of my question, perfectly honestly put, had been curiously unlike the candour I should have expected in a girl of her strange profession, especially considering her defiance of all conventionalities in living alone here with two men, and sitting at this moment in the room of one of them. I respected her the more for her hint of affronted dignity. Yet I confess I felt bewildered.

How long we had talked I know not, when at last Tiel appeared, bringing a very presentable tweed suit, and then they both left me, and I did the one thing I had so firmly resolved not to do. I discarded my uniform with what protection it gave me, and made myself liable to be shot without question or doubt. Yet my only feeling was gladness that I need no longer stay cooped up in my room while those two spent their hours together downstairs.

That afternoon, when we were all three together, I asked Tiel for some definite information regarding his scheme, and we had a long, and I must say a very interesting, talk. The details of this plan it would scarcely be safe to put down on paper at present. Or rather, I should say, the outline of it, for we have scarcely reached the stage of details yet. It is a bold scheme, as was only to be expected of Tiel, and necessitated going very thoroughly into the relative naval strengths of Germany and Britain, so that most of the time for the rest of the day was taken up with a discussion of facts and figures. And through it all Eileen sat listening. I wonder if such a talk ever before had such a charming background?

===Chinese===
说完她便把话头岔开，我再怎么引她也引不回来。坦白承认，我有点困惑了。我这问题问得光明磊落，她的反应却古怪得很，全然不似我料想中她那种奇特行当的姑娘该有的坦率——尤其是想到她全然无视世俗常规，独身与两个男人同住一处，此刻还坐在其中一人的房间里。她那一点自尊受犯的意味，反而让我愈发敬重她。然而我承认，我心里一团迷惑。

我们谈了多久我已记不清，末了蒂尔总算露面，带来一套相当体面的粗花呢衣裳。随后他们俩都离了我那间屋，而我也便做下了那件我一度痛下决心绝不做的事。我脱去了军装，连同它所能给我的那点庇护，使自己落到了一被抓着便可毫无疑义地枪毙的境地。然而我心中唯一的滋味竟是欢喜：再也不必闷守在自己房里，听凭那两位在楼下双双消磨时光了。

那天下午，我们三个聚在一处时，我向蒂尔讨有关他计划的确切内容，我们谈了很久，而且我必须说，谈得极有趣味。这项计划的细节，眼下写到纸面上还欠稳妥。或者不如说，是它的轮廓，因为细节这一步我们还几乎未曾走到。这是一项大胆的计划——不出蒂尔之所料地大胆——并且必须极透彻地钻研德英两国海军实力的消长，于是那天余下的光阴，大半都耗费在对事实与数字的讨论上了。而这一切时间，艾琳都坐在一旁听着。这样的谈话，从前可曾有过这样迷人的背景么？

===Original===
Now at last I am in my room, writing this narrative up to this very point. It is long past midnight, but sleep is keeping very far away from me. The weather has changed to a steady drizzle of rain. Outside, the night is black as pitch, and mild and windless. It may partly be this close damp air that drives sleep away, but I know it is something else as well.

I am actually wondering if I can marry her! She must surrender; that is certain, for I have willed it, and what a German wills with all his soul takes place. It must! As to her heart, I feel sure that her kindness means what a woman’s kindness always means﻿—that a man has only to persevere. But marriage?

I shall never meet another woman like her; that is certain! Yet an adventuress, a paid agent of the Secret Service, marrying a von Belke﻿—is it quite conceivable? On the whole I think *no*. But we can be very happy without that! I never loved a woman so much before﻿—that is my last word for the night!

===Chinese===
此刻我终于坐在自己房里，把这份自述一路写到这里。午夜早过了，睡意却离我远远的。天气转成了绵绵细雨。屋外夜黑如漆，温和而无风。把这湿润沉闷的空气算上一半，或许可以解释睡意为何不至，但我知道还有别的原因。

我竟在琢磨我能不能娶她！她必将降服，这是确定无疑的，因为我已下了此愿，而德国人倾其灵魂所愿之事，必定成真。必定！至于她的心，我确信她的温柔所昭示的，正是女人的温柔永远昭示的东西——男人只消锲而不舍。可是婚姻呢？

我此生再不会遇见第二个她这样的女子，这是确定无疑的！然而，一个女冒险家、一个特务机关雇来的密探，嫁一个冯·贝尔克家的人——这当真可想么？总的说来，我以为*不然*。但没有这一纸婚书，我们也尽可以非常幸福！我从未这样爱过一个女子——这就是我今夜最后的一句话！

## II Thursday / 二 星期四

===Original===
*Friday morning (very early).*﻿—The events of yesterday and last night have left me with more to think about than I seem to have wits to think with. *Mein Gott*, if I could see daylight through everything! What is ahead, Heaven knows, but here is what is behind.

Yesterday morning passed as the afternoon before had passed, in further discussion of naval statistics with Tiel﻿—with a background of Eileen. Then we had lunch, and soon afterwards Tiel put on an oilskin coat and went out. A thin fine drizzle still filled the air, drifting in clouds before a rising wind and blotting out the view of the sea almost completely. Behind it the ships were doing we knew not what; certainly they were not firing, but we could see nothing of them at all.

A little later Eileen insisted on putting on a waterproof and going out too. As the minister’s sister she had to visit a farm, she said. I believed her, of course, though I had ceased to pay much attention to Tiel’s statements as to his movements. I knew that he knew his own business thoroughly, and I had ceased to mind if he had not the courtesy to take me into his confidence. After all, if I come safely out of this business, I am not likely to meet such as Tiel again!

Left to myself, I picked up a book and had been reading for about a quarter of an hour when I was conscious of a shadow crossing the window and heard a step on the gravel. Never doubting that it was either Eileen or Tiel, I still sat reading until I was roused by the sound of voices in the hall, just outside the parlour door. One I recognised as our servant’s, the other was a stranger’s. I dropped my book and started hastily to my feet, and as I did so I heard the stranger say﻿—

===Chinese===
*星期五清晨（极早）。*——昨天和昨夜的事，留给我的思虑，比我的脑力仿佛所能应付的还多。*Mein Gott*，但愿我能把这一切看个透亮！前头是什么，天晓得；以下是已经过去的。

昨天一个上午，是同前一天下午一样过去的：继续同蒂尔讨论海军统计数字——以艾琳为背景。然后我们吃了午饭，饭后不久，蒂尔披上油布雨衣出了门。细密的雨丝仍布满空中，在一阵渐起的风前成团飘移，几乎把海景遮了个严实。屏障后面，那些军舰在做什么我们无从知晓；它们肯定没有开炮，可我们连影子也看不见。

稍后，艾琳也执意披上雨衣出了门。她说，以牧师妹妹的身份，她得去一处农庄走访。我当然信她，虽然蒂尔对其行踪的说辞，我早已不大留心。我知道他把分内之事摸得烂熟，他若不赏脸让我与闻，我也不再介意了。说到底，倘若这桩事我能全身而退，此生谅也再不会遇见蒂尔这路人了！

屋里只剩我一人，我拿起一本书，读了约摸一刻钟，忽觉一道影子掠过窗子，又听见砾石路上一声脚步。我毫不怀疑来者不是艾琳便是蒂尔，兀自坐着读下去，直到客厅门外门厅里传来说话声，才惊觉起来。一个嗓音我听出是我们那位仆人的，另一个却陌生。我丢下书，慌忙起身，就在这时，我听见那陌生人说——

===Original===
“I tell you I recognise her coat. My good woman, d’ye think I’m blind? I’m coming in to wait for her, I tell you.”

The door opened, and a very large stout gentleman appeared, talking over his shoulder as he entered.

“When Miss Holland comes in, tell her Mr. Craigie is waiting to see her,” said he; and with that he closed the door and became aware of my presence.

For a moment we looked at one another. My visitor, I saw, had a grey beard, a large rosy face, and twinkling blue eyes. He looked harmless enough, but I eyed him very warily, as you can readily believe.

===Chinese===
「我说，我认出了她那件外套。好太太，你当我瞎了眼不成？我说了，我这就进去等她。」

门开了，进来一位块头很大、身材肥胖的绅士，进门时还扭头朝身后说着话。

「霍兰德小姐回来时，告诉她克雷格先生等着见她。」他说；说罢便关上门，这才发觉我在屋里。

我们彼此打量了片刻。我看出，来客生着灰白胡须、一张红润的大脸和滴溜溜的蓝眼睛。他瞧着全无危险，可我对他满眼戒备，这一点诸位不难想见。

===Original===
“It’s an awful wet day,” said he in a most friendly and affable tone.

I agreed that it was detestable.

“It’s fine for the crops all the same. The oats is looking very well; do you not think so?”

I perceived that my friend was an agriculturist, and endeavoured to humour him.

===Chinese===
「这天真湿得够呛。」他用一种极其友好和蔼的口吻说。

我附和说这天气可恶之极。

「不过对庄稼倒正合适。燕麦长势很好；您不这么看？」

我看出我这位朋友是位务农的，便竭力迁就他。

===Original===
“They are looking splendid!” I said with enthusiasm.

He sat down, and we exchanged a few more remarks on the weather and the crops, in the course of which he had filled and lit a pipe and made himself entirely at home.

“Are you staying with the minister?” he inquired presently.

“I am visiting him,” I replied evasively,

===Chinese===
「长势好极了！」我热情地说。

他坐下来，我们又就天气和庄稼攀谈了几句；谈话间他已装好烟斗点上，全然宾至如归了。

「您是来牧师家小住的？」他一会儿便问道。

「我是来拜访他的。」我含糊其辞地答道，

===Original===
“I understand Miss Holland’s here too,” said he, with an extra twinkle in his eye.

I knew, of course, that he must mean Eileen, and I must confess that I was devoured with curiosity.

“She is,” I said. “Do you know her?”

“Know her? She was my governess! Has she not told you the joke of how she left me in the lurch?”

===Chinese===
「听说霍兰德小姐也在这儿。」他说，眼里多闪了一下光。

我当然明白他指的必是艾琳，而我也必须承认，好奇心正把我啃得难受。

「她在。」我说，「您认识她？」

「认识？她是从我家的家庭教师！她把那样撂下我拔腿就走的事当笑话讲给您听过没有？」

===Original===
It flashed across my mind that it might seem odd if I were to admit that “Miss Holland” had said nothing about this mysterious adventure.

“Oh yes, she has told us all about it,” I replied with assurance.

Mr. Craigie laughed heartily at what was evidently a highly humorous recollection.

“I was as near being annoyed at the time as I ever was in my life,” said he. “But, man, I’ve had some proper laughs over it since.”

===Chinese===
我脑子里闪过一个念头：倘若承认「霍兰德小姐」对这桩奇遇只字未提，恐怕显得蹊跷。

「哦，讲了，她原原本本都讲给我们听了。」我一口咬定地答道。

克雷格先生想到那段显然妙趣横生的往事，放声大笑。

「当时我平生头一回差点动了气。」他说，「可是老兄，打那以后我可没少为它开怀大笑。」

===Original===
He suddenly grew a trifle graver.

“Mrs. Craigie isn’t laughing, though. Between ourselves, it’s she that’s sent me on this errand today.”

He winked and nodded and relit his pipe, while I endeavoured to see a little light through the extraordinary confusion of ideas which his remarks had caused in my mind.

“Miss Holland came up to the islands as your governess, I understand,” I said in as matter-of-fact tone as I could compass.

===Chinese===
他忽然郑重了些许。

「不过克雷格太太可笑不出来。咱俩私下说，今天派我来办这趟差事的，正是她。」

他挤挤眼、点点头，重新点着烟斗；我则竭力想从他这番话在我心里搅出的一团乱麻里，理出点亮光来。

「据我所知，霍兰德小姐是来这几座岛上当您家的家庭教师的。」我尽量用就事论事的口吻说。

===Original===
“We got her through a Mrs. Armitage in Kensington,” said Mr. Craigie. “It seemed all right﻿—and mind you, I’m not saying it isn’t all right now! Only between you and me, Mr.﻿—?”

“Wilson,” I said promptly, breathing my thanks to Tiel at the same time.

“You’ll be a relation of the minister’s too, perhaps?”

“I am on government business,” I replied in a suitable tone of grave mystery.

===Chinese===
「我们是通过肯辛顿一位阿米蒂奇太太把她请来的。」克雷格先生说，「当初看去一切正常——您听着，我如今也并不是说它不正常！只是你我私下说说，您贵姓是——？」

「威尔逊。」我应声答道，同时在心里向蒂尔千恩万谢。

「那么您想必也是牧师的亲戚？」

「我是因公而来。」我用一种恰到好处的凝重而神秘的口吻答道。

===Original===
“Damn it, Mr. Wilson,” exclaimed my friend with surprising energy, “everyone in the country seems to be on government business nowadays﻿—except myself! And I’ve got to pay their salaries! We’re asked in the catechism what’s our business in this weary world, and damn it, I can answer that conundrum now! It’s just to pay government officials their wages, and build a dozen or two new Dreadnoughts, and send six million peaceable men into the army, and fill a pile of shells with trinitrol-globule-paralysis, or whatever they call the stuff, and all this on the rental of an estate which was just keeping me comfortably in tobacco before this infernal murdering business began! Do you know what I’d do with that Kaiser if I caught him?”

I looked as interested as possible, and begged for information.

“I’d give him my wife and my income, and see how he liked the mess he’s landed me in!”

Though Mr. Craigie had spoken with considerable vehemence, he had not looked at all fierce, and now his not usually very intellectual face began to assume a thoughtful expression.

===Chinese===
「真是见鬼，威尔逊先生，」我的朋友以出人意料的劲头嚷道，「如今这国家里仿佛人人都在办公务——就除了我！偏又得由我来付他们的薪水！教义问答问我们：在这劳什子世上，我们奉差做什么营生？见鬼，如今这道难题我能答了！就是给官老爷们发俸禄，再添造一二十艘无畏舰，把六百万安分人送进军队，往成堆的炮弹里灌满『三硝基—小球子—麻痹症』——随他们管那玩意儿叫什么吧——而这一切全得从一座田产的租金里出；在这场该死的杀人买卖开张之前，那点租金刚够我舒舒服服抽口烟！您可知道，我若逮住了那位德国皇帝，会拿他怎么办？」

我做出尽可能饶有兴致的样子，恳请赐教。

「我就把我的太太和我的进账一股脑送给他，瞧他喜不喜欢他把我陷进去的这摊烂事！」

克雷格先生说这番话时火气十足，神色却丝毫不显凶悍；这时，他那张素来不太有书卷气的脸上，渐渐摆出一副若有所思的表情。

===Original===
“He’s an awful fool, yon man!” he observed.

“Which man?” I inquired.

“Billy,” said he, and with a gasp I recognised my Emperor in this brief epithet. “It’s just astounding to me how he never learns that hot coals will burn his fingers, and water won’t run uphill! He’s always trying the silliest things.”

His eyes suddenly began to twinkle again, and he asked abruptly﻿—

===Chinese===
「那家伙是个天大的傻瓜！」他评点道。

「哪位？」我问。

「比利呀。」他说。我倒抽一口气，从这个简称里认出了我的皇帝。「最叫我惊愕的是，他竟从不知吸取教训：火炭会烫了手指头，水不会往坡上流！他老在试那些顶顶愚蠢的事。」

他的眼睛忽然又眨闪起来，冷不丁地问——

===Original===
“Why’s the Kaiser like my boots?”

I gave it up at once.

“Because he’ll be sold again soon!” he chuckled. “That’s one of my latest, Mr. Wilson. I’ve little to do in these weary times but make riddles to amuse my girls and think of dodges for getting a rise out of my wife. I had her beautifully the other day! We’ve two sons at the front, you must know, and one of them’s called Bob. Well, I got a letter from him, and suddenly I looked awful grave and cried, ‘My God, Bob’s been blown up’﻿—you should have seen Mrs. Craigie jump﻿—‘by his Colonel!’ said I, and I tell you she was nearly as put about to find I’d been pulling her leg as if he’d really been blown to smithereens. Women are funny things.”

I fear I scarcely laughed as much as he expected at this extraordinary instance of woman’s obtuseness, but he did not seem to mind. He was already filling another pipe, and having found an audience, was evidently settling down to an afternoon’s conversation﻿—or rather an afternoon’s monologue, for it was quite clear he was independent of any assistance from me. I was resolved, however, not to forgo this chance of learning something more about Eileen.

===Chinese===
「德皇像我那双靴子，为什么？」

我立刻认输。

「因为他不久就要再被卖一回！」他咯咯笑道，「这是我的近作之一，威尔逊先生。这愁苦的年月里我没什么可做，只好编谜语逗我那几个闺女，再想些点子撩我太太动气。前几天我把她耍了个漂亮！不瞒您说，我们有两个儿子在前线，其中一个叫鲍勃。喏，我收到他一封信，忽然把脸沉得吓人，喊道：『我的天，鲍勃被炸上天了！』——您真该看看克雷格太太怎么蹦起来——『是被他们上校骂上天了！』我说。我告诉您，等她发觉我在逗她，那份气急败坏，几乎不亚于鲍勃真被炸成了碎片。女人真是些妙东西。」

只怕面对这桩女人迟钝的奇闻，我的笑声远不及他期望的那般热烈，但他似乎并不介意。他已在装第二斗烟，既找到了听众，显然打算安顿下来谈上一个下午——或者不如说，独白一个下午，因为很清楚，他并不需要我帮衬半分。但我打定主意，不肯放过这个多探些艾琳底细的机会。

===Original===
“You were talking about Miss Holland,” I said hurriedly, before he had time to get under way again.

“Oh, so I was. And that reminds me I’ve come here just to make some inquiries about the girl.”

Again his blue eyes twinkled furiously.

“Why’s Miss Holland like our hall clock?” he inquired. “I may mention by the way that it’s always going slow.”

===Chinese===
「您方才说到霍兰德小姐。」我趁他还没重新开讲，赶紧插话。

「哦，是啊。这一提倒想起来了，我到这儿来，正是要打听打听这姑娘的底细。」

他的蓝眼睛又使劲眨闪起来。

「霍兰德小姐怎么像我们家门厅那座钟？」他问道，「顺便提一句，那钟老是走得慢。」

===Original===
Again I gave it up.

“Because you take her hand and get forward! That was one for my wife’s benefit. It made her fairly sick!”

“Do you mean,” I demanded, “that you were actually in the habit of holding Miss﻿—er﻿—Holland’s hand?”

“Oh, no fears. I’m past that game. But Mrs. Craigie is a great one for p’s and q’s and not being what she calls vulgar, and a joke like that is a sure draw. I get her every time with my governess riddles. Here’s a good one now﻿—Why’s a pretty governess like a﻿—”

===Chinese===
我又认了输。

「因为你一牵住她的手，就抢到前头去了！那一条是专门说给我太太受用的，把她气得够呛！」

「您是说，」我质问道，「您当真有握霍兰——呃——霍兰德小姐手的习惯？」

「哦，那倒不怕。那套把戏我早玩不动了。只是克雷格太太顶讲究言谈举止、最忌她所谓的『俗气』，这样的玩笑百发百中。拿家庭教师编的谜语，我回回都能逗着她。这会儿正好有一条——漂亮的的家庭女教师像什么——」

===Original===
In spite of the need for caution, my impatience was fast overcoming me.

“Then you have been sent by Mrs. Craigie to make inquiries about Miss Holland?” I interrupted a trifle brusquely.

Mr. Craigie seemed at least to have the merit of not taking offence readily.

“That’s the idea,” he agreed. “You see, it’s this way: my wife’s been at me ever since our governess bolted, as she calls it. Well now, what’s the good in making inquiries about a thing that’s happened and finished and come to an end? If it was a case of engaging another governess, that’s a different story. I’d take care not to have any German spies next time!”

===Chinese===
尽管形势需要谨慎，我的不耐却快要压倒一切了。

「那么，是克雷格太太派您来打听霍兰德小姐的？」我略嫌唐突地打断他。

克雷格先生至少有这么个好处：不轻易见怪。

「正是这个来意。」他表示同意，「您瞧，是这么回事：打从我们那位家庭教师——照她的说法——逃之夭夭起，我太太就没饶过我。可是您说，一件事已经发生、已经了结、已经到了头，再去打听它还有什么用处？若是要另聘一位家庭教师，那另当别论。下一回我可得留神，再不雇德国间谍了！」

===Original===
“German spies!” I exclaimed, with I hope well-simulated horror; “you don’t mean to suspect Miss Holland of that surely!”

“Oh, ‘German Spy’ is just a kind of term nowadays for anyone you don’t know all about,” said Mr. Craigie easily. “Everyone you haven’t seen before is a German Spy. I spotted five myself in my own parish at the beginning of the war, and Mrs. Craigie wrote straight off to the Naval Authorities and reported them all.”

“And were they actually spies?” I asked a trifle uncomfortably.

“Not one of them!” laughed he. “The nearest approach was a tinker who’d had German measles! Ha, ha! It’s no good my wife reporting any more spies, and I just reminded her of that whenever she worried me, and pulled her leg a bit about me and Miss Holland being in the game together, and so it was all right till she got wind of a girl who was the image of the disappearing governess being here at the manse as Mr. Burnett’s sister, and then there was simply no quieting her till I’d taken the car and run over to see what there was in the story. Mind you, I didn’t think there was a word of truth in it myself; but when I’d got here, by Jingo, there I saw Miss Holland’s tweed coat in the hall! Now that’s a funny kettle of fish, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
「德国间谍！」我喊道，带着但愿装得很像的惊骇，「您总不是当真怀疑霍兰德小姐是那号人吧！」

「哦，『德国间谍』如今不过是个名目，泛指你不摸底细的人。」克雷格先生不紧不慢地说，「凡是没见过的人，个个都是德国间谍。开战那阵子，我在自己教区里就认出了五个，克雷格太太当即写信给海军当局，把他们全举报了。」

「那他们当真是间谍？」我略觉不安地问。

「一个都不是！」他笑道，「顶沾边的是个补锅匠，害过德国麻疹！哈哈，哈哈！我太太再举报间谍也没用了，每逢她拿这事烦我，我便这样提醒她，还逗她说我和霍兰德小姐是一伙的，如此相安无事——直到她听说有个姑娘，同那位人间蒸发的家庭教师长得一个模子，如今以伯内特先生妹妹的身份住在牧师寓所里。这下可怎么劝也劝不住了，非得我开了汽车跑一趟，看看这传闻里到底有什么名堂。您听着，我自己原以为这事一个字都不足信；可等我到了这儿，好家伙，门厅里挂着的可不就是霍兰德小姐那件粗花呢外套！这可真是桩新鲜事，您说是不是？」

===Original===
I didn’t say so, but I had to admit that he was not so very far wrong. The audacity of the performance was quite worthy of Tiel, but its utter recklessness seemed not in the least like him. Had the vanishing governess’s employer been anyone less easygoing than Mr. Craigie, how readily our whole scheme might have been wrecked! Even as it was, I saw detection staring me straight in the face. However, I put on as cool and composed a face as I could.

“I understood that Miss Holland’s brother had written to you about it,” I said brazenly.

“Oh! he is really her brother, is he?” said he, looking at me very knowingly.

“Certainly.”

===Chinese===
这话我没说出口，可我不得不承认，他离真相并不太远。这步棋的大胆泼辣十足是蒂尔的手笔，可那份毫无顾忌却半点也不像他。倘若那位失踪的家庭教师的东家不是克雷格先生这样随和的人，我们的全盘计划何等容易便告倾覆！即便如此，我也眼见败露的威胁直瞪着我的脸。然而，我还是尽量摆出一副又冷静又镇定的面孔。

「我还以为霍兰德小姐的兄弟已为此给您写过信。」我厚着脸皮说。

「哦！他当真是她兄弟，是吗？」他说，一副心照不宣的神情望着我。

「当然。」

===Original===
“He being Burnett and she Holland, eh?”

“You have heard of half-brothers, haven’t you?” I inquired with a condescending smile.

“Oh, I have heard of them,” winked Mr. Craigie as good-humouredly as ever; “only I never happened to have heard before of half-sisters running away from a situation they’d taken without a word of warning, just whenever their half-brothers whistled.”

“Did Mr. Burnett whistle?” I inquired, with (I hope) an air of calm and slightly superior amusement.

===Chinese===
「他姓伯内特，她姓霍兰德，嗯？」

「『半个兄弟』这种东西，您总听说过吧？」我带着居高临下的微笑问道。

「哦，听说过，听说过。」克雷格先生一如既往地和气，眨了眨眼，「只是我还从没听说过，有哪位『半个妹妹』会应聘了差事，单等她那『半个兄弟』一声唿哨，连个招呼都不打就拔腿跑掉的。」

「伯内特先生打唿哨了吗？」我问道，带着（但愿是）一种平静而略占上风的怡然。

===Original===
“Someone sent her a wire, and I presume it was Mr. Burnett,” said he. “By Jingo!”

He stopped suddenly with an air as nearly approaching excitement as was conceivable in such a gentleman.

“What’s the matter?” I asked a trifle anxiously.

“One might get a good one about how to make a governess explode, the answer being ‘Burn it!’ By Jove, I must think that out.”

===Chinese===
「有人给她发了封电报，我料定就是伯内特先生。」他说，「好家伙！」

他猛地顿住，那副神情，已算得上这样一位绅士所能有的极限的激动了。

「怎么了？」我略带焦灼地问。

「没准儿能编出一条好谜语——怎么叫一位家庭女教师炸了锅？谜底是『一把火烧（Burn it）』！天哪，这个我可得好好想想。」

===Original===
Before I could recover from my amazement at this extraordinary attitude, he had suddenly resumed his shrewd quizzical look.

“Are you an old friend of Mr. Burnett?” he inquired.

“Oh, not very,” I said carelessly.

“Then perhaps you’ll not be offended by my saying that he seems a rum kind of bird,” he said confidentially.

===Chinese===
我还没从这副非同寻常的态度里回过神来，他脸上已突然恢复了那种精明促狭的神气。

「您是伯内特先生的老友？」他问道。

「哦，算不上。」我漫不经心地说。

「那么我说他仿佛是只怪鸟，您大概不会见怪吧。」他推心置腹地说。

===Original===
“In what way?”

“Well, coming up here just for a Sunday to preach a sermon, and then not preaching it, but staying on as if he’d taken a lease of the manse﻿—him and his twelve-twenty-fourths of a sister!”

“But,” I stammered, before I could think what I was saying, “I thought he did preach last Sunday!”

“Not him! Oh, people are talking a lot about it.”

===Chinese===
「怎么个怪法？」

「喏，大老远跑上来只为一个礼拜天讲一篇道，结果道也不讲了，倒赖着不走，仿佛把牧师寓所租下了一份——他，还有他那二十四分之十二的妹妹！」

「可是，」我不假思索便冲口结巴起来，「我以为他上个礼拜天确实讲了道！」

「他没讲！哦，人们正议论纷纷呢。」

===Original===
This revelation left me absolutely speechless. Tiel had told me distinctly and deliberately that he had gone through the farce of preaching last Sunday﻿—and now I learned that this was a lie. What was worse, he had assured me that he was causing no comment, and I now was told that people were “talking.” Coming straight on top of my discovery of his reckless conduct of Eileen’s affair, what was I to think of him?

It was at this black moment that Tiel and Eileen entered the room. My heart stood still for an instant at the thought that, in their first surprise, something might be disclosed or some slip made by one of us. But the next instant I saw that they had learned who was here and were perfectly prepared.

“How do you do, Mr. Craigie!” cried Eileen radiantly.

Mr. Craigie seemed distinctly taken aback by the absence of all signs of guilt or confusion.

===Chinese===
这一揭底让我彻底哑口无言。蒂尔曾清清楚楚、郑重其事地告诉我，上个礼拜天他把讲道那场戏做足了全套——如今我才知道这是个谎言。更糟的是，他曾向我担保自己没引起任何议论，而我现在听到的却是人们正在「议论」。这桩新发现，恰恰压在我发觉他操办艾琳之事何等鲁莽轻率的上头，我该把他往哪处想？

恰恰在这个漆黑的关口，蒂尔和艾琳进了屋。一想到他们乍见之下的错愕里，我们中间可能有人露了口风、失了脚，我的心顿时停跳了一拍。但下一刻我便看出，他们已知道来客是谁，并且有了万全的准备。

「您好吗，克雷格先生！」艾琳光彩照人地喊道。

人家毫无愧怍慌乱之色，克雷格先生显然被弄了个措手不及。

===Original===
“I’m keeping as well as I can, thank you, considering my anxiety,” said he.

“About my sister, sir?” inquired Tiel with his most brazen effrontery, coming forward and smiling cordially. “Surely you got my letter?”

I started. The man clearly had been at the keyhole during the latter part of our conversation, or he could hardly have made this remark fit so well into what I had said.

“I’m afraid I didn’t.”

===Chinese===
「托赖还好，谢谢，只是心里悬着事。」他说。

「为我妹妹的事么，先生？」蒂尔趋前相问，笑得热诚，脸皮厚到了家，「您总该收到我的信了吧？」

我一惊。此人分明在后半段一直贴着锁眼偷听，否则他这句话断难同我方才的话严丝合缝到这个地步。

「怕是没收到。」

===Original===
“Tut, tut!” said Tiel, with a marvellously well-assumed air of annoyance. “The local posts seem to have become utterly disorganised. Apparently they pay no attention to civilian letters at all.”

“You’re right there,” replied Mr. Craigie with feeling. “The only use we are for is just to be taxed.”

“What must you think of us?” cried Eileen, whose acting was fully the equal of Tiel’s. “However, my brother will explain everything now.”

“Yes,” said Tiel; “if Mr. Craigie happens to be going﻿—and I’m afraid we’ve kept him very late already﻿—I’ll tell him all about it as we walk back to his car.”

===Chinese===
「啧啧！」蒂尔说，恼意装得精妙绝伦，「本地邮政看来是彻底乱套了。老百姓的信，他们压根儿顾不上递。」

「这话说着了。」克雷格先生深有同感地答道，「我们这些人剩下的用处，就是纳税。」

「您心里不定怎么想我们呢！」艾琳喊道——她的演技同蒂尔不差分毫——「好在我哥哥这就把一切讲清楚。」

「正是，」蒂尔说，「克雷格先生若是这就动身——只怕我们已经留他留得太晚——我便陪他走回车上，一路把原委讲给他听。」

===Original===
He gave Mr. Craigie a confidential glance as though to indicate that he had something private for his ear. Our visitor, on his part, was obviously reluctant to leave an audience of three, especially as it included his admired governess; but Tiel handled the situation with quite extraordinary urbanity and skill. He managed to open the door and all but pushed Mr. Craigie out of the room, without a hint of inhospitality, and solely as though he were seeking only his convenience. I could scarcely believe that this was the man who had made at least two fatal mistakes﻿—mistakes, at all events, which had an ominously fatal appearance.

When Mr. Craigie had wished us both a very friendly goodbye and the door had closed behind him, I turned instantly to Eileen and cried, perhaps more hotly than politely﻿—

“Well, I have been nicely deceived!”

===Chinese===
他意味深长地瞟了克雷格先生一眼，仿佛表示有体己话要单独奉告。我们的来客呢，显然舍不得撇下三位听众——其中还有他那位敬慕的家庭教师；但蒂尔应付这场面，那一副温文尔雅、八面玲珑的手段着实罕见。他居然做到了开门相送、半推半送地把克雷格先生请出屋去，非但看不出半点怠慢，倒好像处处只求客人方便。我几乎不能相信，这人便是铸下至少两桩致命过失的人——无论如何，是两桩望之凶多吉少的过失。

克雷格先生向我们两个道了极友好的别，门在他身后合上，我立刻转身冲着艾琳，喊出的话或许失之激烈、有欠客气——

「好啊，我可叫人蒙得漂亮！」

===Original===
“By whom?” she asked quietly.

“By you a little and by Tiel very much!”

“How have I deceived you?”

I looked at her a trifle foolishly. After all, I ought to have realised that she must have had some curious adventure in getting into the islands. She had never told me she hadn’t, and now I had merely found out what it was.

===Chinese===
「蒙你的是谁？」她平静地问。

「你蒙了一点，蒂尔蒙了个十足！」

「我几时蒙过你？」

我有点傻气地望着她。说到底，我早该想到，她进这几座岛时必是有一番奇遇的。她从未对我否认过，如今我不过是把那奇遇查了个明白。

===Original===
“You never told me about your governess adventure﻿—or Mr. Craigie﻿—or that you were called Holland,” I said rather lamely.

She merely laughed.

“You never asked me about my adventures, or I should have. They were not very discreditable after all.”

“Well, anyhow,” I said, “Tiel has deceived me grossly, and I am going to wring an explanation out of him!”

===Chinese===
「你从没跟我讲过你当家庭教师的那段奇遇——也没讲过克雷格先生——也没讲过你姓霍兰德。」我说得相当没力气。

她只是笑。

「你不曾问过我的奇遇，你问了我就讲了。说到底，那些事也不怎么丢人。」

「好吧，反正，」我说，「蒂尔把我蒙得够狠，我要拧出他一个解释来！」

===Original===
She laid her hand beseechingly on my arm.

“Don’t quarrel with him!” she said earnestly. “It will do no good. We may think what we like of some of the things he does, but we have got to trust him!”

“Trust him! But how can I? He told me he preached last Sunday﻿—I find it was a lie. He said nobody in the parish suspected anything﻿—in consequence of his not preaching, I find they are all ‘talking.’ He mismanaged your coming here so badly that if old Craigie weren’t next door to an imbecile we should all have been arrested days ago. How can I trust him now?”

“Say nothing to him now,” she said in a low voice. “Wait till tomorrow! I think he will tell you then very frankly.”

===Chinese===
她把手恳求似的搭在我臂上。

「别同他吵！」她恳切地说，「吵没有半点好处。他做的某些事，我们尽可以随意去想，但我们不能不信任他！」

「信任他！可我怎么信？他告诉我上个礼拜天他讲了道——我发现那是个谎。他说教区里无人起疑——由于他没讲道，我发现他们全在『议论』。他把你的到来办得那样糟，老克雷格若不是同白痴只隔一道门，我们几个早就被抓走了。事到如今，你叫我怎么信他？」

「现在什么也别对他说。」她低声道，「等到明天！我想他到时会对你非常坦白的。」

===Original===
There was something so significant and yet beseeching in her voice that I consented, though not very graciously.

“I can hardly picture Herr Tiel being very ‘frank’!” I replied. “But if you ask me﻿—”

I bowed my obedience, and then catching up her hand pressed it to my lips, saying﻿—

“I trust you absolutely!”

===Chinese===
她的声音里有种那样意味深长、又那样恳求的意味，我便应允了，尽管应允得不太情愿。

「我实在想象不出蒂尔先生会非常『坦白』！」我答道，「不过既是你开口——」

我躬身表示遵命，随即执起她的手，印在唇上，说道——

「我绝对信任你！」

===Original===
When I looked up I caught a look in her eye that I could make nothing of at all. It was beyond question very kind, yet there seemed to be something sorrowful too. It made her look so ravishing that I think I would have taken her in my arms there and then, had not Tiel returned at that moment.

“Well,” asked Eileen, “what did you tell Mr. Craigie?”

“I said that you were secretly married to Mr. Wilson, whose parents would cut him off without a penny if they suspected the entanglement, and this was the only plan by which you could spend a few days together. Of course I swore him to secrecy.”

For a moment I hesitated whether to resent this liberty, or to feel a little pleased, or to be amused. Eileen laughed gaily, and so I laughed too. And that was the end (so far) of my afternoon adventure.

===Chinese===
我抬起头来，撞见她眼中一种我全然读不懂的神情。那分明极为温柔，却又仿佛含着什么哀伤。那神情使她那样动人，我想，若不是蒂尔恰在那一刻回来，我多半当场便已把她拥入怀中了。

「怎么样，」艾琳问，「你同克雷格先生说了些什么？」

「我说，你同威尔逊先生私下成了婚，他父母一旦疑心这段孽缘，便要一个铜板不留地断绝他；这便是你们能相聚几日的唯一办法。当然，我让他发誓守口如瓶。」

一时间我拿不定主意：是该恼他这般放肆，还是该觉得有点受用，还是该一笑置之。艾琳快活地笑了，我便也笑了。我这场下午的奇遇，至此（暂时）收场。

## III Thursday Night / 三 星期四之夜

===Original===
I went up to my room early in the evening. Eileen had been very silent, and about nine o’clock she bade us good night and left us. To sit alone with Tiel, feeling as I did and yet bound by a promise not to upbraid him, was intolerable, and so I left the parlour a few minutes after she did. As I went down the passage to the back, my way lit only by the candle I was carrying, I was struck with a sound I had heard in that house before, only never so loudly. It was the droning of the wind through the crevices of some door, and the whining melancholy note in the stillness of that house of divided plotters and confidences withheld, did nothing to raise my spirits.

When I reached my room I realised what had caused the droning. The wind had changed to a new quarter, and as another consequence my chimney was smoking badly and the room was filled with a pungent blue cloud. It is curious how events arise as consequences of trifling and utterly different circumstances. I tried opening my door and then my window, but still the fire smoked and the cloud refused to disperse. Then I had an inspiration. I have mentioned a large cupboard. It was so large as almost to be a minute room, and I remembered that it had a skylight in its sloping roof. I opened this, and as the room at once began to clear, I left it open.

And then I paced the floor and smoked and thought. What was to be made of these very disquieting events? Clearly Tiel was either a much less capable and clever man than he was reputed﻿—a bit of a fraud in fact﻿—or else he was carrying his fondness for mystery and for suddenly springing brilliant surprises, like conjuring tricks, upon people, to the most extreme lengths. If he were really carrying out a cunning deliberate policy in not preaching last Sunday, good and well, but it was intolerable that he should have deceived me about it. It seemed quite a feasible theory to suppose that he had got out of conducting the service on some excuse in order that he might be asked to stay longer and preach next Sunday instead. But then he had deliberately told me he had preached, and that the people had been so pleased that they had invited him to preach again. It sounded like a schoolboy’s boastfulness!

===Chinese===
傍晚我早早上了楼。艾琳一晚话很少，九点钟光景便向我们道了晚安离开了。要同蒂尔单独对坐——怀着我的心绪，却又受着一言不责他的承诺——实在难以忍受，于是她走后几分钟我也离开了客厅。我朝后面的通道走去，手里一支蜡烛是唯一的光，忽然一种声音撞进耳朵——那声音我在这宅子里听见过，只是从没有这样响。那是风从某扇门的缝隙里挤出的呜咽；在这座谋划者各怀心事、互相提防的宅子的静默里，那哀哀的鸣响丝毫提不起我的精神。

回到房里我才明白呜声的来由。风换了方向，另一个后果便是我的烟囱倒烟倒得厉害，满屋腾着一团呛人的蓝雾。事情竟会由这样微不足道、又全不相干的因由生出来，真是奇妙。我试着开了门，又开了窗，炉火照旧冒烟，烟雾也赖着不散。这时我灵机一动。我提过一只大橱。它大得几乎够得上一间小屋，我记得它那斜顶上开着一扇天窗。我把天窗打开，屋里顿时开始透亮，我便由它敞着。

然后我便在地板上踱步、抽烟、盘算。这几桩令人极不安生的事，该作何解？显然，蒂尔要么远不像传闻中那样能干聪明——实际上是个十足的骗子——要么便是把他那套神秘癖发挥到了极致：动不动就变戏法似的、朝人冷不丁抖出一个漂亮的意外。倘若他上个礼拜天不讲道当真是在行一条深思熟虑的狡计，那也罢了，可他竟连我也一并蒙骗，这就难以容忍了。倒有一个颇为说得通的猜想：他找了借口推掉礼拜，好教人家留他多住、改由下个礼拜天讲道。可他偏偏又处心积虑地告诉我他讲了，还说会众如何满意，已请他再讲一回。这听上去活像个学童的吹牛！

===Original===
Of course if he were the sort of man who would (like myself) have drawn the line at conducting a bogus religious service, I could quite well understand his getting out of it somehow. But when I remembered his tale of the murder of the real Mr. Burnett, I dismissed that hypothesis. Besides, why deceive me in any case? I daresay I should have felt a little anxious as to the result if he had evaded the duty he had professed to come up and perform, but would he care twopence about that? I did not believe it.

And then his method of getting Eileen into the islands, though ingenious enough (if not very original), had been marred by the most inconceivable recklessness. Surely some better scheme could have been devised for getting her out of the Craigies’ house than a sudden flight without a word of explanation﻿—and a flight, moreover, to another house in the same island where gossip would certainly spread in the course of a very few days. Of course Mr. Craigie’s extraordinary character gave the scheme a chance it never deserved, but was Tiel really so diabolically clever that he actually counted on that? How could he have known so much of Craigie’s character? Indeed, that explanation was inconceivable.

And then again, why had Eileen consented to such a wild plan? That neither of them should have realised its drawbacks seemed quite extraordinary. There must be some deep cunning about it that escaped me altogether. If it were not so, we were lost indeed! And so I resolved to believe that there was more wisdom in the scheme than I realised, and simply leave it at that.

===Chinese===
当然，倘若他是那种（像我一样）断不肯主持一场假宗教礼拜的人，他设法躲掉，我全然可以理解。可一想起他讲的谋害真伯内特先生的那段事，这假设便被我丢开了。再说，无论如何又何必骗我？他若躲掉了他自称北上来履行的那项职分，我对结果多少会有些悬心，可这一点他在乎吗？两个铜板也不会在乎。我不信。

还有他送艾琳进岛的法子，虽说够巧（只是不见得多新鲜），却坏在最不可思议的鲁莽上。要送她出克雷格家的门，总归设计得出比这不辞而别、拔腿就跑更好的方案吧——何况这一跑，跑的还是同一座岛上的另一户人家，不出几日，闲话保准传开。当然，克雷格先生那出了奇的好性子，给了这方案一个它本不配有的机会，可蒂尔当真狡猾到算准了这一层？他怎么可能把克雷格的性子摸得这样透？这解释委实不可信。

再者，艾琳又为什么应允了这样一桩胡来的计划？他们两个竟没有一个看清其中的破绽，这未免太反常。这背后必有某种我全然参不透的深谋。若非如此，我们可就真的完了！于是我索性打定主意，相信这计划里自有我没看出的道理，且由它去。

===Original===
Thereupon I sat down and wrote for an hour or two to keep me from thinking further on the subject, and at last about midnight I resolved to go to bed. The want of fresh air had been troubling me greatly, and it struck me that a safe way of getting a little would be to put my head through the open skylight for a few minutes. It was quite dark in the cupboard, so that no light could escape; and I brought a chair along, stood on it, and looked out, with my head projecting from the midst of the sloping slates, and a beautiful cool breeze refreshing my face.

So cool was the wind that there was evidently north in it, and this was confirmed by the sky, which literally blazed with stars. I could see dimly but pretty distinctly the outbuildings at the back of the house, and the road that led to the highway, and the dark rim of hills beyond. Suddenly I heard the back door gently open, and still as I had stood on my chair before, I became like a statue now. In a moment the figure of Tiel appeared, and from a flash of light I saw that he carried his electric torch. He walked slowly towards the highroad till he came to a low wall that divided the fields at the side, and then from behind the wall up jumped the form of a man, illuminated for an instant by a flash from the torch, and then just distinguishable in the gloom.

I held my breath and waited for the crack of a pistol-shot, gently withdrawing my head a little, and prepared to rush down and take part in the fray. But there was not a sound save a low murmur of voices, far too distant and too hushed for me to catch a syllable of what they were saying. And then after two or three minutes I saw Tiel turn and start to stroll back again. But at that moment my observations ceased, for I stepped hastily down from my chair and stood breathlessly waiting for him to run up to my room.

===Chinese===
于是我便坐下写了一两个钟头，免得自己再顺着这事想下去，末了约摸午夜时分，我决意就寝。屋里缺新鲜空气已让我难受了好一阵，我想到一个稳妥办法，能换上一口：把头从敞着的天窗探出去几分钟。橱里一片漆黑，灯光丝毫泄不出去；我搬来一把椅子，站上去朝外望，脑袋从斜斜的石板瓦中间探出，一股清凉美妙的风拂在脸上。

风这样凉，里头分明带着北意，满天简直烧着星斗，更证实了这一点。屋后的杂用房舍、通往大路的道儿、更远处黑黢黢的山影，我都看得到，虽暗，却相当清楚。忽然，我听见后门轻轻开了；先前我还像站在椅子上，此刻更成了一尊雕像。转瞬之间蒂尔的身影出现，一道闪光掠过，我看清他带着手电筒。他慢慢朝大路走去，走到路旁那道分隔田地的矮墙边，墙后随即跳起一个人影，被手电一闪照亮一瞬，又在幽暗里勉强可辨。

我屏住呼吸，等一声枪响，一面把头悄悄缩回一点，准备冲下楼去加入战团。可除了低低的语声，再无半点动静——那语声又远又轻，他们说什么，我一个音节也捉不住。两三分钟后，我看见蒂尔转身，信步往回走了。可就在那一刻，我的观察中断了，因为我急忙跳下椅子，屏息站着，只等他奔上我房里来。

===Original===
He was quiet almost as a mouse. I had not heard him pass through the house as he went out, and I barely heard a sound now as he returned. But I heard enough to know that he had gone off to bed, and did not propose to pay me a visit.

“What in Heaven’s name did it mean?” I asked myself.

A dozen wild and alarming theories flashed through my mind, and then at last I saw a ray of comfort. Perhaps this was only a rendezvous with Ashington, or some subordinate in his pay. It was not a very brilliant ray, for the more I thought over it, the more unlikely it seemed that a rendezvous should take place at that spot and in that inconvenient fashion, when there was nothing to prevent Ashington or his emissary from entering the house by the front door and holding their conversation in the parlour. However, it seemed absolutely the only solution, short of supposing that the house was watched, and so I accepted it for what it was worth in the meantime, and turned into bed.

My sleep was very broken, and in the early morning I felt so wide awake, and my thoughts were so restlessly busy, that I jumped up and resolved to have another peep out of the skylight. Very quietly I climbed on the chair and put my head through again. There was the man, pacing slowly away from me, from the wall towards the highroad! I studied his back closely, and of two things I felt certain: he was not a sailor of any sort﻿—officer or bluejacket﻿—and yet he walked like a drilled man. A tall, square-shouldered fellow, in dark plain clothes, who walks with a short step and a stiff back﻿—what does that suggest? A policeman of some sort﻿—constable or detective, no doubt about that!

===Chinese===
他安静得几乎像只老鼠。出去时我没听见他穿过宅子，回来时我也仅仅勉强听见一点声息。但那点声息已足够让我知道：他径自睡了，并不打算来看我。

「这到底是玩的什么名堂？」我问自己。

十几个狂乱骇人的猜想从脑子里闪过，末了我终于看见一线安慰。这或许不过是同阿兴顿、或他手下某个拿钱的人碰头。这线光并不怎么亮，因为我越想越觉得不像：碰头何必挑那样一个地点、那样一种别扭方式，明明没有任何东西拦着阿兴顿或他的信使从前门进屋、到客厅里谈话。可无论如何，除了设想这宅子受人监视之外，这似乎是唯一的解释，我便姑且信下它值的那点钱，上床睡了。

这一觉支离破碎，清晨时分我毫无睡意，思绪翻腾不休，索性跳起来，决意再从天窗张一眼。我悄悄爬上椅子，又把头探了出去。那人果然在：正缓缓背着我、从矮墙往大路那边踱去！我细细端详他的背影，有两点我可以断定：他绝不是水手——军官不是，水兵也不是——可他走路的样子分明受过操练。一个高个子、方肩膀的家伙，穿深色便衣，迈着短步、挺着僵直的背——这提示着什么？某个当警察的——巡警也好、侦探也好，错不了！

===Original===
At the road he turned, evidently to stroll back again, and down went my head, I did not venture to look out again, nor was there any need. I dressed quickly, and this time put on my uniform. This precaution seemed urgently﻿—and ominously﻿—called for! And then I slipped downstairs, went to the front hall, and up the other stairs, and quietly called “Tiel!” For I confess I was not disposed to sit for two or three hours waiting for information.

At my second cry he appeared at his bedroom door, prompt as usual.

“What’s the matter?” he asked.

“Who did you speak to last night?” I asked point-blank.

===Chinese===
他走到大路便折返，显然又要踱回来，我的头赶紧缩了下去。我没敢再往外看，也无须再看了。我迅速穿好衣裳，这一回穿上了军装。这道防范显得刻不容缓——而且兆头不祥！随后我溜下楼，穿过前厅，上了另一道楼梯，轻轻喊了一声：「蒂尔！」因为我承认，我不打算干坐两三个钟头等消息。
