我第二声喊出，他已出现在卧室门口，一如既往地迅捷。

「什么事？」他问。

「昨夜你同谁说话了？」我直截了当地问。

===Original===
He looked at me for an instant and then smiled.

“Good heavens, it wasn’t you, was it?” he inquired.

“Me!” I exclaimed.

“I wondered how you knew otherwise.”

===Chinese===
他看了我一瞬，随即笑了。

「天哪，在窗后张望的不是你吧？」他问道。

「我！」我叫道。

「不然我正纳闷，你是怎么知道的。」

===Original===
I told him briefly.

“And now tell me exactly what happened!” I demanded.

“Certainly,” said he quietly. “I went out, as I often do last thing at night, to see that the coast is clear, and this time I found it wasn’t. A man jumped up from behind the wall just as you saw.”

“Who was he?”

===Chinese===
我把经过简短讲了。

「现在把原原本本讲给我听！」我要求道。

「当然。」他平静地说，「我像往常一样，睡前最后出去查一趟，看看周围干净不干净，这一回却发现不干净。墙后跳起一个人，同你看见的一样。」

「他是谁？」

===Original===
“I can only suspect. I saw him for an instant by the light of my torch, and then it seemed less suspicious to put it out.”

“I don’t see that,” I said.

“I am a cautious man,” smiled Tiel, as easily as though the incident had not been of life and death importance.

“And what did he say to you?” I demanded impatiently.

===Chinese===
「我只能猜。手电光下我看清他一瞬，随后觉得，把灯熄了倒显得没那么可疑。」

「这我倒不懂了。」我说。

「我是个谨慎的人。」蒂尔微笑道，轻描淡写，仿佛这桩事本无关生死。

「那他同你说了什么？」我不耐烦地追问。

===Original===
“I spoke to him and asked him what he was doing there.”

“What did he say to that?”

“I gave him no chance to answer﻿—because, if the answer was what I feared, he wouldn’t make it. I simply told him he would catch cold if he sat there on the grass, and gave him some details about my own misfortune in getting rheumatism through sleeping in damp sheets.”

“I see,” I said; “you simply tried to bluff him by behaving like an ordinary simple-minded honest clergyman?”

===Chinese===
「是我先开的口，问他在那儿做什么。」

「他怎么答？」

「我没给他答话的机会——因为，倘若答案正是我怕的那一种，他也不会说。我只对他说，坐在那草地上要着凉的，又给他讲了几段我的不幸：怎样因睡在潮湿的被褥里落下了风湿。」

「我明白了，」我说，「你就是装成一个普普通通、心思简单、老实巴交的牧师，把他蒙混过去？」

===Original===
Tiel nodded.

“It was the only thing to do﻿—unless I had shot him there and then. And there might have been more men for all I knew.”

“Well,” I said, “I can tell you something more about that man. He is patrolling the road at the back at this very moment.”

Tiel looked grave enough now.

===Chinese===
蒂尔点了点头。

「那是唯一的办法——除非当场把他崩了。再说我又怎么知道，没准儿还有更多人呢。」

「喏，」我说，「关于那个人，我还能告诉你点新的。此时此刻，他正在后头那条路上来回巡逻呢。」

这回蒂尔的脸色够凝重了。

===Original===
“It looks as if the house were being watched,” he said rather slowly.

“Looks? It *is* being watched!”

He thought for a moment.

===Chinese===
「看样子，这宅子是被人盯上了。」他说得相当慢。

「看样子？它*就是*被人盯着！」

他想了想。

===Original===
“Evidently they only suspect so far. They can know nothing, or they wouldn’t be content with merely watching. Thank you for telling me. We’ll talk about it later.”

Still cool as a cucumber he reentered his room, and I returned to my own.

What can be done? Nothing! I can only sit and wait and keep myself from worrying by writing. I have made up my fire and my door is locked, so that this manuscript will be in flames before anyone can enter, if it comes to the worst. Recalling the words of Tiel a few days ago, I shiver a little to think of what is ahead. Suspicion has *begun*!

===Chinese===
「显然他们眼下只是起疑。他们什么也不知道，否则不会只满足于盯梢。多谢你告诉我。这事我们回头再谈。」

他依旧镇定自若地回了房，我也回到自己屋里。

还能做什么？什么也做不了！我只能坐着等，靠写东西岔开心焦。炉火已封好，门已锁上，万一到了最坏那一步，任何人进门之前，这份手稿便已化为灰烬。想起蒂尔几天前说的话，想到前头的光景，我不由得微微发抖。疑心*已经开始*了！

## IV Friday / 四 星期五

===Original===
This is written under very different circumstances﻿—and in a different place. My last words were written with my eyes shut; these are written with them open, but I shall simply tell what happened as calmly as I can. Let the events speak; I shall make no comment in the meanwhile.

On that Friday morning our breakfast was converted into a council of war. We all three discussed the situation gravely and frankly. I felt tempted to say some very bitter words to Tiel, for it seemed to me quite obvious that it was simply his gross mismanagement which had brought us to the edge of this precipice; but I am glad now I refrained. I was at no pains, however, to be over-polite.

“There is nothing to be done in the meanwhile, I’m afraid,” said he.

This coolness seemed to me all very well in its proper season, but not at present.

===Chinese===
这一篇写于截然不同的境况之下——也在一个不同的地方。我最后那几行字是闭着眼睛写下的；这几行睁着眼写，但我只把经过尽量平静地记下。让事实自己说话；此刻我不置一词。

那个星期五的早晨，我们的早饭变成了一场军事会议。我们三个把局势严肃而坦率地讨论了一番。我很想说几句狠话刺蒂尔，因为在我看来再明白不过：把我们逼到这道悬崖边上的，正是他那场一塌糊涂的失算；但如今我庆幸自己忍住了。不过，我也没费什么力气去讲究客套。

「眼下只怕无事可做。」他说。

这份冷静，在我看来合平时自有它的好处，只是不合当下。

===Original===
“Yes, there is,” I said urgently. “We might get out of this house and look for some other refuge!”

He shook his head.

“Not by daylight, if it is being watched.”

“Besides,” said Eileen, “this is the day we have been waiting for. We don’t want to be far away, do we?”

===Chinese===
「有的。」我急切地说，「我们可以离开这宅子，另找个藏身之处！」

他摇了摇头。

「若是受人监视，白天走不得。」

「再说，」艾琳说，「这正是我们等候的日子。我们不想离得远远的，对吧？」

===Original===
“Personally,” I said, “it seems to me that as I cannot be where I ought to be” (and here I looked at Tiel somewhat bitterly), “with my brave comrades in their attack on our enemies, I should much prefer to make for a safer place than this﻿—if one can be found.”

“It can’t,” said Tiel briefly.

And that indeed became more and more obvious the longer we talked it over. Had our house stood in the midst of a wood, or had a kindly fog blown out of the North Sea, we might have made a move. As it was, I had to agree that it would be sheer folly, before nightfall anyhow; and there was nothing for it but waiting.

To add to the painfulness of this ordeal, I found myself obliged to remain in my room, now that I had resumed my uniform. This time it did not need Tiel to bid me take this precaution. In fact, I was amazed to hear him suggesting that I would be just as safe in the parlour. At the time I naturally failed altogether to understand this departure from his usual caution, and I asked him sarcastically if he wished to precipitate a catastrophe.

===Chinese===
「就我私心而论，」我说，「既然我不能待在我该待的地方」（说到这里，我颇为怨愤地瞪了蒂尔一眼），「同我那些勇敢的战友一起向敌人出击，那么我宁可去寻一处比这更稳妥的地方——倘若找得到的话。」

「找不到。」蒂尔言简意赅。

而且我们越商量，这一点果然越明白。倘若我们的宅子坐落在密林中央，或是北海发善心吹来一场合宜的浓雾，我们或许还能挪动。眼下这样，我只得承认那是十足的蠢行——无论如何天黑之前是如此；除等待外别无他法。

更给这场煎熬添堵的是：既然重新穿上了军装，我便只得守在自己房里。这一回，用不着蒂尔来嘱咐我防范。事实上，听他建议说我待在客厅也一样安全时，我惊诧得很。当时我自然全然不懂，这一反他素常谨慎的做法用意何在，便讥诮地问他，是不是巴望加快一场大祸的到来。

===Original===
“We have still a good deal to discuss,” said he.

“I thought there was nothing more to be said.”

“I mean in connection with the other scheme.”

“The devil may take the other scheme!” said I, “anyhow till we escape from this trap. What is the good in planning ahead, with the house watched night and day?”

===Chinese===
「我们还有不少要商量的。」他说。

「我以为已没有什么可说的了。」

「我是说那另一项计划。」

「让那另一项计划见鬼去吧！」我说，「反正得先逃出这个陷阱。宅子昼夜有人盯着，抢先谋划有什么用？」

===Original===
“We only suspect it is watched,” said he calmly.

“Suspect!” I cried. “We are not idiots, and why should we pretend to be?”

And so I went up to my room and spent the most miserable and restless day of my life. How slowly the hours passed, no words of mine can give the faintest idea. In my present state of mind writing was impossible, and I tried to distract myself by reading novels; but they were English novels, and every word in them seemed to gall me. I implored Eileen to come and keep me company. She came up once for a little, but the devil seemed to have possessed her, for I felt no sympathy coming from her at all; and when at last I tried to be a little affectionate she first repulsed me, saying it was no time for that, and then she left me. With baffled love added to acute anxiety, you can picture my condition!

===Chinese===
「我们只是疑心它被监视。」他心平气和地说。

「疑心！」我喊道，「我们不是白痴，何必装白痴？」

于是我便上楼回房，度过了平生最悲惨、最坐立不安的一天。时光流逝得多慢，我的笔墨连个零头也形容不出。以我那时的心境，写作全无可能，我便读小说排遣；可那些是英国小说，字字句句都在硌我。我哀求艾琳来陪我。她上来待了一小会儿，却像着了魔，我丝毫感觉不到她的怜惜；末了我想稍稍亲热一下，她先把我推开，说眼下不是时候，随即撇下我走了。热恋受挫，又添焦灼，我的光景可想而知！

===Original===
For the first part of that horrible day I kept listening for some sign of the police, and now and then looking out from the skylight at the back, but the watcher was no longer visible, and not a fresh step or voice was to be heard in the house. My door stood locked, my fire was blazing, and my papers lay ready to be consumed, and at moments I positively longed to see them blazing and myself arrested, and get it over, yet nothing happened.

In the afternoon the direction of my thoughts began to change as the hour approached when the fleet should sail and my country reap the reward of the enterprise and fidelity which I felt conscious I had shown, and the sacrifice which I feared I should have to make. I began to make brief visits to the parlour to look out of the window and see if I could see any signs of movement in the Armada. And then for the second time I saw Tiel in a genial cheerful humour, and this time there was no doubt of the cause. He too was in a state of tension, and his mind, like mine, was running on the coming drama. In fact, as the afternoon wore on, his thoughts were so entirely wrapped up in this that he frankly talked of nothing else. Was I sure we should have at least four submarines? he asked me; and would they be brought well in and take the risk? Indeed, I never heard him ask so many questions, or appear so pleased as he did when I reassured him on all these points.

As for Eileen, she was quite as excited as either of us, and when Tiel was not asking me questions, she was; until once again prudence drove me back to my room. On one of my visits she gave us some tea, but that is the only meal I remember any of us eating between our early and hurried lunch and the evening when the crash came.

===Chinese===
那可怕的一天的上半晌，我一直竖着耳朵捕捉警察的动静，不时从后面的天窗张一眼，可盯梢的人已不见踪影，宅子里也听不到一点新的脚步或人声。我的门锁着，炉火烧着，文件摊在手边随时待焚。有几次我当真巴望看见它们烧起来、我自己被捕，好歹一了百了，然而什么也没有发生。

到了下午，我的思路开始转变：舰队起航的时辰近了，我的国家即将收获我自觉已贡献出的那份勤勉与忠悃，还有我担心自己将不得不作的牺牲。我开始一趟趟短去客厅，凭窗眺望，看那支大舰队可有开动的迹象。于是我第二次见到蒂尔兴致盎然、满面春风，而这一回，缘由已毫无疑义。他也处在紧绷之中，他的心，同我的一样，都在那出即将上演的大戏上。事实上，随着下午渐渐过去，他的心思整个缠绕在这上头，坦白得很，别的一概不谈了。我们保准至少有四艘潜艇吗？他问我。它们会不会大胆开进近处、冒这个险？说真的，我从没听他问过这么多问题，也没见他像这样——当我在这些问题上一一宽他的心时——那样喜形于色。

至于艾琳，她同我们两个一样兴奋；蒂尔不问我时，她便问，直到谨慎又一次把我赶回房里。在我其中一次下楼时，她给我们备了点茶，可在那顿赶早的午饭同大祸临头的那个傍晚之间，我们三个还吃过什么，我记得起来的也仅此一顿了。

===Original===
The one thing I looked for as I gazed out of that window was the rising of smoke from the battle-fleet, and at last I saw it. Stream after stream, black or grey, gradually mounted, first from one leviathan and then from another, till the air was darkened hundreds of feet above them, and if our flotilla were in such a position that they could look for this sign, they must have seen it. This time I returned to my room with a heart a little lightened.

“I have done my duty,” I said to myself, “come what may of it!”

And I do not think that any impartial reader will deny that, so far as my own share of this enterprise was concerned, I had done my very utmost to make it succeed.

===Chinese===
凭窗眺望时，我一心期待的只有一样东西——战舰队升起的烟——末了我终于看见了。一股又一股，黑的或灰的，渐渐腾起，先是一头巨兽，又是一头，直到它们上空几百尺的空气都为之昏暗；倘若我们的小舰队正处在望得见这信号的位置，它们必然已经望见了。这一回我回房时，心头轻了些许。

「我尽了职责，」我对自己说，「管它结果如何！」

而且我不认为任何一位不偏不倚的读者能够否认：就此番行动我个人承担的那一份而言，我已尽了最大努力求其成功。

===Original===
The next time I came down my spirits rose higher still, and for the moment I quite forgot the danger in which I stood. The light cruisers, the advance-guard of the fleet, were beginning to move! This time when I went back to my room I forced myself to read two whole chapters of a futile novel before I again took off the lid and peeped in to see how the stew was cooking. The instant I had finished the second chapter I leapt up and opened the door﻿—and then I stood stock-still and listened. A distant sound of voices reached me, and a laugh rang out that was certainly neither Tiel’s nor Eileen’s.

I locked my door, slipped back again, and prepared to burn my papers; but though I stood over the fire for minute after minute, there was no sound of approaching steps. Very quietly I opened the door and listened once more, and still I heard voices. And thus I lingered and hesitated for more than an hour. By this time the attack had probably been made, and I could stand the suspense no longer, so I went recklessly downstairs, strode along the passage, and opened the parlour door.

Nothing will ever efface the memory of the scene that met my eyes. Tiel, Eileen, and Ashington sat there, the two men each with a whisky-and-soda, and all three seemingly in the most extraordinarily high spirits. It was Ashington’s face and voice that suddenly rent the veil from before my eyes. Instead of the morose and surly individual I had met before, he sat there the incarnation of the jovial sailor. He was raising his glass to his lips, and as I entered I heard the words﻿—

===Chinese===
再下一次下楼，我的兴致愈发高了，一时竟全然忘了自身处境的危险。轻巡洋舰——舰队的先头卫队——开始移动了！这一回回房后，我强迫自己读完了一本无聊小说整整两章，才又掀开锅盖，张一眼锅里这炖菜熬得如何了。第二章一读完，我霍地起身开门——随即僵立在那里细听。一阵隐隐的人声传来，一串笑声响起，那笑声分明既非蒂尔的，也非艾琳的。

我锁上门，又缩了回去，准备焚毁文件；可我在炉火边站了一分钟又一分钟，始终没有脚步声逼近。我轻轻推开门再听，人声依旧。就这样，我磨蹭迟疑了一个多钟头。到这时，进攻多半已经发动，那悬心我再也承受不住，便不管不顾地下楼，大步穿过通道，推开了客厅的门。

没有任何东西能磨掉我眼前那一幕的记忆。蒂尔、艾琳和阿兴顿坐在那里，两个男人人手一杯威士忌苏打，三个人的兴头都高得异乎寻常。是阿兴顿的脸和嗓音，骤然撕去了蒙在我眼前的帷幕。坐在那里的不是我先前来见的那个阴郁乖戾的家伙，而是快活水手的化身。他正举杯到唇边，我进门时听见这样一句——

===Original===
“Here’s to you again, Robin!”

What had happened I did not clearly grasp in that first instant, but I *felt* I was betrayed. My hand went straight to my revolver pocket, but before I could seize it, Tiel, who sat nearest, leapt up, grasped my wrist, and with the shock of his charge drove me down into a chair. It was done so suddenly that I could not possibly have resisted. Then with a movement like a conjurer he picked the revolver out of my pocket, and said in his infernally cool calm way﻿—

“Please consider yourself a prisoner of war, Mr. Belke.”

Even then I had not grasped the whole truth.

===Chinese===
「再敬你一杯，罗宾！」

头一瞬我并没弄清发生了什么，但我*感到*自己被出卖了。我的手直奔左轮枪的口袋，但还没抓住，坐得最近的蒂尔已一跃而起，攥住我的手腕，借着冲撞的力道把我撞进一张椅子。这一切来得那样突然，我根本无从抵抗。随后，他像魔术师一样一个手法，把左轮枪从我口袋里摄了出去，用他那地狱般冷静的口吻说——

「请把自己当作战俘吧，贝尔克先生。」

即便那时，我也还没明白全部真相。

===Original===
“A prisoner of war!” I exclaimed. “And what the devil are you, Herr Tiel? A traitor?”

“You have got my name a little wrong,” said he, with that icy smile of his. “I am Commander Blacklock of the British Navy, so you can surrender either to me or to Captain Phipps, whichever you choose.”

“Phipps!” I gasped, for I remembered that as the name of a member of Jellicoe’s staff.

“That’s me, old man,” said the gross person with insufferable familiarity. “The Honourable Thomas Bainbridge Ashington would have a fit if he looked in the glass and saw this mug!”

===Chinese===
「战俘！」我喊道，「那你自己是什么鬼东西，蒂尔先生？叛徒么？」

「我的名字你稍稍记错了。」他说，脸上挂着那种冰冷的微笑，「我是英国海军的布莱克洛克中校，所以，向我投降也好，向皮普斯上校投降也好，随你挑。」

「皮普斯！」我倒抽一口气，因为我记得，那是杰利科参谋部里一个成员的名字。

「正是在下，老兄。」那肥硕的家伙说，熟不拘礼得让人受不了，「尊贵的托马斯·班布里奇·阿兴顿若是照照镜子，瞧见这副尊容，非得当场犯病不可！」

===Original===
“Then I understand I am betrayed?” I asked as calmly as I could.

“You’re nabbed,” said Captain Phipps, with brutal British slang, “and let me tell you that’s better than being dead, which you would have been if you’d rejoined your boat.”

I could not quite control my feelings.

===Chinese===
「那么我明白，我被出卖了？」我尽量平静地问。

「你是被逮住了。」皮普斯上校说，用的是英国粗话，「而且我告诉你，这总比死了强——你要是回到了你那条艇上，早就是死人了。」

我的情绪一时按捺不住。

===Original===
“What has happened?” I cried.

“We’ve bagged the whole four﻿—just at the very spot on the chart which you and I arranged!” chuckled the great brute.

(At this point Lieutenant von Belke’s comments become a little too acid for publication, and it has been considered advisable that the narrative should be finished by the Editor.)

===Chinese===
「到底出了什么事？」我叫道。

「四艘全叫我们兜住了——就在你我商量好的海图上那个点位！」那大块头咯咯直笑。

（到了这里，冯·贝尔克中尉的评述火气稍重，不宜刊布，故而本书认为，由编者来续完这段自述方为妥当。）

# Part V — A Few Concluding Chapters by the Editor / 第五部分 — 编者的收束数章

## I Tiel’s Journey / 一 蒂尔的旅程

===Original===
For the moment the fortitude of the hapless young lieutenant completely broke down when he heard these tidings. It took him a minute to control his voice, and then he said﻿—

“Please give me back my revolver. I give you my word of honour not to use it on any of you three.”

Commander Blacklock shook his head.

“I am sorry we can’t oblige you,” said he.

===Chinese===
听到这消息的一刻，这位倒霉的年轻中尉的坚忍彻底垮了。他过了一分钟才把嗓音稳住，然后说道——

「请把左轮枪还给我。我以名誉向你们担保，绝不把它用在你们三位身上。」

布莱克洛克中校摇了摇头。

「很抱歉，这个忙我们帮不上。」他说。

===Original===
“Poor old chap,” said Phipps with genial sympathy; “it’s rotten bad luck on you, I must admit.”

These well-meant words seemed only to incense the captive.

“I do not wish your damned sympathy!” he cried.

“Hush, hush! Ladies present,” said Phipps soothingly.

===Chinese===
「可怜的老伙计。」皮普斯满含宽厚的同情说，「你的运气实在坏透了，这我承认。」

这些好话似乎只把俘虏激怒了。

「我才不要你们那该死的怜悯！」他喊道。

「嘘，嘘！有女士在呢。」皮普斯劝慰道。

===Original===
Von Belke turned a lowering eye on Miss Holland. She had said not a word, and scarcely moved since he came into the room, but her breathing was a little quicker than usual, and her gaze had followed intently each speaker in turn.

“Ach so!” he said; “the decoy is still present. I had forgot.”

Blacklock’s eye blazed dangerously.

“Mr. Belke,” he said, “Captain Phipps and I have pleaded very strongly that, in spite of your exceedingly ambiguous position, and the fact that you have not always been wearing uniform, you should not suffer the fate of a spy. But if you make any more remarks like your last, I warn you we shall withdraw this plea.”

===Chinese===
冯·贝尔克阴沉沉地转过眼去看霍兰德小姐。自他进屋以来她一言未发，几乎一动不动，只是呼吸比平常略急，目光依次紧紧追着每一个说话的人。

「*Ach so*（原来如此）！」他说，「诱饵还在场呢。我倒忘了。」

布莱克洛克的眼里腾起危险的火光。

「贝尔克先生，」他说，「尽管您的处境暧昧之极，而且您并非始终穿着军装，皮普斯上校和我仍竭力为您陈情，主张您不该受间谍的处置。但您若再说方才那样的言辞，我警告您，我们便要收回这项陈情。」

===Original===
For the first time Eileen spoke.

“Please do not think it matters to me, Captain Blacklock﻿—” she began.

In a whisper Phipps interrupted her.

“Eyewash!” he said. “It’s the only way to treat a Hun﻿—show him the stick!”

===Chinese===
艾琳第一次开口了。

「请您别以为我在乎这个，布莱克洛克上校——」她开口道。

皮普斯耳语着打断了她。

「鬼话！」他说，「对付德国佬只有这一个法子——让他看看棍子！」

===Original===
The hint had certainly produced its effect. Von Belke shrugged his shoulders, and merely remarked﻿—

“I am your prisoner. I say nothing more.”

“That’s distinctly wiser,” said Captain Phipps, with a formidable scowl at the captive and a wink at Miss Holland.

For a few moments von Belke kept his word, and sat doggedly silent. Then suddenly he exclaimed﻿—

===Chinese===
这暗示显然起了作用。冯·贝尔克耸了耸肩，只说了一句——

「我是你们的俘虏。旁的我不说了。」

「这就高明多了。」皮普斯上校说，朝俘虏狠狠一瞪，又朝霍兰德小姐挤了挤眼。

有那么几分钟，冯·贝尔克说到做到，倔强地坐着不吭声。忽然他嚷起来——

===Original===
“But I do not understand all this! How should a German agent be a British officer? My Government knew all about Tiel﻿—I was told to be under his orders﻿—it is impossible you can be he!”

Blacklock turned to the other two.

“I almost think I owe Mr. Belke an explanation,” he said with a smile.

“Yes,” cried Eileen eagerly, “do tell him, and then﻿—then he will understand a little better.”

===Chinese===
「可这一切我不明白！一个德国特务，怎么会是英国军官？我国政府对蒂尔的底细一清二楚——我奉的就是他的调遣——你们不可能是他！」

布莱克洛克转向另外两位。

「我几乎觉得，我欠贝尔克先生一个解释。」他微笑着说。

「是啊，」艾琳急切地喊道，「快讲给他听吧，那样——那样他就能多明白一点了。」

===Original===
Blacklock filled a pipe and leaned his back against the fireplace, a curious mixture of clergyman in his attire and keen professional sailor in his voice and bearing, now that all need for pretence was gone.

“The story I told you of the impersonation and attempted murder of Mr. Alexander Burnett,” he began, “was simply a repetition of the tale told me by Adolph Tiel at Inverness﻿—where, by the way, he was arrested.”

Von Belke started violently.

“So!” he cried. “Then﻿—then you never were Tiel?”

===Chinese===
布莱克洛克装上一斗烟，背倚壁炉站定。如今伪装已全无必要，他这个人便成了一副奇特的混合体：一身装束是牧师，嗓音做派却是精悍的职业水手。

「我先前讲给你们听的，有人冒名顶替、谋害亚历山大·伯内特先生未遂的那段故事，」他开口道，「不过是阿道夫·蒂尔在因弗内斯讲给我听的翻版——顺便说一句，他就是在那儿被捕的。」

冯·贝尔克猛地一震。

「原来如此！」他喊道，「那么——那么你从来就不是蒂尔？」

===Original===
“I am thankful to say I never was, for a more complete scoundrel never existed. He and his friend Schumann actually did knock Mr. Burnett on the head, tie a stone to his feet, and pitch him over the cliff. Unfortunately for them, they made a bad job of the knot and the stone came loose. In consequence, Mr. Burnett floated long enough to be picked up by a patrol boat, which had seen the whole performance outlined against the sky at the top of the cliff above her. By the time they had brought him back to a certain base, Mr. Burnett had revived and was able to tell of his adventure. The affair being in my line, was put into my hands, and it didn’t take long to see what the rascals’ game was.”

“No,” commented Phipps; “I suppose you spotted that pretty quick.”

“Practically at once. A clergyman on his way here﻿—clothes and passport stolen﻿—left for murdered﻿—chauffeur so like him that the minister noticed the resemblance himself in the instant the man was knocking him down﻿—what was the inference? Pretty obvious, you’ll agree. Well, the first step was simple. The pair had separated; but we got Tiel at Inverness on his way North, and Schumann within twenty-four hours afterwards at Liverpool.”

“Good business!” said Phipps. “I hadn’t heard about Schumann before.”

===Chinese===
「谢天谢地，我从来就不是，因为天下再没有比他更彻底的恶棍了。他和他那位朋友舒曼，确实敲了伯内特先生的头，在他脚上拴了石头，把他抛下了悬崖。不幸的是，他们那个结没打结实，石头松了。于是伯内特先生在海面上漂了许久，够一条巡逻艇把他捞起——那条艇恰好眼见这整幕戏以艇上方崖顶的天空为背景，演了个轮廓分明。等他们把他带回某处基地，伯内特先生已还过魂来，能讲述自己的历险了。这案子归我这一行管，便交到了我手里，没过多久，那两个流氓玩的什么把戏，已看得一清二楚。」

「可不是，」皮普斯评点道，「我料你很快就看穿了。」

「差不多一眼便知。一位牧师正在来此地的路上——衣裳和护照被窃——被当作已死抛下——那司机又同他像到这个地步：那人把他打倒的一瞬，牧师自己便认出了这相像——这推论是什么？您得承认，相当明显。于是，第一步很简单。那两个已经分头走散，但我们在北上途中的因弗内斯逮住了蒂尔，不出二十四小时，又在利物浦逮住了舒曼。」

「干得漂亮！」皮普斯说，「舒曼的事我先前还没听说过。」

===Original===
“Well,” continued Blacklock, “I interviewed Mr. Tiel, and I found I’d struck just about the worst thing in the way of rascals it has ever been my luck to run up against. He began to bargain at once. If his life was spared he would give me certain very valuable information.”

“*Mein Gott!*” cried Belke. “Did a German actually say that?”

“Tiel belongs to no country,” said Blacklock. “He is a cosmopolitan adventurer without patriotism or morals. I told him his skin would be safe if his information really proved valuable; and when I heard his story, I may say that he did save his skin. He gave the whole show away, down to the passwords that were to pass between you when you met.”

He suddenly turned to Phipps and smiled.

===Chinese===
「于是，」布莱克洛克接着说，「我提审了蒂尔先生，发现我这运气，算是撞上了此生所遇流氓行当里最恶劣的一件货色。他当下便讨价还价起来。若饶他一命，他便交给我一些极有价值的情报。」

「*Mein Gott*（我的上帝）！」贝尔克喊道，「一个德国人当真说得出这种话？」

「蒂尔不属于任何国家。」布莱克洛克说，「他是个世界主义的冒险家，没有爱国心，也没有道德。我告诉他，倘若他的情报果真值钱，他这张皮便保得住；等听过他的交代，我可以说，他确实保住了这张皮。他把全盘都兜了出来，一直兜到你们会面时对暗号用的口令。」

他忽然转向皮普斯，笑了。

===Original===
“It’s curious how the idea came to me. I’ve done a good bit of secret service work myself, and felt in such a funk sometimes that I’ve realised the temptation to give the show away if I were nailed. Well, as I looked at Tiel, I said to myself, ‘There, but for the grace of God, stands Robin Blacklock!’ And then suddenly it flashed into my mind that we were really not at all unlike one another﻿—same height, and tin-opener nose, and a few streaks of anno domini in our hair, and so on.”

“I know, old thing,” said his friend, “it’s the wife-poisoning type. You see ’em by the dozen in the Chamber of Horrors.”

Their Teutonic captive seemed to wax a little impatient.

“What happened then?” he demanded.

===Chinese===
「说来奇怪，这主意怎么钻进我脑子里的。我自己也干过不少特工差事，有时吓得魂不附体，因而很明白：人一旦被拿住，把全盘兜出去的诱惑有多大。喏，我打量着蒂尔，心里对自己说：『若非上帝恩典，站在那儿的便是罗宾·布莱克洛克！』接着我脑中忽地一闪：我们两个实在长得不算不像——个头一样，都是开罐器式的鼻子，头发里都掺了几绺岁月的灰白，如此等等。」

「我知道，老伙计，」他的朋友说，「这是毒杀老婆的那一号长相。到恐怖陈列室里瞧瞧，这种人一抓一把。」

他们这位条顿俘虏似乎有点不耐烦起来。

「后来怎样？」他追问。

===Original===
“What happened was that I decided to continue Mr. Tiel’s journey for him. The arrest and so on had lost a day, but I knew that the night of your arrival was left open, and I had to risk it. That splash of salt water on your motor bike, and your resource in dodging pursuit, just saved the situation, and we arrived at the house on the same night.”

“So that was why you were late!” exclaimed von Belke. “Fool that I was not to have questioned and suspected!”

“It might have been rather a nasty bunker,” admitted Blacklock, “but luckily I got you to lose your temper with me when I reached that delicate part of my story, and you forgot to ask me.”

“You always were a tactful fellow, Robin,” murmured Phipps.

===Chinese===
「后来的事便是：我决定替蒂尔先生把行程走下去。逮捕等等耽搁了一天的工夫，但我知道，你到达的那一夜是留着没安排的，我只得赌这一把。你摩托车上那摊咸海水，还有你甩掉追踪的急智，恰好救了场，我们便在同一个夜里到达了那所宅子。」

「原来这就是你迟到的缘故！」冯·贝尔克喊道，「我这个傻瓜，竟没有追问、没有起疑！」

「那一下本可能弄得相当难堪。」布莱克洛克承认道，「所幸我讲到故事里那个微妙的节骨眼上，惹得你对我动了火，你便忘了问。」

「你从来都是个会做人的，罗宾。」皮普斯喃喃道。

===Original===
“Of course,” resumed Blacklock, “I was in touch with certain people who advised me what scheme to recommend. My only suggestion was that the officer sent to advise us professionally should be one whose appearance might lead those who did not know him to suspect him capable of treasonable inclinations. My old friend, Captain Phipps﻿—”

“Robin!” roared his old friend, “I read your bloomin’ message. You asked for the best-looking officer on the staff, and the one with the nicest manners. Get on with your story!”

These interludes seemed to perplex their captive considerably.

“You got a pretended traitor? I see,” he said gravely.

===Chinese===
「当然，」布莱克洛克续道，「我同几位人士保持着联络，该举荐什么方案，是他们教的。我唯一的提议是：派来担任专业顾问的那位军官，其相貌最好能让不认识他的人疑心他有叛国的倾向。我的老友皮普斯上校——」

「罗宾！」他的老友咆哮道，「你那封混账电报我看了。你要的是参谋部里相貌最漂亮的军官，外加举止最文雅的那位。接着讲你的故事！」

这些插科打诨似乎把他们这位俘虏搅得相当糊涂。

「你们找来一个装叛徒的？我明白了。」他正色道。

===Original===
“Exactly. I tried you first with Ashington of the *Haileybury*﻿—whom I slandered grossly by the way. If you had happened to know him by sight I should have passed on to another captain, till I got one you didn’t know. Well, I needn’t recall what happened at our council of war, but now we come to rather a﻿—” he hesitated and glanced for an instant at Miss Holland﻿—“well, rather a delicate point in the story. I think it’s only fair to those concerned to tell you pretty fully what happened. I believe I am right in thinking that they would like me to do so.”

Again he glanced at the girl, and this time she gave a little assenting nod.

“That night, after you left us, Mr. Belke, Captain Phipps and I had a long discussion over a very knotty point. How were we to get you back again here after you had delivered your message to your submarine?”

“I do not see exactly why you wished me to return?” said von Belke.

===Chinese===
「正是。我头一炮先拿*黑利伯里*号的阿兴顿试你——顺带说一句，我把他说得坏透了，实在冤枉。倘若你碰巧见过他、认得他的脸，我便换下一位上校，直到换到你认不得的为止。好在我们的军事会议上经过如何，不必细表；可眼下要讲到的，是一个相当——」他顿了顿，朝霍兰德小姐瞥了一眼——「嗯，故事里一个相当微妙的关节。我认为，把经过相当完满地讲给你听，对相关的人才是公道。我相信我这样想没有想错：他们正愿意我这样做。」

他又望了那姑娘一眼，这一回，她轻轻点头表示同意。

「那天夜里，你走了之后，贝尔克先生，皮普斯上校和我为一个非常棘手的问题商议了很久：你向你的潜艇交了信之后，怎样再把你引回这里？」

「我不太明白，你们为什么希望我回来？」冯·贝尔克说。

===Original===
“There were at least three vital reasons, In the first place someone you spoke to might have known too much about Tiel and have spotted the fraud. Then again, someone might easily have known the real Captain Ashington, and it would be a little difficult to describe Captain Phipps in such a way as to confound him with anyone else. Finally, we wished to extract a little more information from you.”

Von Belke leapt from his seat with an exclamation.

“What have I not told you!” he cried hoarsely. “*Mein Gott*, I had forgotten that! Give me that pistol! Come, give it to me! Why keep me alive?”

“I suppose because it is an English custom,” replied Commander Blacklock quietly. “Also, you will be exceedingly glad some day to find yourself still alive. Please sit down and listen. I am anxious to explain this point fully, for a very good reason.”

===Chinese===
「起码有三条要紧的缘由。第一，同你说过话的人里，保不齐有谁对蒂尔知之甚详，看穿这场骗局。第二，也保不齐有谁认得真正的阿兴顿上校，而要把皮普斯上校描述得同任何别人混而不分，又着实有点难度。最后，我们还指望从你嘴里再多掏一点情报。」

冯·贝尔克一声喊，从座位上跳了起来。

「我还有什么没告诉你们的！」他声音嘶哑地喊道，「*Mein Gott*，这我倒忘了！把那把枪给我！来呀，给我！干吗还留我活口？」

「大概因为这是英国人的规矩。」布莱克洛克中校平静地答道，「再者，将来有一天，你会发现自己居然还活着，会庆幸之极的。请坐下听我说。我急于把这一点讲透，缘由很充分。」

===Original===
With a groan their captive sat down, but with his head held now between his hands and his eyes cast upon the floor.

“We agreed that at all costs this must be managed, and so I tried my hand at exercising my authority over you. I saw that was going to be no good, and gave it up at once for fear you’d smell a rat. And then I thought of Miss Holland.”

Von Belke looked up suddenly.

“Ah!” he cried, “so that is why this lady appeared﻿—this lady I may not call a decoy!”

“That is why,” said Blacklock.

===Chinese===
俘虏一声呻吟坐下了，只是双手抱头，双眼盯着地板。

「我们商定，此事无论如何要办成，我便试着对你摆了一回权威。我看那不管用，又怕你嗅出耗子味儿，当即收了手。然后，我便想到了霍兰德小姐。」

冯·贝尔克猛地抬起头。

「啊！」他喊道，「原来这就是那位女士出现的缘故——这位我不许称之为诱饵的女士！」

「正是这个缘故。」布莱克洛克说。

## II The Lady / 二 那位女士

===Original===
Lieutenant von Belke looked for a moment at the lady who had enslaved him, but for some reason he averted his gaze rather quickly. Then with an elaborate affectation of sarcastic politeness which served but ill to conceal the pain at his heart and the shock to his pride, he inquired﻿—

“May I be permitted to ask what agency supplies ladies so accomplished at a notice so brief?”

“Providence,” said Blacklock promptly and simply. “Miss Holland had never undertaken any such work before, and her name is on the books of no bureau.”

“I believe you entirely,” said von Belke ironically. “You taught her her trade then, I presume?”

===Chinese===
冯·贝尔克中尉朝那位把他迷住了的女士望了片刻，却不知为何，相当快地移开了目光。随后，他摆出一副精心做作的讥诮客气——这客气遮掩不住他心头的痛楚和自尊所受的震击——开口问道——

「可否容我问一句，是哪一家机关，能在这样短的知会里，供应出这般多才多艺的女士？」

「天意。」布莱克洛克干脆而简洁地答道，「霍兰德小姐从前从未做过这类工作，任何中介的名册上也找不见她的名字。」

「我完全相信。」冯·贝尔克讥讽地说，「那么，她这一行的本事是您调教出来的，我猜？」

===Original===
“I did.”

The German stared at him.

“Is there really any need to deceive me further?” he inquired.

“I am telling you the simple truth,” said Blacklock unruffled. “I had the great good fortune to make Miss Holland’s acquaintance on the mail-boat crossing to these islands. She was going to visit Mr. Craigie﻿—that intellectual gentleman you met yesterday﻿—under the precise circumstances he described. I noticed Miss Holland the moment she came aboard the boat.” He paused for a moment, and then turned to Eileen with a smile. “I have a confession to make to you, Miss Holland, which I may as well get off my chest now. My mind, naturally enough perhaps, was rather running on spies, and when I discovered that you were travelling with a suitcase of German manufacture I had a few minutes’ grave suspicion. I now apologise.”

===Chinese===
「正是。」

德国人直盯着他。

「真还有再骗我一层的必要么？」他问道。

「我讲的是大实话。」布莱克洛克说，不动声色，「我运气极好，在开往这几座岛的邮船上结识了霍兰德小姐。她正是去拜访克雷格先生——就是您昨天见到的那位睿智绅士——情形同他描述的分毫不差。她一上船我便注意到她了。」他顿了片刻，随即含笑转向艾琳。「有件事我得向您坦白，霍兰德小姐，不妨趁现在卸下这块心事。我的心思，或许很自然，正满是对间谍的念头；待我发现您随身带着一只德国制造的衣箱时，有那么几分钟，我疑心得不轻。现在我一并道歉。」

===Original===
Eileen laughed.

“Only a few minutes!” she exclaimed. “It seems to me I got off very easily!”

“That was why I was somewhat persistent in my conversation,” he continued, still smiling a little, “but it quickly served the purpose of satisfying me absolutely that my guns were on the wrong target. And so I promptly relieved you of my conversation.”

He turned again to von Belke.

===Chinese===
艾琳笑了。

「才有几分钟！」她喊道，「照我看，我算是全身而退了！」

「这也正是我在攀谈上稍显纠缠的缘故。」他接着说，脸上仍带着点笑意，「不过这番攀谈很快便彻底达到了目的：它使我确信，我的炮口瞄错了目标。于是我当即把我的谈话从您那儿撤了下来。」

他又转向冯·贝尔克。

===Original===
“Then, Mr. Belke, a very curious thing happened, which one of us may perhaps be pardoned for thinking diabolical and the other providential. Miss Holland happened to have met the real Mr. Burnett and bowled me out. And then I had another lucky inspiration. If Miss Holland will pardon me for saying so in her presence, I had already been struck with the fact that she was a young lady of very exceptional looks and brains and character﻿—and, moreover, she knew Germany and she knew German. It occurred to me that in dealing with a young and probably not unimpressionable man such an ally might conceivably come in useful.”

“Robin,” interrupted his old friend, with his rich laugh, “you are the coldest-blooded brute I ever met!”

“To plot against a man like that!” agreed von Belke with bitter emphasis.

“Oh, I wasn’t thinking of you,” said Captain Phipps, with a gallant glance at the lady. “However, on you go with your yarn.”

===Chinese===
「接着，贝尔克先生，便发生了一件非常蹊跷的事：这件事，我们中的一个把它想作魔鬼的安排、另一个把它想作上天的安排，恐怕都情有可原。霍兰德小姐偏偏见过真正的伯内特先生，把我拆穿了。跟着我又灵光一闪。倘若霍兰德小姐容我当着她的面这样说：我早已留意到，她是一位容貌、头脑、品性都极为出众的年轻女士——何况她了解德国，也通德语。我转念想到，对付一个年轻、而且多半并非无动于衷的男人，这样一位盟友说不定派得上用场。」

「罗宾，」他的老友插进来，朗声笑道，「你真是我平生所见心肠最冷的畜生！」

「竟这样暗算一个人！」冯·贝尔克深以为然，语气苦涩。

「哦，我说的可不是你。」皮普斯上校说，朝那位女士献了个殷勤的眼风，「不过，你接着讲你的故事吧。」

===Original===
“Well, I decided on the spot to take Miss Holland into my confidence﻿—and I should like to say that confidence was never better justified. She seemed inclined to do what she could for her country.” Commander Blacklock paused for an instant, and added apologetically, “I am putting it very mildly and very badly, but you know what I mean. She was, in fact, ready to do anything I asked her on receipt of a summons from me. I had thought of her even when talking to Captain Phipps, but I felt a little reluctant to involve her in the business, with all it entailed, unless no other course remained open. And no other course was open. And so I first telegraphed to her and then went over and fetched her. That was how she came to play the part she did, entirely at my request and instigation.”

“You﻿—you then told her to﻿—to make me admire her?” asked von Belke in an unsteady voice.

“Frankly I did. Of course it was not for me to teach a lady how to be attractive, but I may say that we rehearsed several of the scenes very carefully indeed﻿—I mean in connection with such matters as the things you should say to Commander Wiedermann, and so on. Miss Holland placed herself under my orders, and I simply told her what to say. She was in no sense to blame.”

===Chinese===
「于是，我当场决定把内情相托于霍兰德小姐——而且我愿意说，一份信任从没有换来过更好的回报。她似乎有意为她的国家尽一份力。」布莱克洛克中校顿了一顿，又带着歉意补充道，「我说得太轻太拙劣了，可你们明白我的意思。事实上，只消我一纸相召，我请她做的任何事她都已准备去做。同皮普斯上校商议时我便已想到她，只是多少不忍把她牵进这件事里——连同它所牵带的一切——除非别无他路可走。而确实别无他路。于是我先是发了一封电报，随后又亲自过去接她。她之所以扮下那个角色，从头到尾，全出于我的请求和主使。」

「你——你于是吩咐她——吩咐她引我倾慕她？」冯·贝尔克问，声音发颤。

「坦白说，是。教授一位女士如何施展魅力，自然轮不到我，但我可以说，有几幕戏我们确实排演得非常仔细——我指的是诸如你该对维德曼中校说些什么之类的关节。霍兰德小姐把自己置于我的号令之下，说什么，全由我口授。她无论怎样都无可指责。」

===Original===
“Blame!” cried Captain Phipps. “She deserves all the decorations going!”

“I was trying to look at it from Mr. Belke’s point of view,” said Blacklock, “as I think Miss Holland probably desires.”

She gave him a quick, grateful look, and he continued﻿—

===Chinese===
「指责！」皮普斯上校喊道，「现有的勋章她全够领！」

「我只是试着从贝尔克先生的角度看这件事。」布莱克洛克说，「我想霍兰德小姐多半也愿意如此。」

她飞快地、感激地望了他一眼，他接着说——

===Original===
“It was I who suggested that she should appear critical of me, and endeavour, as it were, to divide our household into two camps, so that you should feel you were acting against me when you were actually doing what I wished. I tell you this frankly so that you may see who was responsible for the deceit that we were forced to practise.”

“Forced!” cried the young lieutenant bitterly. “Who forced you to use a woman? Could you not have deceived me alone?”

“No,” said Blacklock candidly, “I couldn’t, or I should not have sent for Miss Holland. It was an extremely difficult problem to get you to risk your life, and stand out against your commanding officer’s wishes and your own inclinations and your apparent duty, and come back to this house after the whole plan was arranged and every argument seemed to be in favour of your going aboard your boat again. Nobody but a man under the influence of a woman would have taken such a course. Those were the facts I had to face, and﻿—well, the thing came off, thanks entirely to Miss Holland. I have apologised to her twenty times already for making such a use of her, and I apologise again.”

===Chinese===
「是我出的主意，让她显得对我不满，并且设法——不妨这么说——把我们那一户人家分作两个营垒，好让你自觉是在同我作对，实则做的正合我的心意。我把这一点向你坦白说了，好让你看明白：那套出于不得已而施行的骗术，责任在谁。」

「不得已！」年轻的中尉苦涩地喊道，「谁逼你们动用一个女人的？只骗我一个人，就办不到么？」

「办不到。」布莱克洛克坦白地说，「否则我也不会把霍兰德小姐请来。要让你甘冒性命之险，违拗你上司的意思、你自己的心愿和你表面的职责，在全盘计划已然定妥、条条道理都劝你回艇之后再回到这所宅子里——这是个难得不能再难的题目。除了被一个女人迷住的男人，谁也不会走这条路。这便是我必须面对的事实，而——喏，事情成了，全亏霍兰德小姐。为这样用她，我已向她道过二十回歉，现在再道歉一回。」

===Original===
Suddenly the young German broke out.

“Ah! But were there not consolations?”

“What do you mean?”

===Chinese===
那年轻的德国人突然发作了。

「啊！可这里面不也有慰藉么？」

「你什么意思？」

===Original===
“You and Miss Holland living by yourselves in this house﻿—is it that you need apologise for?”

“Miss Holland never spent a single night under this roof,” said Blacklock quietly.

“Not﻿—not a night,” stammered von Belke. “Then where﻿—?”

“She stayed at a house in the neighbourhood.”

===Chinese===
「你同霍兰德小姐两个，单独住在这所宅子里——你要道歉的是这个么？」

「霍兰德小姐在这片屋顶底下过夜，一夜也不曾有过。」布莱克洛克平静地说。

「不曾——不曾过一夜，」冯·贝尔克结结巴巴地说，「那她住——？」

「她住在近处一户人家里。」

===Original===
The lieutenant seemed incapable of comment, and Captain Phipps observed genially,

“There seem to have been some rum goings-on behind your back, Mr. Belke!”

Von Belke seemed to be realising this fact himself, and resenting it.

“You seem to have amused yourself very much by deceiving me,” he remarked.

===Chinese===
中尉似乎连评论都做不出了，皮普斯上校和气地说，

「看来你背地里出了不少古怪事呢，贝尔克先生！」

冯·贝尔克自己似乎也正认清这个事实，并且为之愤懑。

「你拿骗我取乐，想必取乐得不轻。」他说。

===Original===
“I assure you I did nothing for fun,” said Blacklock gravely, yet with a twinkle in his eye. “It was all in the way of business.”

“The story that you preached, for instance!”

“Would you have felt quite happy if I had told you I had omitted to do the one thing I had professed to come here for?”

Von Belke gave a little sound that might have meant anything. Then he exclaimed﻿—

===Chinese===
「我向你担保，我没有一件事是为了取乐。」布莱克洛克正色道，眼里却闪着一点狡黠，「那全是正经公事。」

「就说你讲道那一出吧！」

「倘若我告诉你，我来此地自称要办的那一件事偏偏没办，你心里会痛快么？」

冯·贝尔克出了个小声，那声音什么意思都可能。随即他嚷道——

===Original===
“But your servant who was not supposed to know anything﻿—that was to annoy me, I suppose!”

“To isolate you. I didn’t want you to speak to a soul but me.”

The captive sat silent for a moment, and then said﻿—

“You had the house watched by the police﻿—I see that now.”

===Chinese===
「还有你那位什么都不该知道的仆人——那总算是存心气我的吧！」

「为的是孤立你。除了我，我不愿你和任何人交谈。」

俘虏默坐了片刻，然后说——

「这宅子是你叫警察盯的——我如今明白了。」

===Original===
“A compliment to you, Mr. Belke,” smiled the Commander; and then he added, “You gave me one or two anxious moments, I may tell you. Your demand for mufti necessitated a very hurried interview with the commander of a destroyer, and old Craigie’s visit very nearly upset the applecart. I had to tell him pretty nearly the whole truth when I got him outside. But those incidents came after the chief crisis was over. The nearest squeak was when I thought you were safely engaged with Miss Holland, and a certain officer was calling on me, who was *not* Captain Phipps. In fact, he was an even more exalted person. Miss Holland saved the situation by crying out that you were coming, or I’m afraid that would have been the end of the submarine attack.”

“So?” said the young German slowly and with a very wry face, and then he turned to Eileen. “Then, Miss Holland, every time you did me the honour to appear kind and visit me you were carrying out one of this gentleman’s plans? And every word you spoke was said to entangle me in your net, or to keep me quiet while something was being done behind my back? I hope that some day you may enjoy the recollection as much as I am enjoying it now!”

“Mr. Belke,” she cried, “I am very deeply sorry for treating even an enemy as I treated you!”

She spoke so sincerely and with so much emotion that even Captain Phipps assumed a certain solemn expression, which was traditionally never seen on his face except when the Chaplain was actually officiating, and jumping up she came a step towards the prisoner. There she stood, a graceful and beautiful figure, her eyes glowing with fervour.

===Chinese===
「那是给您的一份恭维，贝尔克先生。」中校微笑道；随即又补充说，「我可以告诉您，您让我悬过一两回心。您要便服，害得我同一位驱逐舰舰长来了一场十万火急的会面；老克雷格的来访，又差点把整车苹果掀翻在地。把他弄出门外之后，我几乎把全部真相都讲给了他听。不过这些都是头号危机过去之后的事。最险的一回，是我以为您正同霍兰德小姐安稳周旋，偏偏有位军官来拜访我——而那位*不是*皮普斯上校。说实在的，他是个身份更尊贵的人物。亏得霍兰德小姐喊了一声您来了，救了全场，否则我怕那便是潜艇袭击的终局了。」

「原来如此？」年轻的德国人说得很慢，一脸苦相，随即转向艾琳。「那么，霍兰德小姐，你每一次屈尊对我示好、来看我，都是在执行这位先生的某一项计划？你说的每一句话，都是为了把我缠进你们的网里，或是稳住我，好听你们在背后行事？但愿有朝一日，你回想起这段往事时，能像我现在回味它这样愉快！」

「贝尔克先生，」她喊道，「我那样待你——哪怕对一个敌人——我也深感歉疚！」

她说得那样真诚、那样动情，连皮普斯上校都换上了一副肃穆的神情——照惯例，那张脸上除非牧师正在主持礼拜，是从不见这种神情的。她一跃而起，朝俘虏迈近一步，就站在那里，一个优雅而美丽的身影，双眼灼灼发光。

===Original===
“All I can say for myself, and all I can ask you to think of when your recollections of me pain you, is only this﻿—if you had a sister, would you have had her hesitate to do one single thing I did in order to defeat her country’s enemies?”

Von Belke looked at her for a moment with frowning brow and folded arms. Then all he said was﻿—

“Germany’s cause is sacred!”

Her eyes opened very wide.

===Chinese===
「我为自己能说的，以及日后回忆起我而感到刺痛时，我能求你想到的，只有这一句——倘若你有一个妹妹，为了挫败她祖国的敌人，我做过的事里哪一件，你会容许她犹豫不做？」

冯·贝尔克皱着眉、抱着臂，看了她片刻。然后他只说了一句——

「德国的事业是神圣的！」

她的眼睛睁得极大。

===Original===
“Then what is right for Germany is wrong for her enemies?”

“Naturally. How can Germany both be right﻿—as she is, and yet be wrong?”

“I﻿—I don’t think you quite understand what I mean,” she said with a puzzled look.

===Chinese===
「那么对德国是正义的，对她的敌人便是非正义的？」

「自然。德国怎么可能既是对的——她本来就是对——又是错的？」

「我——我想你不太明白我的意思。」她说，一脸困惑。

===Original===
“Germany never will,” said Blacklock quietly. “That is why we are at war.”

A tramp of footsteps sounded on the gravel outside, and Captain Phipps sprang up.

“Your guard has come for you, Mr. Belke,” he said. “I’m sorry to interrupt this conversation, but I’m afraid you must be moving.”

===Chinese===
「德国永远也不会明白。」布莱克洛克平静地说，「所以我们在打仗。」

外头砾石路上一阵脚步声踏响，皮普斯上校跳了起来。

「您的卫兵来接您了，贝尔克先生。」他说，「很抱歉打断这场谈话，但恐怕您得动身了。」

## III The Empty Envelope / 三 空信封

===Original===
Commander Blacklock closed the front door.

“Chilly night,” he observed.

“It is rather,” said Eileen.

The wind droned through a distant keyhole mournfully and continuously. That melancholy piping sound never rose and never fell; monotonous and unvarying it piped on and on. Otherwise the house had that peculiar feeling of quiet which houses have when stirring events are over and people have departed.

===Chinese===
布莱克洛克中校关上前门。

「夜里挺冷。」他说。

「是挺冷。」艾琳说。

风从一个远处的锁眼里哀哀地、不绝地呜咽。那凄凉的笛音不扬也不抑，单调划一，一径呜呜地吹。除此之外，宅子里便只剩下一种特别的静谧——大事已了、人已散去时，宅子所特有的那种静谧。

===Original===
The two remaining inhabitants reentered the parlour, glanced at one another with a half smile, and then seemed simultaneously to find a little difficulty in knowing what to do next.

“Well,” said Blacklock, “our business seems over.”

He felt he had spoken a little more abruptly than he intended, and would have liked to repeat his observations in a more genial tone.

“Yes,” said she almost as casually, “there is nothing more to be done tonight, I suppose.”

===Chinese===
剩下的两位重新进了客厅，彼此半含笑意地交换了一瞥，随即又像是同时觉出了一点为难：接下来该做什么呢？

「喏，」布莱克洛克说，「我们的事算是办完了。」

他自觉这话说得比本意生硬了些，很想换一副和悦些的口气再说一遍。

「是啊。」她说得也差不多同样随口，「今晚想必没什么要办的了。」

===Original===
“I shall have to write up my report of our friend Mr. Belke’s life and last words,” said he with a half laugh.

“And I have got to get over to Mrs. Brown’s,” she replied, “and so I had better go at once.”

“Oh, there’s no such desperate hurry,” he said hastily; “I haven’t much to write up tonight. We must have some supper first.”

“Yes,” she agreed, “I suppose we shall begin to feel hungry soon if we don’t. I’ll see about it. What would you like?”

===Chinese===
「我得把我们的朋友贝尔克先生的生平和遗言写成报告。」他半笑着说。

「我还得回布朗太太那边去呢。」她答道，「所以最好这就动身。」

「哦，也没急到那个份上。」他赶紧说，「今晚我没什么可写的。咱们总得先吃点宵夜。」

「也好。」她应道，「不吃的话，一会儿就该饿了。我去张罗。你想吃点什么？」

===Original===
“The cold ham and a couple of boiled eggs will suit me.”

She agreed again.

“That won’t take long, and then you can begin your report.”

Again he protested hastily.

===Chinese===
「冷火腿，再加两个煮鸡蛋，就很合适。」

她又点了头。

「这用不了多久，然后你就可以动手写报告了。」

他又急忙推辞。

===Original===
“Oh, but there’s no hurry about that, I assure you. I only wanted to save trouble.”

While she was away he stood before the fire, gazing absently into space and scarcely moving a muscle. The ham and boiled eggs appeared, and a little more animation became apparent, but it was not a lively feast. She talked for a little in an ordinary, cheerful way, just as though there was no very special subject for conversation; but he seemed too absentminded and silent to respond even to these overtures, except with a brief smile and a briefer word. They had both been quite silent for about five minutes, when he suddenly said in a constrained manner, but with quite a different intonation﻿—

“Well, I am afraid our ways part now. What are you going to do next?”

“I’ve been wondering,” she said; “and I think if Mrs. Craigie still wants me I ought to go back to her.”

“Back to the Craigies!” he exclaimed. “And become﻿—er﻿—a governess again?”

===Chinese===
「哦，那可当真不急，我向你保证。我不过是想省点事。」

她走开后，他站在炉火前，目光茫然望着半空，几乎一动不动。火腿和煮鸡蛋端了上来，他身上多少活了些气色，可这仍算不上一顿热闹的晚饭。她寻常而愉快地谈了一小会儿，仿佛并没有什么特别的话题；他却心不在焉、沉默寡言，连这些攀谈也接不住，只以一个短促的微笑和更短的一个字应对。两个人相对无言了约摸五分钟，他忽然开口，神态拘束，腔调却全然不同了——

「喏，只怕咱们这就分道了。接下来你打算做什么？」

「我也正琢磨呢。」她说，「我想，克雷格太太若还用得着我，我该回到她那儿去。」

「回克雷格家去！」他喊道，「再当——呃——再当家庭教师？」

===Original===
“It will be rather dull at first,” she laughed; “but one can’t have such adventures as this every day, and I really have treated the Craigies rather badly. You see you told Mr. Craigie the truth about my desertion of them, and they may forgive me. If they do, and if they still need me, I feel I simply must offer my services.”

“It’s very good of you.”

She laughed again.

“It is at least as much for my own interest as Mrs. Craigie’s. I have nowhere else to go to and nothing else to do.”

===Chinese===
「起初未免闷些。」她笑道，「可这样的奇遇也不是天天有的，何况我对克雷格一家实在亏欠。你看，你把我不辞而别的实情告诉了克雷格先生，他们兴许肯饶我这一回。倘若他们肯，倘若还用得着我，我觉得自己实在应当毛遂自荐。」

「你真是太好心了。」

她又笑了。

「这至少一半是为我自己打算，同克雷格太太各占一半。旁的我无处可去，也无事可做。」

===Original===
“I wish I could offer you another job like this,” said he.

A sparkle leapt into her eyes.

“If you ever do see any chance of making any sort of use of me﻿—I mean of letting me be useful﻿—you will be sure to let me know, won’t you?”

“Rather! But honestly, I’m not likely to have such a bit of luck as this again.”

===Chinese===
「但愿我还能给你这样一件差事。」他说。

她的眼睛里跳进一点光。

「往后你若当真看出有什么地方用得上我——我是说，容我派上用场——你一定告诉我，好不好？」

「那是自然！不过说老实话，这样的好运，我怕是再难碰上一回了。」

===Original===
“What will you be doing?”

“Whatever I’m told to do; the sort of thing I was on before﻿—odd jobs of the ‘hush’ type. But I wish I could think of you doing something more﻿—well, more worthy of your gifts.”

“One must take one’s luck as it comes,” she said with an outward air of philosophy, whatever her heart whispered.

“Exactly,” he agreed with emphasis. “Still﻿—”

===Chinese===
「那你打算做什么？」

「上面派什么做什么；还是先前那一类——『保密』性质的零碎差事。只是我总盼着，能想到你去做点更——嗯，更配得上你才具的事。」

「人的运气来了，就得接着。」她说，外表一派达观，心里嘀咕的什么，就不得而知了。

「正是。」他重重点头，「只是——」

===Original===
He broke off, and pulled a pipe out of his pocket.

“I’ll leave you to smoke,” she said, “and say good night now.”

“One moment!” said he, jumping up; “there’s something I feel I must say. I’ve been rather contrite about it. I’m afraid I haven’t quite played cricket so far as you are concerned.”

She looked at him quickly.

===Chinese===
他收住话头，从口袋里掏出一只烟斗。

「我让你抽烟吧，」她说，「这就道晚安了。」

「且慢！」他说着跳起来，「有句话我觉得非说不可。为这个我一直挺过意不去。只怕在你这件事上，我行事不够光明磊落。」

她飞快看了他一眼。

===Original===
“What do you mean?” she asked.

“It’s about Belke. I’m afraid Phipps was quite right in saying I’m rather cold-blooded when I am keen over a job. Perhaps it becomes a little too much of a mere problem. Getting you to treat Belke as you did, for instance. You were very nice to him tonight﻿—though he was too German to understand how you felt﻿—and it struck me that very possibly you had been seeing a great deal of him, and he’s a nice-looking fellow, with a lot of good stuff in him, a brave man, no doubt about it, and﻿—well, perhaps you liked him enough to make you wish I hadn’t let you in for such a job. I just wondered.”

She looked at him for an instant with an expression he did not quite understand; then she looked away and seemed for a moment a little embarrassed, and then she looked at him again, and he thought he had never seen franker eyes.

“You’re as kind and considerate as﻿—as, well, as you’re clever!” she said with a half laugh. “But, if you only knew, if you only even had the least guess how I’ve longed to do something for my country﻿—something really useful, I mean; how unutterably wretched I felt when the trifling work I was doing was stopped by a miserable neglected cold and I had to have a change, and as I’d no money I had to take this stupid job of teaching; and how I envied the women who were more fortunate and really *were* doing useful things; oh, then you’d know how grateful I feel to you! If I could make every officer in the German navy﻿—and the army too﻿—fall in love with me, and then hand them over to you, I’d do it fifty times over! Don’t, please, talk nonsense, or think nonsense! Good night, Mr. Tiel, and perhaps it’s goodbye.”

===Chinese===
「你什么意思？」她问。

「是关于贝尔克的事。皮普斯说我办起案子来相当冷酷，恐怕说得一点不错。也许在我这里，事情有点太变成一道纯题目了。比如，让你那样去待贝尔克。今晚你待他很好——虽说他那德国脾气，领略不了你的心意——而我忽然想到，你先前同他相处的时日恐怕很不少，他又是个体面的男人，骨子里有许多好材料，无疑是个勇敢的人，于是——嗯，说不定你对他已有几分喜欢，宁愿我没有把你卷进这样一桩差事。我不过是揣想。」

她带着一种他不大读得懂的神情望了他一瞬；随即把目光移开，一时似乎有点局促；然后又回望他，他觉得从没见过比这更坦白的眼睛。

「你又仁厚又体贴，就像——就像你聪明一样！」她半笑着说，「可是，你若知道——哪怕只稍稍猜到——我是怎样盼望为我的国家做点事——我是说，真正有用的事；当那点微不足道的工作被一场拖坏了的伤风断送、我不得不换换环境，又因为没钱、只好揽下教书这件蠢差事时，我心里是何等地道的凄惶；还有，我是怎样羡慕那些运气更好、当真*做着*有用事情的女人们——啊，那你就知道我对你怀着怎样的感激了！倘若我能叫德国海军的军官——陆军也算上——个个爱上我，再把他们一个个交到你手里，这样的事我情愿做上五十遍！请别再说傻话，也别再想傻话了！晚安，蒂尔先生，也许，是永别。」

===Original===
She laughed as she gave him his nom-de-guerre, and held out her hand as frankly as she had spoken. He did not take it, however.

“I’m going to escort you over to Mrs. Brown’s,” he said with a very different expression now in his eyes.

“It’s very good of you,” she said; “you are sure you have time?”

“Loads!” he assured her.

===Chinese===
她叫他这个化名时笑了，又像方才说话那样坦率地伸出手来。他却没有握。

「我送你回布朗太太家去。」他说，眼里此刻是一种全然不同的神情。

「你太客气了。」她说，「你确定抽得出工夫？」

「有的是！」他向她保证。

===Original===
He opened the door for her, but she stopped on the threshold. A young woman was waiting in the hall.

“Mrs. Brown has sent her girl to escort me,” she said, “so we’ll have to”﻿—she corrected herself﻿—“we must say good night now. Is it goodbye, or shall I see you in the morning?”

His face had become very long again.

“I’m very much afraid not. I’ve got to report myself with the lark. Goodbye.”

===Chinese===
他为她开了门，她却在门槛上停住了。门厅里候着一个年轻女子。

「布朗太太派了她家的女仆来接我了。」她说，「那么我们就得——」她改口道——「这就道晚安了。是永别呢，还是明早还能见到你？」

他的脸又拉得老长。

「只怕见不着了。天一亮我就得去销差。再见。」

===Original===
The front door closed behind her, and Commander Blacklock strode back to the fire and gazed at it for some moments.

“Well,” he said to himself, “I suppose, looking at things as they ought to be looked at, Mrs. Brown’s girl has saved me from making a damned fool of myself! Now to work: that’s my proper stunt.”

He threw some sheets of foolscap on the table, took out his pen, and sat down to his work. For about five minutes he stared at the foolscap, but the pen never made a movement. Then abruptly he jumped up and exclaimed﻿—

“Dash it, I must!”

===Chinese===
前门在她身后合上，布莱克洛克中校大步回到炉火边，凝望了片刻。

「喏，」他自言自语道，「照本分想来，布朗太太家的女仆倒救了我，没叫我把自己弄成个天大的傻瓜！干活去吧：那才是我的本分。」

他往桌上撂了几张大页书写纸，取出钢笔，坐下干活。约摸五分钟，他直勾勾盯着那纸，笔却纹丝未动。忽然他一跃而起，喊道——

「见鬼，我非去不可！」

===Original===
Snatching up an envelope, he thrust it in his pocket, and a moment later was out of the house.

---

Miss Holland and her escort were about fifty yards from Mrs. Brown’s house when the girl started and looked back.

“There’s someone crying on you!” she exclaimed.

===Chinese===
他抓起一只信封，塞进口袋，转眼间已出了宅子。

---

霍兰德小姐和护送她的人才走到离布朗太太家约五十码的地方，那女仆忽然一惊，回头望去。

「有人在后头喊您呢！」她喊道。

===Original===
Eileen stopped and peered back into the night. It had clouded over and was very dark. Very vaguely something seemed to loom up in the path behind them.

“Miss Holland!” cried a voice.

“It’s the minister!” said the girl.

“The﻿—who?” exclaimed Eileen; and added hastily, “Oh yes, I know who you mean.”

===Chinese===
艾琳停下脚，朝身后的夜色里张望。天已转阴，黑得很。她们身后的小路上，隐隐约约仿佛浮起一个影子。

「霍兰德小姐！」一个声音喊道。

「是牧师！」女仆说。

「那位——谁？」艾琳喊道，又赶忙补上一句，「哦对了，我知道你说的是谁。」

===Original===
A tall figure disengaged itself from the surrounding night.

“Sorry to trouble you,” said the voice in curiously quick and jerky accents, “but I’ve got a note I want this girl to deliver immediately.”

He handed her an envelope.

“Hand that in at the first farm on the other side of the Manse,” he commanded, pointing backwards into the darkness. “I’ll escort Miss Holland.”

===Chinese===
一个高大的身影从四围的夜色里脱了出来。

「抱歉打扰。」那声音说道，语调出奇地急促而断续，「我这里有张字条，要这姑娘立刻送去。」

他递给她一只信封。

「送到牧师寓所那边过去的第一家农庄，当面交割。」他指着身后的黑暗命令道，「霍兰德小姐由我护送。」

===Original===
“Which hoose﻿—” began the girl.

“The first you come to!” said the Commander peremptorily. “Quick as you can!”

Then he looked at Eileen, and for a moment said nothing.

“What’s the matter?” she asked anxiously. “Has anything gone wrong?”

===Chinese===
「哪一户——」女仆开口道。

「走到头一家就是！」中校不容分说道，「越快越好！」

然后他望着艾琳，一时没有言语。

「怎么了？」她不安地问，「出了什么岔子？」

===Original===
“Yes,” he said with a half laugh, “I have. I even forgot to lick down that envelope. How the deuce I’m to explain an empty, unaddressed, unfastened envelope the Lord only knows!” His manner suddenly changed and he asked abruptly, “Are you in a desperate hurry to get in? I’ve something to say to you.”

He paused and looked at her, but she said not a word in reply, not even to inquire what it was. A little jerkily he proceeded﻿—

“I’m probably making just as great a fool of myself as Belke. But I couldn’t let you go without asking﻿—well, whether I am merely making a fool of myself. If you know what I mean and think I am, well, please just tell me you can manage to see yourself safely home﻿—I know it’s only about fifty yards﻿—and I’ll go and get that wretched envelope back from the girl and tell her another lie.”

===Chinese===
「是啊，」他半笑着说，「我出了岔子。我竟忘了把那信封舔上。一只空空的、没写地址、没封口的信封，我该怎么圆过去，只有老天爷知道！」他的态度陡然一变，唐突地问道：「你急着进门么？我有话要对你说。」

他顿住，望着她，她却一言不答，连问都不问是什么事。他略略发涩地续道——

「我这会儿的模样，多半同贝尔克一样是个大傻瓜。可我不能让你就这么走，而不问一问——嗯，问一问我究竟是不是在出丑。倘若你懂我的意思，也认为我是，那么，就请你只说一句：你自己能平平安安到家——我知道这不过五十码光景——我便去找那姑娘讨回那只倒霉信封，再对她撒一个谎。」

===Original===
“Why should I think you are making a fool of yourself?” she asked in a voice that was very quiet, but not quite as even as she meant.

“Let’s turn back a little way,” he suggested quickly.

She said nothing, but she turned.

===Chinese===
「我为什么要想你是在出丑呢？」她问，声音很轻，却不如她想装的那样平稳。

「咱们往回走一小段吧。」他赶紧提议。

她什么也没说，可她转身了。

===Original===
“Take my arm, won’t you,” he suggested.

In the bitterness of his heart he was conscious that he had rapped out this proposal in his sharpest quarterdeck manner. And he had meant to speak so gently! Yet she took his arm, a little timidly it is true, but no wonder, thought he. For a few moments they walked in silence, falling slower and slower with each step; and then they stopped. At that, speech seemed to be jerked out of him at last.

“I wonder if it’s conceivable that you’d ever look upon me as anything but a calculating machine?” he inquired.

“I never thought of you in the least as that!” she exclaimed.

===Chinese===
「挽着我的胳膊，好么。」他又提议。

心底苦涩之中，他分明觉察到，这句请求是用他那最凌厉的后甲板腔调蹦出来的。他本来打算说得何等温柔！然而她还是挽住了他的手臂，确实略带羞怯，不过也难怪，他想。有片刻工夫，他们默默走着，脚步一步一步慢下来；随后他们停住了。这一停，话似乎终于从他嘴里给逼了出来。

「不知可有可能——你看待我，永远只能是一台算计的机器？」他问道。

「我从来没有半点那样看过你！」她喊道。

===Original===
The gallant Commander evidently regarded this as a charitable exaggeration. He shook his head.

“You must sometimes. I know I must have seemed that sort of person.”

“Not to me,” she said.

He seemed encouraged, but still a little incredulous.

===Chinese===
这位英武的中校显然把这话当作了善意的夸张。他摇了摇头。

「你一定有时这样想过。我知道，我必定显得就是那样一种人。」

「在我眼里不是。」她说。

他像是受了鼓舞，却仍有些不敢置信。

===Original===
“Then did you ever really think of me as a human being﻿—as a﻿—as a﻿—” he hesitated painfully﻿—“as a friend?”

“Yes,” she said, “of course I did﻿—always as a friend.”

“Could you possibly﻿—conceivably﻿—think of me as”﻿—he hesitated, and then blurted out﻿—“as, dash it all, head over ears in love with you?”

===Chinese===
「那么你可曾当真把我当作一个人——当作一个——一个——」他艰难地踌躇着——「当作一个朋友？」

「是的。」她说，「我当然当过——一直当作一位朋友。」

「你可能够——哪怕有一丝可能——把我当作——」他踌躇着，随即冲口而出——「当作一个——唉，见鬼——一个为你神魂颠倒、不可救药爱上你的人？」

===Original===
And then suddenly the Commander realised that he had not made a fool of himself after all.

The empty envelope was duly delivered, but no explanation was required. Mrs. Brown’s girl supplied all the information necessary.

“Of course I knew fine what he was after,” said she.

===Chinese===
就在那一刻，中校恍然明白：他终究没有出丑。

那只空信封如期送到了，也无人要求什么解释。布朗太太家的女仆把必要的情况一五一十全讲了。

「俺当然心里明镜似的，他打的是什么主意。」她说。
